# Evidence pack: Dust SED fitting (modified blackbody, panchromatic codes) (`sed-fitting`)

Scope: fitting (sub)mm/FIR photometry to get dust temperature, beta, L_IR, SFR

Built from 1001 non-held-out ALMA papers whose methods cards passed mechanical evidence verification (every quote below is verbatim from the paper). Years 2012-2026.
Matcher (regex over analysis steps): `\bsed\b|seds\b|modified black[- ]?body|grey[- ]?body|graybody|magphys|cigale|dust temperature fit|far[- ]infrared luminosity|\blir\b|l_ir|ir luminosit`
Tagging is by regex, so a few papers may be off-topic: ignore those.

- Conclusions unchanged if upper limits are used in the fits; median L_IR and M_dust agree with literature MS values: 1
- Dust-based and Schmidt-Kennicutt gas masses consistent for galaxies with 850 um detections: 1

- Rescaled [CII]/CO ratio compared with the 2000-10000 range in the literature: 1
- Reasonable changes in α and βd do not change the optical-depth conclusion.: 1
- Peak temperature and total infrared luminosity insensitive to assumed λ0.: 1
- Compare with dynamical-minus-stellar masses and CO estimates; test 35 K mass-weighted temperature.: 1
- Compare depletion times with the decreasing-redshift trend from SCOVILLE16.: 1
- ALPINE stacked-SED luminosities agree within <0.2 dex.: 1

- Integrated emission fits; radial continuum and C18O distributions do not reproduce observations well.: 1
- Settling models improve the ~100 μm SED; mass estimates are insensitive to tested alpha_v choices.: 1

- SFR–LIR relation approximately 0.1 dex below the Chabrier-calibrated Kennicutt relation.: 1
- Propagate molecular-mass and dust-mass uncertainties; fgas median uncertainty approximately 60%.: 1

### Key parameters and settings (bibcode: value)
- 2018MNRAS.481.1976Z: Stellar population age 100 Myr - 2 Gyr, fixed solar metallicity, constant-SFR templates
- 2018MNRAS.481.1976Z: Coordinate shifts Delta RA = 0 arcsec, Delta DEC = -0.2 arcsec applied to HST
- 2018MNRAS.481.1976Z: L_IR integrated over 8-1000 um from SED fitting
- 2018MNRAS.481.1976Z: <U> = L_IR/(125 M_dust)
- 2018MNRAS.481.1976Z: SFR = 1e-10 L_IR (Kennicutt 1998); SK relation dispersion 0.2 dex
- 2018MNRAS.481.1976Z: alpha_CO metallicity dependence: log alpha_CO = 0.64 - 1.5 (12 + log(O/H) - 8.7)
- 2018MNRAS.481.1976Z: Rescaling implies L_[CII]/L_CO ~ 6000
- 2020MNRAS.498.4192F: Comparison grid: α=2, βd=2, λ0=100 or 200 μm.
- 2020MNRAS.498.4192F: 18 000 samples; target acceptance 0.99.
- 2020MNRAS.498.4192F: Fit in linear space.
- 2020MNRAS.498.4192F: Tpeak [K] = 2.898×10^3 [μm K] / λpeak [μm].
- 2020MNRAS.498.4192F: Band 6 input flux in mJy; luminosity distance DL(z) in Gpc.
- 2020MNRAS.498.4192F: MAGNELLI14 comparison selected at log(LIR/L⊙)~10.5–12.5.
- 2024ApJ...965..108U: AGN required for ΔBIC>10; AGN always excluded for tier-2/3/4/5/6
- 2024ApJ...965..108U: Basic grid for tier-1/2/5/6; limited dust-emission grid for tier-3/4; extended AGN grid for A2744-ID227
- 2024ApJ...965..108U: Polar dust: emissivity index 1.6; temperature 150 K; opacity unity at 200 μm
- 2024ApJ...965..108U: Logarithmic: SFR, stellar mass, dust luminosity, dust mass
- 2024ApJ...965..108U: Linear: dust temperature, E(B−V), IRX, β, δ, bolometric AGN luminosity
- 2024ApJ...965..108U: Exclude ALCS-XAGNs and ALCS-nonXAGNs from MAGPHYS tests; code comparison uses tier-1

- 2020MNRAS.498.4192F: TSED and Tpeak measure light-weighted dust temperatures.

### Limitations the authors admit
- 2018MNRAS.481.1976Z: Fluxes depend critically on the assumed model size taken from optical images
- 2018MNRAS.481.1976Z: Source size cannot be constrained from individual sources because of low S/N
- 2018MNRAS.481.1976Z: Observed in a more extended configuration than requested, giving 5-10 times higher flux errors
- 2020MNRAS.498.4192F: Only four galaxies, selected among the infrared brightest at z~5.5 because of observing-time constraints.
- 2020MNRAS.498.4192F: The sample likely misses more metal-poor systems.
- 2020MNRAS.498.4192F: HZ6 dynamical mass is uncertain and covers less of the system than the continuum gas mass.
- 2020MNRAS.498.4192F: CO comparisons have large errors and uncertain conversion factors and brightness-temperature ratios.
- 2020MNRAS.498.4192F: The analytical model motivates trends; larger samples are needed to establish the physics.
- 2024ApJ...965..108U: Lens-model and magnification uncertainties are ignored
- 2024ApJ...965..108U: Photometric-redshift uncertainties are ignored in the main analysis
- 2024ApJ...965..108U: Model uncertainties make reported physical-property errors underestimated
- 2024ApJ...965..108U: Herschel-selected comparison subsamples may be biased
- 2024ApJ...965..108U: M0416-ID156 high temperature is uncertain because its far-infrared SED is poorly fitted
- 2024ApJ...965..108U: A2537-ID24/66 high temperatures may reflect residual blending
- 2024ApJ...965..108U: Best-fit SEDs show substantial ALMA or Herschel discrepancies for some sources
- 2024ApJ...965..108U: AGN identifications and luminosity-density conclusions depend strongly on SED-model assumptions
- 2024ApJ...965..108U: Bolometric-to-X-ray conversion has large scatter and possible Eddington-ratio dependence
- 2018ApJ...869...27V: Abundance differences driven by assumed alpha_CO and delta_GDR; degeneracies cannot be broken with current data
- 2018ApJ...869...27V: Systematic uncertainties on gas masses not included
- 2014ApJ...796...84R: Spatial resolution insufficient to resolve structure; higher resolution needed
- 2014ApJ...796...84R: Photometric uncertainties near the SED peak are large
- 2024A&A...687A.217D: 2.5'' beam dilutes core emission, so core temperatures can be under- or over-estimated
- 2024A&A...687A.217D: Optically thin assumption may bias temperature and mass at high column density
- 2024A&A...687A.217D: Reference opacity is poorly known and may vary across the protoclusters
- 2024A&A...687A.217D: G333.60 is the least constrained region because of saturation and missing SOFIA data
- 2024A&A...687A.217D: Ring-like artifacts may bias core temperatures
- 2019ApJ...878..103R: Uncertainties are qualitative; selection effects make conclusions difficult; brightest disks may be atypical
- 2025ApJ...995...98D: Gas-phase heating and cooling omitted; gas masses may be underestimated by up to ~2.
- 2025ApJ...995...98D: Inside ≲40 AU, optical thickness makes surface densities lower limits.
- 2025ApJ...995...98D: Dust composition can shift mass by ~5; omitted ice-mantle opacity effects could increase dust mass by ~20%.
- 2025ApJ...995...98D: Axisymmetry neglects observed spiral arms; simple drift calculation is only an order-of-magnitude consistency estimate.
- 2025ApJ...995...98D: The dust-dynamics test smooths away low-level structures and is not an independent surface-density inference.
- 2024ApJ...964...95S: Sample selection may favor intense starbursts.
- 2024ApJ...964...95S: Warm graybody dust may miss the diffuse cold ISM and underestimate total dust mass.
- 2024ApJ...964...95S: Reported dust-mass uncertainties omit the full uncertainty in adopted κ850μm.
- 2024ApJ...964...95S: Stellar masses are more uncertain for the four objects with substantial optical-NIR AGN emission.
- 2024ApJ...964...95S: Comparison of stellar and dynamical masses deferred to future work.
- 2021A&A...645A..49G: Some extra-nuclear contribution to the lowest-lying H2O lines is not excluded.
- 2021A&A...645A..49G: Core lines are saturated; core optical depth, abundance, mass, and size are uncertain.
- 2021A&A...645A..49G: The three-component geometry is schematic with sharp transitions.
- 2021A&A...645A..49G: Other molecular species favor disk tau_100 approximately 1.5–3, potentially higher than the fiducial H2O value.
- 2021A&A...645A..49G: Limited spatial resolution and beam smearing prevent more detailed nuclear velocity modeling.
- 2021A&A...645A..49G: High turbulence masks rotation and possible CO inflow at radii <= 1 arcsec.
- 2021A&A...645A..49G: Flow lines rely on four radial samples and interpolation and are not realistic gas orbits.
- 2021A&A...645A..49G: CO inflow rates are not corrected for feedback from the nuclear region.
- 2021A&A...645A..49G: OH119 profiles have significant modeling discrepancies; OH inflow rate is uncertain.
- 2018Sci...362.1034D: U1 may be at a different redshift; stellar mass and SFR uncertain by factor of 2
- 2021ApJ...918...76C: The new 33 GHz VLA observations suffer from significant decorrelation due to weather and low declination (-26 deg), so the 33 GHz flux densities must be treated as lower limits and excluded from SED …
- 2021ApJ...918...76C: Significant flux calibration discrepancies exist between the 2004 AJ314 / 2016 16B-067 VLA maps and the 1995 AK0400 maps (~2x higher at 5 GHz and ~1.4x lower at 15 GHz), requiring ad hoc adjustments …
- 2021ApJ...918...76C: Forcing a 0.3'' circular restoring beam results in significant super-resolution in the declination direction for the 5 GHz map (native 0.620'' x 0.248'') and modest/moderate super-resolution for the …
- 2021ApJ...918...76C: Q_Lyc, stellar mass, and A_V estimates are lower limits because ionizing photons can be absorbed by dust and both radio and Pa-alpha fluxes are lower limits; dust temperatures are unmeasured and dust…
- 2016ApJ...832..187B: SEDs lack points near peak so Tdust, tau, beta cannot be constrained simultaneously
- 2023PASJ...75..874M: Limited angular resolution prevents distinguishing competing emission scenarios
- 2023PASJ...75..874M: Coronal-parameter assumption systematics are excluded from quoted errors
- 2023PASJ...75..874M: The SED fit misses the inverse spectrum between Bands 6 and 7
- 2023PASJ...75..874M: Absorber parameters cannot be uniquely determined
- 2023PASJ...75..874M: Cold dust properties remain uncertain
- 2023PASJ...75..874M: The spectral index below 10 GHz is difficult to determine
- 2025A&A...699A.183H: Large uncertainties on the observed Halpha/Hbeta ratio (10 to nearly 90%)
- 2020ApJ...905...89Y: Absolute gas-to-dust ratios are not interpreted because of uncertain C18O abundance and dust absorption coefficient
- 2020ApJ...905...89Y: Dust scattering not modelled in the SED fit
- 2020ApJ...905...89Y: Velocity features within the 0.45 arcsec beam are uncertain
- 2020ApJ...905...89Y: Axisymmetric Keplerian models leave significant residuals in the asymmetric disk
- 2020ApJ...905...89Y: Resolution too limited to separate vortex from spiral origin or to resolve the bump profile
- 2014ApJ...791...42Z: Fit uncertainties underestimate true uncertainty due to gamma degeneracy
- 2016ApJ...818...45S: Putative 13-AU belt needs deeper data; SED models degenerate due to lack of Rayleigh-Jeans constraints
- 2026A&A...705A.196H: frank does not account for the primary beam, which could bias profiles of large discs
- 2026A&A...705A.196H: frank uncertainties are likely underestimated
- 2026A&A...705A.196H: Planet masses from gaps ignore gap depth
- 2026A&A...705A.196H: Classification of discs is phenomenological
- 2026ApJ...999...32W: Simplified parameterizations of the grain size distribution due to having only three resolved mid-IR filters and one mm upper limit per aperture.
- 2026ApJ...999...32W: JWST saturation at r < 25,000 au prevents directly resolving whether the grain size distribution evolves shortly after formation.
- 2026ApJ...999...32W: Degeneracy between grain chemistry (ACAR, ACH2, BE) and grain size prevents determining the hydrogen content of carbon grains from continuum SED fitting alone.
- 2026ApJ...999...32W: Uncertainties in applying bulk laboratory optical constants and bulk graphite heat capacities to ~1 nm grains with discrete energy states.
- 2026ApJ...999...32W: DustEM computes steady-state temperature distributions under a constant radiation field without an absorbing sublimation boundary, which is an oversimplification for grains moving outward in a time-v…
- 2025MNRAS.543..862V: Jet-vs-wind estimate is order-of-magnitude only
- 2025MNRAS.543..862V: Extrapolated millimeter limits for non-detected SgXBs are not constraining relative to the SFXT range
- 2025MNRAS.543..862V: 4U 1907+09 was offset from the phase centre, raising the local RMS and making the ALMA detections formally insignificant
- 2025MNRAS.543..862V: Small number of measurements prevents detailed variability analysis
- 2025MNRAS.543..862V: Systematic uncertainties of the literature wind methods likely exceed the quoted errors
- 2014A&A...567A.125G: Outflow rate is a lower limit due to limited velocity coverage
- 2014A&A...567A.125G: Resolution does not resolve kinematics of inner r~50 pc
- 2015ApJ...801...82H: Missing flux and lack of short baselines; BN outside field of view; flux calibration unreliable for sources outside primary beam; no size correction in SED
- 2020MNRAS.499.5915M: Patchy ISM prevents control-star polarisation versus distance alone from determining the target's interstellar component.
- 2020MNRAS.499.5915M: A composite 425SP observation may have underestimated error.
- 2020MNRAS.499.5915M: Unavailable optical constants prevent a direct composition comparison with the earlier spectral fit.
- 2020MNRAS.499.5915M: Component weightings are degenerate and depend on spectral sampling.
- 2020MNRAS.499.5915M: The model does not reproduce the steep short-wavelength polarisation rise.
- 2020MNRAS.499.5915M: Optical-constant extrapolation and assumed grain shapes prevent tight constraints on exact polarising grain sizes.
- 2020MNRAS.499.5915M: Non-spherical scattering and radiation-pressure-modified size distributions require further testing.
- 2026MNRAS.549ag678P: Empirical constant magnitude offsets applied to align multi-facility optical light curves do not account for the time-evolving color of the transient and leave a systematic flux calibration uncertain…
- 2026MNRAS.549ag678P: Early blackbody temperature and radius estimates are somewhat uncertain because at T > 30 kK the true blackbody peak lies in the unobserved extreme UV.
- 2026MNRAS.549ag678P: Host ISM UV absorption lines in the COS spectrum are saturated, preventing abundance determinations.
- 2026MNRAS.549ag678P: The simplified spherical forward-shock non-thermal SSA model cannot explain the rapid radio flux drop at constant peak frequency after 119 days and omits thermal electron or reverse-shock contributio…
- 2021ApJ...910...21R: Parameter errors come only from MIGRAD; MCMC deferred to future work
- 2021ApJ...910...21R: Fit may be biased by parameter degeneracies
- 2017PASJ...69...20H: ALMA astrometry bugs reported; large offset not explained
- 2018A&A...614A..17B: Present-day mass-loss rate is poorly constrained and should be interpreted with care
- 2018ApJ...853..195W: Optical selection of members may bias the star-forming census; small samples of CO detections.
- 2020MNRAS.491.1174M: Non-LTE effects may introduce uncharacterised spectroscopic errors
- 2020MNRAS.491.1174M: Only three Fe II lines; no unique stable positive-log(g) solution
- 2020MNRAS.491.1174M: Insufficient infrared history to determine changing bolometric luminosity
- 2020MNRAS.491.1174M: Circumstellar absorption estimate excludes grey opacity from large grains
- 2020MNRAS.491.1174M: CO J=2→1 broad excess has uncertain reality and origin
- 2020MNRAS.491.1174M: CO-profile formal errors exclude steady spherical homogeneous-outflow assumption errors
- 2020MNRAS.491.1174M: Mass-loss scaling relations are uncalibrated for this regime and ALMA beam sizes
- 2020MNRAS.491.1174M: VISIR residual telluric contamination limits mineralogy
- 2020MNRAS.491.1174M: Dusty assumptions may be unrealistic; fits and derived radii are indicative
- 2015A&A...581A..97S: Axisymmetric discs only; models not unique
- 2021A&A...650A.153D: Chandra source counts do not constrain the ICM temperature distribution.
- 2021A&A...650A.153D: Most X-ray hardness structures have low significance.
- 2021A&A...650A.153D: Radio resolution, sparse sampling, and weak high-frequency detections prevent a detailed spectral-index map.
- 2021A&A...650A.153D: SZ masses may be biased by nonhydrostatic gas and mass–inner-slope degeneracy.
- 2021A&A...650A.153D: Limited spectral coverage leaves thermal and kinetic SZ strongly degenerate.
- 2021A&A...650A.153D: Interferometric filtering favors compact, low-mass pressure models and limits extrapolation to large scales.
- 2021A&A...650A.153D: Weak-lensing positional precision limits confidence in the southern gas–mass offset.
- 2021A&A...650A.153D: Dust mass and temperature require better high-frequency observations.
- 2019A&A...626A.126A: Lowest ALMA contour has low significance; only a third of observations completed; deeper data needed
- 2023A&A...673A.132B: Pointing inaccuracies in the crowded HST/STIS long-slit observations leave flux calibration uncertainties of order 10% (up to a factor of two in extreme cases), and potential relative calibration div…
- 2023A&A...673A.132B: The adopted NIR extinction law slope (alpha_NIR = 1.61) is likely imperfect for R136, causing systematic residual patterns in NIR bands and high reduced chi^2 values (dominated by H and K_s bands).

### 2018MNRAS.481.1976Z: The [C II] emission as a molecular gas mass tracer in galaxies at low and high redshifts (2018; high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Are z~2 main-sequence galaxies [C II] deficient, and what physical parameter drives [C II] luminosity (is [C II] an empirical molecular gas mass tracer)?
- step 3: Derive optical sizes, positions and astrometric shift from HST
  - method: Pixel-by-pixel SED fitting of HST bands (PSF-matched to F160W) gives stellar mass/SFR maps; exponential profile fit with GALFIT to the stellar mass maps gives sizes and peak positions; HST frame shifted to match ALMA [2.1 Sample selection and ancillary data: "we performed pixel-by-pixel spectral energy distribution (SED) fitting considering all the available *HST* filters mentioned above"] [2.3.1 Individual [C II] detections: "We therefore determined the size of the galaxy disks by fitting the stellar mass maps with an exponential profile"]
  - software: GALFIT
  - parameter: Stellar population age 100 Myr - 2 Gyr, fixed solar metallicity, constant-SFR templates [2.1 Sample selection and ancillary data: "The stellar population age in the models varied between 100 Myr and 2 Gyr, assuming fixed solar metallicity."] [2.1 Sample selection and ancillary data: "templates with constant SFR to limit the degeneracy with dust extinction"]
  - parameter: Coordinate shifts Delta RA = 0 arcsec, Delta DEC = -0.2 arcsec applied to HST [B. Astrometry: "In our analysis we adopt the following coordinate shifts $\Delta RA = 0$”, $\Delta DEC = -0.2$”."]
  - assumption: Calzetti dust extinction; Bruzual & Charlot templates [2.1 Sample selection and ancillary data: "We corrected the fluxes for dust extinction following the prescriptions by (cite: Calzetti2000)."]
- step 9: Measure continuum fluxes and fit IR SEDs for L_IR, M_dust and <U>
  - method: Exponential-profile uv fits of the 450 and 850 um continuum, combined with Spitzer and Herschel photometry in Draine & Li model and modified black body fits [2.4 Continuum emission: "We extracted the continuum flux by fitting the data with an exponential profile, adopting the same procedure described in Section"] [2.4 Continuum emission: "we fitted the IR photometry with (cite: Draine2007) models, supplemented by the use of a single temperature modified black body (MBB) fit to derive a representative dust temperature of the ISM"]
  - software: GILDAS
  - parameter: L_IR integrated over 8-1000 um from SED fitting [Table 1: "IR luminosity integrated over the wavelength range 8 – 1000 $\mu$m as estimated from SED fitting"]
  - parameter: <U> = L_IR/(125 M_dust) [2.4 Continuum emission: "We also determined the average radiation field intensity as $\langle U\rangle = L_{\mathrm{IR}}/(125 M_{\mathrm{dust}})$"]
  - assumption: Modified black body beta = 2.0 [3.1 The [C II] deficit: "with fixed $\beta$ = 2.0 (the same as used in the SED fitting for our sample galaxies)"]
  - assumption: Measured fluxes with uncertainties used even when S/N < 3 instead of upper limits [2.4 Continuum emission: "In these fits we considered the measured *Spitzer*, *Herschel*, and ALMA flux (even if S/N $<$ 3, e.g. there is no detection)"]
  - fitting: Uncertainties on L_IR and M_dust by Monte Carlo simulations; points weighted by their uncertainties [2.4 Continuum emission: "Uncertainties on $L_{\mathrm{IR}}$ and $M_{\mathrm{dust}}$ were quantified using Monte Carlo simulations"] [2.4 Continuum emission: "The contribution of each photometric point to the best fit is weighted by its associated uncertainty."]
  - validation: Conclusions unchanged if upper limits are used in the fits; median L_IR and M_dust agree with literature MS values [2.4 Continuum emission: "If we were to use upper limits in these fits instead our conclusions would not have changed."]
- step 10: Estimate molecular gas masses of the sample
  - method: (1) M_mol = delta_GDR x M_dust with metallicity from stellar mass via a mass-metallicity relation; (2) integrated Schmidt-Kennicutt relation from SFR; the Schmidt-Kennicutt masses are adopted; [C II]-based masses use alpha_[CII] = 31 Msun/… [2.4 Continuum emission: "Given their stellar mass and the mass-metallicity relation by (cite: Zahid2014) we estimated their gas phase metallicity."] [2.4 Continuum emission: "In the following we use the $M_{\mathrm{mol}}$ obtained from the Schmit-Kennicutt relation"]
  - parameter: SFR = 1e-10 L_IR (Kennicutt 1998); SK relation dispersion 0.2 dex [Table 2: "$SFR = 10^{-10}L_{\mathrm{IR}}$"]
  - assumption: Chabrier IMF; flat LCDM with Om = 0.3, OL = 0.7, H0 = 70 [1 Introduction: "We assumed a (cite: Chabrier2003) initial mass function (IMF)"]
  - validation: Dust-based and Schmidt-Kennicutt gas masses consistent for galaxies with 850 um detections [2.4 Continuum emission: "we concluded that both methods give consistent results"]
- step 11: Compile literature samples and fit L_[CII] versus M_mol and alpha_[CII]
  - method: Homogenise literature samples (IR luminosities to 8-1000 um, metallicities to Pettini2004 calibration, alpha_CO = f(metallicity) for CO-based masses), fit the log-log L_[CII]-M_mol relation, and compute alpha_[CII] = L_[CII]/M_mol medians … [C. Literature data: "Also we homogenized all the IR luminosities reporting them to the 8 – 1000 $\mu$m range."] [3.4 Invariance of alpha_[CII] with gas …: "we converted all metallicity estimates to the calibration by (cite: Pettini2004) using the parametrizations by (cite: Kewley2008)"]
  - parameter: alpha_CO metallicity dependence: log alpha_CO = 0.64 - 1.5 (12 + log(O/H) - 8.7) [3.4 Invariance of alpha_[CII] with gas …: "\log \alpha_{\mathrm{CO}} = 0.64 - 1.5 (12 + \log (O/H) - 8.7)"]
  - assumption: alpha_CO slope of -1 or -2 would not change conclusions [3.4 Invariance of alpha_[CII] with gas …: "would not change our conclusions"]
  - fitting: Linear fit in log space; Pearson correlation coefficient quoted [3.2 [C II] as a tracer of molecular gas: "The Pearson test yields a coefficient $\rho = 0.97$"]
  - validation: Scatter of L_[CII]-L'_CO relation compared with that of alpha_[CII]; starburst (SPT) and local samples compared [3.2 [C II] as a tracer of molecular gas: "The mean absolute deviation of the relation is $\sim$ 0.2 dex, similar to that of the $L_{\rm [C~{\small II}]}$ - $M_{\rm mol}$ relation."]
- step 12: Compare L_[CII]/L_IR versus sSFR/sSFR_MS with depletion-time models
  - method: Bin samples by distance from the main sequence, overplot Sargent2014 and Scoville2017 depletion-time models rescaled to the main-sequence [CII]/IR ratio and convolved with a 0.2 dex Gaussian [3.3 The dependence of the [C II]-to-IR …: "predicting how the depletion time of galaxies changes as a function of their distance from the main-sequence and rescaled them"] [3.3 The dependence of the [C II]-to-IR …: "we convolved the models by (cite: Sargent2014) and (cite: Scoville2017) with a Gaussian function with FWHM $\sim$ 0.2 dex"]
  - parameter: Rescaling implies L_[CII]/L_CO ~ 6000 [3.3 The dependence of the [C II]-to-IR …: "we estimated $L_{\mathrm{[C~{\small II}]}}/L_{\mathrm{CO}} \sim 6000$"]
  - assumption: Starbursts defined as > 4 times above the main sequence [1 Introduction: "Throughout this paper we will consider as starbursts all the sources that fall $> 4$ times above the main sequence (cite: Rodighiero2011)."]
  - validation: Rescaled [CII]/CO ratio compared with the 2000-10000 range in the literature [3.3 The dependence of the [C II]-to-IR …: "This is in good agreement with the typical values reported in the literature and ranging between 2000 – 10000"]
- result: Range of L_IR (8-1000 um) of the sample = 3.5e11 - 1.2e12 ± not stated Lsun [2.4 Continuum emission: "lie between $3.5 \times 10^{11}$ – $1.2 \times 10^{12}$ L$_{\odot}$"]
- result: L_[CII]-M_mol relation: log L = -1.28 + 0.98 log M_mol (dispersion 0.3 dex) = intercept -1.28, slope 0.98 ± +/-0.21 (intercept), +/-0.02 (slope) dimensionless [3.2 [C II] as a tracer of molecular gas: "\log L_{\mathrm{[C~{\small II}]}} = -1.28(\pm 0.21) + 0.98(\pm 0.02) \log M_{\mathrm{mol}}"]
- result: Median alpha_[CII] for main-sequence galaxies = 31 ± MAD 0.2 dex (standard deviation 0.3 dex) Msun/Lsun [3.2 [C II] as a tracer of molecular gas: "we get a median $\alpha_{\mathrm{[C~{\small II}]}} = 31$ M$_\odot$/L$_\odot$ with a median absolute deviation of 0.2 dex"]
- result: Median alpha_[CII], local sample = 28 ± MAD 0.2 dex Msun/Lsun [Table 3 (alpha_[CII] estimates): "| Local | 30 | 0.3 | 28 | 0.2 |"]
- result: Median alpha_[CII], high-z sample = 38 ± MAD 0.1 dex Msun/Lsun [Table 3 (alpha_[CII] estimates): "| High-$z$ | 35 | 0.2 | 38 | 0.1 |"]
- result: Median alpha_[CII], SPT lensed starburst sample = 22 ± not stated Msun/Lsun [3.2 [C II] as a tracer of molecular gas: "We obtained a median $\alpha_{\mathrm{[CII]}} = 22$ M$_\odot$/L$_\odot$ for this sample"]
- result: L_[CII]/L_CO implied by rescaled depletion-time models = ~6000 ± not stated dimensionless [3.3 The dependence of the [C II]-to-IR …: "we estimated $L_{\mathrm{[C~{\small II}]}}/L_{\mathrm{CO}} \sim 6000$"]
- result: Astrometric offset of HST relative to ALMA in declination = -0.2 ± > 3 sigma significant arcsec [2.2 Details of ALMA observations: "instead significant in declination ($\Delta DEC = -0.2$”, $> 3\sigma$ significant)"]
- limitation (authors): Fluxes depend critically on the assumed model size taken from optical images [2.3.1 Individual [C II] detections: "However, the returned fluxes critically depend on the model size that we used and that we determined from the optical images."]
- limitation (authors): Source size cannot be constrained from individual sources because of low S/N [2.3.1 Individual [C II] detections: "Unfortunately, the size of the emission cannot be constrained from the data on individual sources, given the limited S/N ratio."]
- limitation (authors): Observed in a more extended configuration than requested, giving 5-10 times higher flux errors [2.2 Details of ALMA observations: "Our sources were therefore resolved."]
- not stated (card): CASA and pipeline version used for the Band 9 and Band 7 reductions, and whether the archive-delivered calibration was used as is; Flux, bandpass and phase calibrators and the Band 9 flux scale uncertainty; Whether any self-calibration was done; Which array (12-m / 7-m) and antenna numbers were used; Exact channel ranges (optimal channel range) used for each detection and the line-free channels u…

### 2020MNRAS.498.4192F: ALMA characterizes the dust temperature of z ∼ 5.5 star-forming galaxies (2020; high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Constrain infrared SED shapes, dust temperatures, emissivity indices, infrared luminosities, and molecular gas masses of four main-sequence galaxies at z~5.5.
- step 2: Choose the optical-depth prescription
  - method: Compare Band 6-normalized fluxes with modified-blackbody models for λ0=100 and 200 μm over a range of SED temperatures. [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "with fixed $\alpha=2$ and $\beta_{\rm d}=2$) for $\lambda_{0}=100\,{\rm \mu m}$ (left) and $\lambda_{0}=200\,{\rm \mu m}$ (right) for a range of SED temperatures"] [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "The observed fluxes of our galaxies normalised to Band 6 (at $205\,{\rm \mu m}$) are shown by symbols."]
  - parameter: Comparison grid: α=2, βd=2, λ0=100 or 200 μm. [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "with fixed $\alpha=2$ and $\beta_{\rm d}=2$) for $\lambda_{0}=100\,{\rm \mu m}$ (left) and $\lambda_{0}=200\,{\rm \mu m}$ (right)"]
  - assumption: Adopt λ0=100 μm; retain both λ0 choices for HZ6. [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "The emission at $\sim100\,\mu m$ is therefore likely optically thin and we therefore assume $\lambda_{0}=100\,{\rm \mu m}$ in the following."] [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "With the current data, it is not possible to make further conclusions and we therefore show in the following derivations using both values of $\lambda_0$ for *HZ6*."]
  - validation: Reasonable changes in α and βd do not change the optical-depth conclusion. [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "Different values of $\alpha$ and $\beta_{\rm d}$ in a reasonable range do not change this conclusion."]
- step 3: Fit the infrared SED
  - method: Fit all three ALMA continuum measurements with a single modified black body plus mid-infrared power-law using PyMC3 MCMC. [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "We use the Markov Chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) method provided by the Python package PyMC3"] [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "to fit the infrared SEDs of our four galaxies including all the three ALMA continuum measurements described above"]
  - software: PyMC3
  - parameter: 18 000 samples; target acceptance 0.99. [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "We draw $18\,000$ samples in total with a target acceptance of $0.99$, which we found to provide the best performance."]
  - parameter: Fit in linear space. [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "We found that fitting in linear space is more appropriate given the errors of the data."]
  - assumption: α=2.0; λ0=100 μm, with an additional λ0=200 μm solution for HZ6. [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "we fix the mid-IR power-law slope to $\alpha=2.0$ as suggested by the measurements in (cite: CASEY12)."] [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "we therefore assume $\lambda_{0}=100\,{\rm \mu m}$ in the following."]
  - assumption: Flat dust-temperature prior; Gaussian βd prior centered on 1.8 with σ=0.5. [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "we adopt a flat prior for the dust temperature, and a Gaussian prior for $\beta_{\rm d}$ with a $\sigma(\beta_{\rm d})=0.5$ centered on $1.8$ (cite: HILDEBRAND83)."]
  - assumption: Gaussian normalization prior in linear space around a Band 7-derived initial guess. [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "The normalisation is also sampled with a Gaussian prior in linear space around an initial guess derived by the normalisation in Band 7."]
  - fitting: No-U-Turn Sampler, an extension to Hamilton Monte Carlo. [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "To perform the fitting, we use the *No-U-Turn Sampler* (cite: HOFFMAN11), which is an extension to the Hamilton Monte Carlo algorithm (cite: NEAL12) and is less sensitive to tuning."]
- step 4: Derive temperatures and infrared luminosities
  - method: Derive Tpeak from the SED peak using Wien’s law; integrate best-fit SEDs over 3–1100 μm for LIR and 42.5–122.5 μm for LFIR. [Conclusions: "The peak temperature is derived using Wien’s law"] [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "The total infrared luminosities ($L_{{\rm IR}}$) are derived by integrating the best-fit SED between $3-1100\,{\rm \mu m}$"]
  - parameter: Tpeak [K] = 2.898×10^3 [μm K] / λpeak [μm]. [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "T_{\rm peak}\,{\rm [K]} = \frac{ 2.898\times10^3\,{\rm [\mu m\,K]}}{ \lambda_{\rm peak}\,{\rm [\mu m]}}."]
  - assumption: ΛCDM: H0=70 km s^-1 Mpc^-1, ΩΛ=0.70, Ωm=0.30. [Introduction: "Throughout this work, we assume a $\Lambda$CDM cosmology with $H_0 = 70\,{\rm km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}$, $\Omega_\Lambda = 0.70$, and $\Omega_{\rm m} = 0.30$."]
  - assumption: TSED and Tpeak measure light-weighted dust temperatures. [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "Note that both are a measure of the *light-weighted* dust temperature."]
  - fitting: Use best-fit SEDs and MCMC uncertainty contours. [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "Figure [4](#fig:temperaturetraces) shows the derived SED (left) and peak (middle) dust temperature as well as total infrared luminosity (right) contours ($1\sigma$) as a function of the emissivity index $\beta_{\rm d}$ for our four galaxies."]
  - validation: Peak temperature and total infrared luminosity insensitive to assumed λ0. [Infrared SED Fits and Dust Temperature: "We note that the peak temperature and the total infrared luminosity is insensitive to the assumed $\lambda_0$."]
- step 5: Derive molecular gas masses and fractions
  - method: Extrapolate Band 6 rest-frame 205 μm flux using the full βd posterior and SCOVILLE16 equation (16); derive fgas=Mmol/(Mmol+Mstellar). The extrapolation wavelength is printed as 805 μm. [Molecular Gas Masses from the RJ dust c…: "that is extrapolated to $805\,{\rm \mu m}$ using the full probability distribution of $\beta_{\rm d}$ from our MCMC fit and equation (16) in (cite: SCOVILLE16) with similar assumptions"] [Molecular Gas Masses from the RJ dust c…: "using the observed flux at rest-frame $205\,{\rm \mu m}$"]
  - parameter: Band 6 input flux in mJy; luminosity distance DL(z) in Gpc. [Molecular Gas Masses from the RJ dust c…: "Band 6, $S_{\rm \nu_{obs}}$ in mJy"] [Molecular Gas Masses from the RJ dust c…: "In this case, $D_{\rm L}(z)$ is the luminosity distance at redshift $z$ in Gpc"]
  - assumption: α850=6.7×10^19 erg s^-1 Hz^-1 M⊙^-1. [Molecular Gas Masses from the RJ dust c…: "we assume $\alpha_{850} = 6.7 \times 10^{19}\,{\rm erg\,s^{-1}\,Hz^{-1}\,M_{\odot}^{-1}}$"]
  - assumption: Mass-weighted dust temperature 25 K for the Rayleigh–Jeans correction. [Molecular Gas Masses from the RJ dust c…: "$\Gamma_{\rm RJ}(\nu_{\rm obs},T_{d},z)$ is the correction for departure in the rest-frame of the Planck function from Rayleigh-Jeans and depends on the *mass-weighted* dust temperature"] [Molecular Gas Masses from the RJ dust c…: "For the latter, we adopt $25\,{\rm K}$"]
  - fitting: Propagate the full βd probability distribution from the SED MCMC fit. [Molecular Gas Masses from the RJ dust c…: "using the full probability distribution of $\beta_{\rm d}$ from our MCMC fit"]
  - validation: Compare with dynamical-minus-stellar masses and CO estimates; test 35 K mass-weighted temperature. [Molecular Gas Masses from the RJ dust c…: "Generally, we find an agreement within a factor of two ($<1\sigma$) between gas masses derived from dust continuum and dynamical masses."] [Molecular Gas Masses from the RJ dust c…: "The CO-gas mass estimate of *HZ10* is a factor $2.5$ larger ($\sim1\sigma$ discrepancy) than what we measure from dust continuum and dynamical masses."]
- step 6: Derive SFRs and depletion times
  - method: Convert LIR to SFR with KENNICUTT98; compute tdepl=Mmol/SFRIR. [Molecular Gas Masses from the RJ dust c…: "From the total infrared luminosity we can derive the total SFRs using the (cite: KENNICUTT98) relation and from that the gas depletion time via $t_{\rm depl} = \rm M_{\rm mol}$/SFR$_{\rm IR}$."]
  - assumption: SFRs normalized to a Chabrier IMF. [Introduction: "stellar masses and SFRs are normalised to a (cite: CHABRIER03) initial mass function (IMF)."]
  - validation: Compare depletion times with the decreasing-redshift trend from SCOVILLE16. [Molecular Gas Masses from the RJ dust c…: "This is in agreement with the trend of decreasing depletion time with higher redshifts (cite: SCOVILLE16)."]
- step 7: Compare temperatures and luminosities with literature
  - method: Compare temperature evolution, z>5 DSFGs, and previous single-band luminosities; remeasure peak temperatures for two z>6 literature samples. [Rising Temperature towards High Redshif…: "Figure [6](#fig:tempevolution) puts our measurements at $z\sim5.5$ into context with measurements from the literature at $z<4$ and $z>6$."] [Rising Temperature towards High Redshif…: "For the latter two, we have re-measured the peak temperature with our method."]
  - parameter: MAGNELLI14 comparison selected at log(LIR/L⊙)~10.5–12.5. [Rising Temperature towards High Redshif…: "For the latter, we select a similar range in total infrared luminosity as our sample ($\log(L_{{\rm IR}}/{\rm L_{\odot}}) \sim 10.5-12.5$)."]
  - assumption: For DSFG comparison use TSED and LFIR; normalize the optically thick L∝T^4 relation to the DSFG median. [Comparison with Dusty Star-Forming Gala…: "For a fair comparison, we show SED dust temperatures and far-IR luminosities"] [Comparison with Dusty Star-Forming Gala…: "This is indicated by the $L\propto T^{4}$ relation (optically thick case) normalised to the median of the DSFGs"]
  - validation: ALPINE stacked-SED luminosities agree within <0.2 dex. [A Final Note on Implication on $L_{{\rm…: "This approach leads to consistent total infrared luminosities with ours within less than $0.2\,{\rm {\rm dex}}$ ($<60\%$ difference)."]

### 2024ApJ...965..108U: ALMA Lensing Cluster Survey: Full Spectral Energy Distribution Analysis of z ∼ 0.5─6 Lensed Galaxies Detected with millimeter Observations (2024; high_z_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; large_program_or_survey)
- question: UV-to-millimeter properties, dust properties, and obscured AGNs in the ALCS sample
- step 5: Fit galaxy and AGN SEDs
  - method: CIGALE multi-component UV-to-millimeter SED fitting; compare fits with and without an AGN [SED modeling with `CIGALE`: "We conduct multi-component SED modeling to the UV to millimeter photometry of the ALCS tier-1/2/3/4/5/6 samples"] [AGN emission: "To identify whether a galaxy hosts an AGN or not, we utilize the Bayesian Information Criterion"]
  - software: CIGALE, EThemis, Skirtor
  - parameter: AGN required for ΔBIC>10; AGN always excluded for tier-2/3/4/5/6 [AGN emission: "If the addition of an AGN component reduces BIC by more than 10"] [AGN emission: "For the tier-2/3/4/5/6 samples, the AGN component is always excluded"]
  - parameter: Basic grid for tier-1/2/5/6; limited dust-emission grid for tier-3/4; extended AGN grid for A2744-ID227 [Detailed description of SED modeling: "First, the ALCS tier-1/2/5/6 samples were analyzed with a basic parameter set"] [Detailed description of SED modeling: "Hence, we reanalyzed the SED with an extended parameter set of the AGN module"]
  - parameter: Polar dust: emissivity index 1.6; temperature 150 K; opacity unity at 200 μm [AGN emission: "We also add a polar dust emission modeled by a single graybody, where we assume an emissivity index of 1.6, temperature of 150 K, and opacity of unity at 200 $\mu$m."]
  - assumption: Energy balance between UV/optical absorption and infrared re-emission [SED modeling with `CIGALE`: "`CIGALE` assumes energy balance between UV/optical absorption and infrared re-emission"]
  - assumption: Delayed SFH plus exponentially decaying burst [Stellar emission, dust attenuation, and…: "we employ a delayed star-formation history (SFH), assuming an additional burst with exponential decay."]
  - assumption: Bruzual–Charlot stellar templates; Chabrier IMF; modified Calzetti attenuation; Inoue nebular emission [Stellar emission, dust attenuation, and…: "The simple stellar population (SSP) is modeled with the stellar templates of (cite: 2003MNRAS.344.1000B), assuming the (cite: 2003PASP..115..763C) initial mass function."] [Stellar emission, dust attenuation, and…: "The dust attenuation is modeled with the Calzetti starburst attenuation law (cite: 2000ApJ...533..682C), where we allow slightly steeper or flatter curves than the original one"]
  - fitting: Model grid; BIC=χ²+k ln(n), with non-reduced χ², k degrees of freedom, n photometric points [Estimation of physical properties: "we calculate the likelihood-weighted mean of all the models on the grid as a Bayesian estimation"] [AGN emission: "The BIC is calculated as $\mathrm{BIC}=\chi^2+k\times\ln(n)$"]
  - validation: χ²/dof<5; inspect SED residuals; reject R0032-ID57 AGN classification using other multiple images and lack of obvious AGN signs [Results: "We successfully reproduce the SEDs of the ALCS tier-1/2/3/4/5/6 samples with a fit quality of $\chi^2/\mathrm{dof}<5$."] [Overall properties of ALCS sample: "However, the other multiple images (R0032-ID32/53/55/58) are not classified as AGNs, and no obvious signs of AGN are found in the SED of R0032-ID57."]
- step 6: Estimate intrinsic physical properties
  - method: Likelihood-weighted marginalized-PDF means; derive sSFR; divide quantitative variables by magnification factors [Estimation of physical properties: "we calculate the likelihood-weighted mean of the marginalized PDF with a log-uniform prior for the former parameters and with a uniform prior for the latter, respectively."] [Estimation of physical properties: "The specific star-formation rates (sSFR = SFR / stellar mass) are derived from the stellar masses and the SFRs"]
  - software: CIGALE
  - parameter: Logarithmic: SFR, stellar mass, dust luminosity, dust mass [Estimation of physical properties: "where we treat the following parameters in a logarithmic space:"] [Estimation of physical properties: "Star-formation rate (SFR)"]
  - parameter: Linear: dust temperature, E(B−V), IRX, β, δ, bolometric AGN luminosity [Estimation of physical properties: "while the following parameters are treated in a linear space:"] [Estimation of physical properties: "Infrared excess ($\mathrm{IRX}=\log (L_{\mathrm{dust}}/L_{\mathrm{UV}})$)"]
  - assumption: SFR averaged over the last 10 Myr; dust luminosity/mass exclude AGN-torus dust [Estimation of physical properties: "refers to the average star-formation rate in the last 10 Myr."] [Estimation of physical properties: "to describe those of interstellar dust (i.e., not including those in the AGN torus)."]
  - assumption: Flat universe; H0=70.4 km s−1 Mpc−1; ΩM=0.272; errors 1σ unless specified [Introduction: "Throughout the paper, we assume a flat universe with $H_0=70.4\ \mathrm{km\ s^{-1}\ Mpc^{-1}}$ and $\Omega_M=0.272$ (cite: 2011ApJS..192...18K)."] [Introduction: "If not specifically mentioned, errors correspond to $1\sigma$ confidence."]
  - fitting: Log-uniform priors for logarithmic quantities; uniform priors for linear quantities [Estimation of physical properties: "we calculate the likelihood-weighted mean of the marginalized PDF with a log-uniform prior for the former parameters and with a uniform prior for the latter, respectively."]
  - validation: sSFR confidence intervals propagate stellar-mass and SFR uncertainties [Estimation of physical properties: "the confidence intervals of the sSFRs are calculated by propagating the uncertainties associated with the stellar masses and the SFRs."]
- step 7: Assess redshift and SED-model systematics
  - method: MAGPHYS photo-z and fixed-EAZY-redshift fits; compare CIGALE and MAGPHYS physical properties with Passing-Bablok regression [Validity of photometric redshift and Im…: "First, we calculate the photometric redshifts of tier-1/2/5/6 sources by optical to millimeter SED analysis with `MAGPHYS photo-z extension`"] [Validity of photometric redshift and Im…: "where the redshifts are fixed at the photometric ones derived by `EAZY`."]
  - software: CIGALE, MAGPHYS photo-z extension, MAGPHYS high-z extension (v2), EAZY
  - parameter: Exclude ALCS-XAGNs and ALCS-nonXAGNs from MAGPHYS tests; code comparison uses tier-1 [Comparison between `CIGALE` and `MAGPHY…: "For a conservative discussion, we only discuss the tier-1 samples"] [Comparison between `CIGALE` and `MAGPHY…: "since the current version of `MAGPHYS` cannot treat an AGN component, ALCS-XAGNs and ALCS-nonXAGNs are excluded."]
  - fitting: Passing-Bablok regression for inter-code property conversions [Comparison between `CIGALE` and `MAGPHY…: "We perform Passing-Bablok regression analysis for each parameter, where the conversion relation is shown at the bottom of each panel."]
  - validation: Compare photo-z against spectroscopy and EAZY; compare variable/fixed-redshift properties; rerun CIGALE with the MAGPHYS attenuation model [Validity of photometric redshift and Im…: "The left panel plots photometric redshifts estimated by `MAGPHYS photo-z extension` versus spectroscopic ones."] [Validity of photometric redshift and Im…: "plots the physical properties estimated with variable redshifts versus those estimated at fixed redshifts."]
- step 8: Compare star formation and dust properties
  - method: Compare SFR–mass, IRX–mass, IRX–β, attenuation slope, dust temperature, and dust mass with literature samples [SFR versus stellar mass: "Figure [8](#figure:Ms_SFR) plots the relation between stellar mass and SFR."] [IRX versus stellar mass: "The left panel of Figure [9](#figure:beta_IRX) shows the relation between stellar mass and IRX."]
  - parameter: Apply −0.37 dex to stellar masses of AS2UDS, ASAGAO, ALESS, ASPECS [Comparison samples: "Accordingly, we impose a systematic offset of –0.37 dex in the stellar mass of the AS2UDS, ASAGAO, ALESS, and ASPECS samples."]
  - parameter: Temperature comparison: Herschel detections over 2σ for ALCS, ASPECS, AS2UDS; well-sampled ALESS subset [Dust temperature: "For a conservative discussion, we only plot the sources that are detected with Herschel over 2$\sigma$ for the ALCS, ASPECS, and AS2UDS samples"] [Dust temperature: "For the same reason, the ALESS sample is limited to the well-sampled subset reported in (cite: 2021ApJ...919...30D)."]
  - parameter: Characteristic temperature Tdustpeak[K]=5099/λpeak[μm]; ALCS conversion approximately 1.7×Tdust [Characteristic dust temperature: "T_{\mathrm{dust}}^{\mathrm{peak}}\,[\mathrm{K}]=5099/\lambda^{\mathrm{peak}}\,[\mu\mathrm{m}]"] [Conversion method of dust temperature: "T_{\mathrm{dust}}^{\mathrm{peak}}\simeq 1.7\times T_{\mathrm{dust}}"]
  - assumption: Characteristic temperature uses the dust-SED peak in Jy and is not intrinsic dust temperature [Characteristic dust temperature: "we utilize the peak wavelength in the dimension of “Jy”"] [Characteristic dust temperature: "the characteristic dust temperature does not denote the intrinsic temperature of dust"]
  - validation: Inter-code comparison found no obvious systematic differences requiring corrections to SFR, dust luminosity, or dust mass [Comparison samples: "The SFRs, dust luminosities, and dust masses are not corrected, because we did not find obvious systematic differences for these parameters."]
- step 10: Assess AGN obscuration
  - method: Compare SED bolometric AGN luminosities against obscuration-dependent X-ray upperbounds [Comparison with X-ray luminosity upperb…: "plots the X-ray luminosity upperbounds with various assumptions on line-of-sight obscuration, against bolometric AGN luminosity derived by `CIGALE`."]
  - software: CIGALE
  - assumption: Empirical local-AGN Lbol/L2–10 keV=20 [Comparison with X-ray luminosity upperb…: "the empirical bolometric-to-X-ray luminosity relation of local AGNs ($L_{\mathrm{bol}}/L_{\text{2--10 keV}}=20$; (cite: 2007MNRAS.381.1235V))"]
  - validation: Consider X-ray-weak AGNs as an alternative allowing less obscured cases [Comparison with X-ray luminosity upperb…: "If this is applicable in our sample, the ALCS-nonXAGNs can be consistent with their X-ray luminosity upperbounds even in the less obscured cases"]
- step 11: Estimate AGN luminosity density
  - method: Multiply summed best-fit AGN/infrared luminosity ratios by the integrated U/LIRG infrared luminosity density [Cosmological evolution of AGN luminosit…: "These values are calculated by comparing the summed best-fit AGN luminosities and infrared luminosities of the U/LIRGs in the tier-1 sample in each redshift bin."] [Cosmological evolution of AGN luminosit…: "Then, we apply the luminosity ratio to the infrared luminosity density of high-redshift U/LIRGs and obtain the AGN luminosity density."]
  - parameter: Redshift bins z=1–2, 2–3, 3–4 [Cosmological evolution of AGN luminosit…: "First, we estimate the average $L_{\mathrm{AGN}}$-to-$L_{\mathrm{IR}}$ ratio in redshift bins of $z=1\text{--}2$, $z=2\text{--}3$, and $z=3\text{--}4$."]
  - parameter: Integrate the literature infrared luminosity function over 10^11 L⊙<LIR<10^13 L⊙ [Cosmological evolution of AGN luminosit…: "integrating the infrared luminosity function derived by (cite: 2023arXiv230301658F) over $10^{11}L_{\odot}<L_{\mathrm{IR}}<10^{13}L_{\odot}$."]
  - assumption: Infrared luminosity excludes the AGN component; include previously analyzed ALCS-XAGNs [Cosmological evolution of AGN luminosit…: "the infrared luminosity mentioned in this subsection refers to the one that does not contain the AGN component"] [Cosmological evolution of AGN luminosit…: "For the ALCS-XAGNs, we employ the values derived by the SED analysis performed by (cite: 2023ApJ...945..121U)."]
  - validation: Compare with luminosity density estimated by X-ray surveys below 10 keV [Cosmological evolution of AGN luminosit…: "We also plot the AGN luminosity density estimated by X-ray surveys below 10 keV (cite: 2014ApJ...786..104U)."]
- result: Main-sequence sources within a factor of four above or below the sequence = 82/162 [51%] ± not stated dimensionless [SFR versus stellar mass: "82/162 \[51%\] sources are in the ranges four times above or below the star-forming main sequence"]
