### 2018ApJ...869...27V: A Survey of Atomic Carbon [C I] in High-redshift Main-sequence Galaxies (2018; nearby_galaxies, high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Is [C I](3P1-3P0) a good tracer of molecular gas in main-sequence galaxies at z~1.2, and how do its luminosity ratios and abundance compare with starbursts, SMGs and local LIRGs?
- step 2: derive L_IR, M_dust and SFR via SED fitting
  - method: Fit super-deblended photometry with expanded Draine & Li 2007 models plus AGN torus; bootstrap for errors [2.2 Ancillary data: "We then bootstrapped the values within the observed errors to estimate the statistical uncertainties on the derived quantities."]
  - parameter: L_IR over 8-1000 um; SFR = L_IR/(9.86e9) [2.2 Ancillary data: "$\mathrm{SFR} = L_{\mathrm{IR}}\,[L_\odot] / (9.86 \times 10^9)$ $M_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$"]
  - assumption: Chabrier IMF [1 Introduction: "a Chabrier initial mass function (cite: chabrier_2003)"]
  - fitting: upper limits included as nominal values weighted by large uncertainties [2.2 Ancillary data: "While fitting the SEDs, we included the upper limits in every band, modeling the nominal values weighted by their large uncertainties."]
- step 3: compute line luminosities and fit L_IR-L'[CI] relation
  - method: L' from Solomon & Vanden Bout formula; Spearman/Pearson correlation; linear regression in log-log with Bayesian and chi2 fits [4.1 L_IR - L'[CI] relation: "We further applied a linear regression analysis on $\mathrm{log}(L_{\rm IR})$ – $\mathrm{log}($$L'_{\mathrm{[C\,\scriptscriptstyle{I}\scriptstyle{]}}^3P_1\,-\, ^3P_0}$$)$ both using a Bayesian (LINMIXERR.PRO, (cite: kelly_2007))"]
  - software: LINMIXERR.PRO, MPFIT.PRO
  - parameter: AGN and QSOs excluded; 57 detections and 10 upper limits [4.1 L_IR - L'[CI] relation: "We modeled a total of $57$ [C I]$(^3P_1\,-\,^{3}P_0)$-detected galaxies and $10$ upper limits."]
  - assumption: cosmology Omega_m=0.3, Omega_L=0.7, H0=70 [1 Introduction: "$\Omega_{\rm m} = 0.3$, $\Omega_{\rm \Lambda} = 0.7$, and $H_0 = 70$ km s$^{-1}$ Mpc$^{-1}$"]
  - fitting: Bayesian (Kelly 2007) with upper limits; chi2 (MPFIT) for check [4.1 L_IR - L'[CI] relation: "The two algorithms we applied provided fully consistent results within the uncertainties, and the effect of upper limits is negligible."]
  - validation: two algorithms consistent [4.1 L_IR - L'[CI] relation: "The two algorithms we applied provided fully consistent results within the uncertainties, and the effect of upper limits is negligible."]
- result: Slope of log L_IR - log L'[CI] relation = 0.78 ± 0.05 dimensionless [4.1 L_IR - L'[CI] relation: "The Bayesian best fit model returns a sublinear slope of $0.78\pm0.05$ with an observed scatter of $\sigma=0.26$ dex."]
- result: Mean log(L'[CI]/L_IR) for 16 MS galaxies = -2.43 ± 0.06 log K km/s pc^2 / Lsun [4.1 L_IR - L'[CI] relation: "for $16$ MS galaxies is $(-2.43\pm0.06)$"]
- limitation (authors): Abundance differences driven by assumed alpha_CO and delta_GDR; degeneracies cannot be broken with current data [Conclusions: "a degeneracy that cannot be broken with the available data and tools"]
- limitation (authors): Systematic uncertainties on gas masses not included [4.4 Gas masses from dust and CO: "Possible larger systematic uncertainties affecting the gas masses are not listed in the error budget"]
- not stated (card): CASA version and pipeline version; Channel selection and exact velocity widths per source for flux measurement; Continuum/line-free channels and local continuum treatment details; Details of the iterative uv-fitting (see Daddi et al. 2015); SED fit settings (DL07 library expansion, bootstrap count); Survival analysis implementation details; Redshift/CO line fluxes for Daddi et al. in prep. data; …

### 2014ApJ...796...84R: ALMA Imaging of Gas and Dust in a Galaxy Protocluster at Redshift 5.3: [C II] Emission in "Typical" Galaxies and Dusty Starbursts ≈1 Billion Years after the Bi… (2014; high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the gas (CII, OH) and dust properties of the SMG AzTEC-3 and of 'typical' Lyman-break galaxies in the z=5.3 protocluster?
- step 3: Fit dust SED of AzTEC-3
  - method: Affine-invariant MCMC modified blackbody joined to a power law; optically thin and thick models [3.4 SED, AzTEC-3: "We used an affine-invariant Markov Chain Monte-Carlo (MCMC) approach"]
  - software: mbb_emcee
  - parameter: Gaussian prior beta = 1.9±0.3; normalization at observed 500 µm [3.4 SED, AzTEC-3: "A weak Gaussian prior of 1.9$\pm$0.3 is adopted for $\beta$."]
  - assumption: kappa_nu = 2.64 m^2/kg at 125 µm (Dunne et al. 2003) [3.4 SED, AzTEC-3: "a dust absorption coefficient of $\kappa_\nu$=2.64 m$^2$kg$^{-1}$ at 125 $\mu$m"]
  - fitting: affine-invariant MCMC; chi2 19.44/10 dof (thin), 14.63/9 dof (thick) [3.4 SED, AzTEC-3: "The best fit solution has a $\chi^2$ of 14.63 for 9 degrees of freedom"]
  - validation: Compared chi2 of optically thin vs thick models [3.4 SED, AzTEC-3: "suggesting a significantly better fit than in the optically-thin case"]
- step 4: Constrain FIR luminosity, SFR and dust mass of LBGs
  - method: Normalise nearby galaxy templates to Subaru i-band; integrate dwarf templates over 42.5-122.5 µm; scale to ALMA limit [3.4 SED, LBGs: "we thus integrated the dwarf galaxy templates over their infrared peaks."]
  - parameter: T_dust=30 K, beta=1.5 for dust mass limit [3.4 SED, LBGs: "Assuming a dust temperature of $T_{\rm dust}$=30 K and an opacity power law index of $\beta$=1.5"]
  - assumption: Chabrier IMF for SFR from L_FIR; LBG-2/3 share LBG-1 redshift and SED shape [3.5 Derivation of Further Galaxy Proper…: "We assume a Chabrier ((cite: cha03)) stellar initial mass function (IMF) to derive star formation rates from $L_{\rm FIR}$."]
  - fitting: template matching [Spectral Energy Distribution, Lyman-Bre…: "Given the detailed available photometry from COSMOS, we match galaxy templates to the observed SED of LBG-1"]
  - validation: UV-based SFR of 22 Msun/yr compared with FIR limit [4.2 LBGs: "This is comparable to the best SFR$_{\rm FIR}$ limit, and again consistent with low dust extinction."]
- result: AzTEC-3 L_FIR = 1.10e13 ± +0.22/-0.21 e13 Lsun [3.4 SED, AzTEC-3: "(1.10$^{+0.22}_{-0.21}$)$\times$10$^{13}$ $L_{\odot}$"]
- result: AzTEC-3 T_dust (optically thick) = 88.4 ± +9.8/-9.6 K [3.4 SED, AzTEC-3: "$T_{\rm dust}$=88.4$^{+9.8}_{-9.6}$ K"]
- result: AzTEC-3 dust mass = 2.66e8 ± +0.74/-0.80 e8 Msun [3.4 SED, AzTEC-3: "$M_{\rm dust}$=2.66$^{+0.74}_{-0.80}$$\times$10$^8$ $M_{\odot}$"]
- result: LBG-1 FIR SFR upper limit = <18–54 ± not stated Msun/yr [abstract: "suggesting far-infrared SFRs of $<$18–54 $M_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$"]
- result: LBG-1 UV SFR = 22 ± not stated Msun/yr [4.2 LBGs: "This yields SFR$_{\rm UV}$=22 $M_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$."]
- result: AzTEC-3 dust optical depth at 157.7 µm = 1.28 ± 0.04 dimensionless [4.1 AzTEC-3: "$\tau_{\rm 157.7\,{\mu}m}$=1.28$\pm$0.04"]
- limitation (authors): Spatial resolution insufficient to resolve structure; higher resolution needed [3.2 [CII] Line Emission: "Observations at higher spatial resolution are required to further resolve the detailed structure of the interstellar medium in this massive starburst galaxy."]
- limitation (authors): Photometric uncertainties near the SED peak are large [3.4 SED, AzTEC-3: "the uncertainties on the photometry close to the peak of the SED are $>$30%–50%"]
- not stated (card): CASA/AIPS versions; CLEAN mask, threshold, cell and image size; Line-free channels and continuum subtraction method; Which processing (AIPS vs CASA) produced the final maps and fluxes; Aperture/extraction regions for spectra of LBG components; MCMC priors on T_dust, alpha, lambda0 and run settings beyond beta prior; Which dwarf templates were used and their normalisation; Exact virial coefficient…

### 2024A&A...687A.217D: ALMA-IMF. XII. Point-process mapping of 15 massive protoclusters (2024; star_formation, milky_way_ism; large_program_or_survey)
- question: Measure luminosity, mass, column density and dust temperature of the 15 ALMA-IMF protoclusters at 2.5 arcsec resolution with PPMAP SED fitting, then extract luminosity peaks and compare luminosity-to-mass ratios with evolutionary stage and…
- step 1: Preprocess input maps
  - method: Repair or drop saturated Herschel maps, build PSFs for all instruments, apply Herschel colour corrections, and set per-map errors; use free-free-subtracted and native Band 6 versions [6.1.1 Correction of saturated pixels: "we chose to entirely remove these maps from the pool of input data"] [2.3 Hi-GAL survey: "This interpolation process was performed using Gaussian process regression, as implemented in the"]
  - software: astrofix, astropy.convolution (Gaussian2DKernel)
  - parameter: Maps with 25% or more saturated area removed (11 of 124 images); maps with less than 25% saturated were interpolated [6.1.1 Correction of saturated pixels: "In severe cases of saturation (11 out of 124 images in our study)"] [6.1.1 Correction of saturated pixels: "In cases where the saturation level was lower (less than 25% of the map is saturated), we applied interpolation to estimate the missing values."]
  - parameter: 25 of 124 images interpolated, median 6 pixels per image [6.1.1 Correction of saturated pixels: "25 out of 124 images underwent interpolation for the present study, with a median of 6 interpolated pixels per image"]
  - parameter: Herschel PSFs replaced by versions on 2048 x 2048 frames [6.1.2 Preparation of point spread funct…: "We replaced these files with new ones projected on a larger $2048 \times 2048$ frame."]
  - parameter: Per-map error = MAD of empty areas plus flux-calibration error times sky background [6.2.1 Determination of measurement erro…: "Therefore, we incorporated the median absolute deviation measurement, along with the systematic error on the absolute flux calibration of the sky background"]
  - parameter: Gaussian synthetic beams for SABOCA, LABOCA and ALMA [6.1.2 Preparation of point spread funct…: "We produced our own synthetic beam profile for SABOCA, LABOCA, and ALMA observations using the"]
  - assumption: Herschel colour corrections assume a modified blackbody with beta = 2 [6.2.2 Herschel color correction: "assuming a MBB profile with $\beta = 2$"]
  - assumption: Free-free subtraction assumes electron temperature of 7000 K and region-dependent helium abundance [6.1.3 Free-free emission in the ALMA 1.…: "This was achieved by assuming an electron temperature of $7000$ K and variable helium abundances"]
  - validation: Interpolation uncertainty measured by comparing PPMAP runs with and without the interpolated maps [6.1.1 Correction of saturated pixels: "we performed two iterations of the PPMAP analysis: one with the interpolated maps and the other without any interpolation"]
- step 2: Run PPMAP SED fitting (Run1 and Run2)
  - method: Multi-resolution Bayesian SED fitting of 70 um to 1.3 mm maps with PPMAP; Run1 uses free-free-subtracted Band 6 for column density and temperature, Run2 uses native Band 6 for luminosity [1 Introduction: "Through PPMAP, we perform SED fitting for a large set of continuum observations, while preserving the high-angular resolution capabilities of ALMA"] [6.1.3 Free-free emission in the ALMA 1.…: "we either used the free-free subtracted maps to obtain the dust temperature and column density maps"]
  - software: PPMAP
  - parameter: 8 MBB components, 10-50 K [3.2 PPMAP analysis: "we employ 8 MBB components with temperature values ranging from 10 K to 50 K"]
  - parameter: Opacity index fixed to beta = 1.8 [3.2 PPMAP analysis: "We therefore fix the opacity index to $\beta = 1.8$"]
  - parameter: 1.25'' pixels, 2.5'' synthetic resolution [3.2 PPMAP analysis: "The pixel sizes of the Nyquist-sampled PPMAP arrays are 1.25$^{\prime \prime}$, corresponding to an angular resolution of 2.5$^{\prime \prime}$."]
  - parameter: Reference opacity kappa_300 = 0.1 cm2/g [Table 2 (PPMAP input parameters): "ref. opacity (cm$^2$ g$^{-1}$) | 0.1 (b)"]
  - parameter: Maximum 10 000 iterations [Table 2 (PPMAP input parameters): "maximum nb. of iterations | 10 000"]
  - parameter: Dilution eta = 0 (internal default N_prior/N_states) [6.2.3 PPMAP parameters: "causes an internal PPMAP subroutine to adopt a default value, defined as"]
  - parameter: Cells of 60 px, overlap 40 px (50 px in some cases giving 7x7 mosaics) [Table 2 (PPMAP input parameters): "cell size (px) | 60 (a)"] [Table 2 (PPMAP input parameters): "cell overlap (px) | 40, 50 (a)"]
  - parameter: High-pass option on, subtracting a constant background from model images at ground-based wavelengths [6.2.3 PPMAP parameters: "allowing PPMAP to subtract a constant background from the internal model images"]
  - parameter: Temperature range lowered per region after a first run (example G012.80: 21-39 K) [6.2.4 Temperature and opacity index opt…: "we systematically lowered the amplitude of the temperature range"]
  - assumption: Dust emission optically thin at all wavelengths [3.1 Point process mapping: "PPMAP operates under the assumption that the radiation emitted by dust across observed wavelengths is optically thin."]
  - assumption: Gas-to-dust mass ratio of 100 (kappa_300 defined w.r.t. total mass) [3.1 Point process mapping: "is in line with a gas-to-dust mass ratio of 100"]
  - assumption: X_H = 0.7, X_H2 = 1, Z_D = 0.01 for the H2 column density [3.1 Point process mapping: "fractional abundance by mass of hydrogen and molecular hydrogen $X_\mathrm{H}=0.7$, $X_\mathrm{H_2}=1$, and fractional abundance by mass of dust $Z_\mathrm{D}=0.01$"]
  - assumption: Measurement noise is Gaussian [3.1 Point process mapping: "The term $\mu$ represents the measurement noise, assumed to be a Gaussian random process."]
  - fitting: Iterative Bayesian algorithm with a Gaussian-process prior, minimising the reduced chi-square per pixel; posterior expectation gives column density and temperature [3.1 Point process mapping: "This is achieved by minimizing the mean square error, ensuring that the best estimate is the a posteriori expectation value of"] [3.1 Point process mapping: "In each pixel of the maps, PPMAP minimizes the reduced-"]
  - validation: A posteriori chi-square test and SED comparison; iteration number tweaked to avoid over-fitting; 86% of observations within 1 sigma of the model [6.2.5 Criteria to stop PPMAP iterations: "The quality of PPMAP outputs underwent a systematic validation process through an a posteriori chi-square test"] [6.2.5 Criteria to stop PPMAP iterations: "We systematically controlled for and prevented over-fitting through minor tweaks of the PPMAP iteration number"]
- result: Range of average column density = 2.5e22 to 2.5e23 ± not stated cm^-2 [4.1 Results: "The average column density measured in the 2.5$^{\prime \prime}$ maps ranges from $2.5 \times 10^{22}$ to $2.5 \times 10^{23}$ cm$^{-2}$"]
- result: Fraction of observations within 1 sigma of the PPMAP MBB model = 86 ± not stated percent [3.3 Bolometric luminosity measurements: "All of the observations align within $\pm 2$ standard deviation ($\sigma$) of the PPMAP MBB model, and 86% of the observations maintain agreement within $\pm 1 \sigma$."]
- result: Reference opacity at 1.3 mm for kappa_300 = 0.1 and beta = 1.8 = 0.007 ± not stated cm2 g^-1 [3.1 Point process mapping: "yields $\kappa_{1.3\mathrm{mm}} = 0.007~\mathrm{cm^2 g^{-1}}$"]
- result: Temperature change when adopting beta = 1.5 and beta = 1 = +3 and +12 ± not stated K [6.2.4 Temperature and opacity index opt…: "we observed average changes of $\Delta T_\mathrm{dust} = + 3$ K and $\Delta T_\mathrm{dust} = + 12$ K when adopting $\beta = 1.5$ and $\beta = 1$"]
- result: Best-fit beta range when beta was left free = 1.4 to 2.1 depending on field ± not stated dimensionless [6.2.4 Temperature and opacity index opt…: "(between $\beta=1.4$ and $\beta=2.1$, depending on the specific fields of observation)"]
- result: Run2 minus Run1 increase in column density over the ALMA footprint = 12.5 ± not stated percent [6.1.3 Free-free emission in the ALMA 1.…: "While the overall column density within the ALMA footprint registers a mere 12.5% increment between the two instances"]
- result: Saturated Herschel images removed from the inputs = 11 of 124 ± not stated images [6.1.1 Correction of saturated pixels: "In severe cases of saturation (11 out of 124 images in our study)"]
- limitation (authors): 2.5'' beam dilutes core emission, so core temperatures can be under- or over-estimated [3.4 Temperature correction and final pr…: "This dilution of the signal may result in underestimating the temperature of protostellar cores and overestimating that of prestellar cores"]
- limitation (authors): Optically thin assumption may bias temperature and mass at high column density [4.3.2 Quantification of errors: "the bias induced by the PPMAP assumption of optical thinness, that may significantly affect the temperature and mass estimates, in particular toward high column density pixels"]
- limitation (authors): Reference opacity is poorly known and may vary across the protoclusters [3.1 Point process mapping: "The reference opacity is in fact not well known, and may vary across the protoclusters."]
- not stated (card): The free-free-subtracted Band 6 maps (Run1 input) come from an in-preparation paper; their method details and availability are not given here beyond Te = 7000 K and region-dependent helium abundance; Per-region PPMAP temperature ranges after the refinement (only G012.80, 21-39 K, is given) and which regions used noverlap = 50 versus 40; Number of PPMAP iterations used per region (only the 10 000 …

### 2019ApJ...878..103R: Modeling the Protoplanetary Disks of Two Brown Dwarfs in the Taurus Molecular Cloud (2019; protoplanetary_disks, star_formation; archival_reuse)
- question: What are the disk radii and masses of two Taurus brown dwarfs (CFHT Tau 4, 2M0444), and what do they imply for brown dwarf formation and planet formation?
- step 1: Fit SEDs with DIAD models
  - method: Grid of DIAD models, best fit by minimum chi2, visual inspection and iterative grid refinement [3.1.1 SED Fitting: "We ran grids of models and determined the best-fit parameters by selecting models with minimum $\chi^2$, visually inspecting the model fits, and iteratively refining the grids."]
  - software: DIAD
  - parameter: Ranges: amax_atm 0.25–1.0 um, amax_mid 500 um–1 cm, epsilon 0.001–0.1, Twall,out 75–1400 K, alpha 0.0001–0.01, Rout 30–100 au, i 25–80 deg [3.1.1 SED Fitting: "0.25–1.0 $\mu$m for a$_{max, atm}$, 500 $\mu$m–1 cm for a$_{max, mid}$, 0.001–0.1 for $\epsilon$"]
  - assumption: Dust-to-gas 0.01; Twall,in 1400 K; Mdot fixed (1e-10 CFHT Tau 4, 2e-10 2M0444); grain size power law -3.5, min size 0.005 um [3.1.1 SED Fitting: "We assumed a fixed dust-to-gas mass ratio of 0.01."]
  - fitting: Grid search, chi2 minimum; no MCMC because too costly [3.1.1 SED Fitting: "This expense renders common statistical methods of estimating best-fit model parameters and their uncertainties"]
  - validation: Uncertainties are only qualitative ranges [3.1.1 SED Fitting: "should not be considered an accurate quantitative estimate of the uncertainties, but rather a qualitative approximation"]
- step 2: Fit ALMA continuum geometry and radius
  - method: Make model images from best SED models, Fourier transform and sample at observed uv coverage in CASA; adjust inclination, PA, radius [3.1.2 ALMA Data: "Three parameters were adjusted to improve the fit of the model image to the data: inclination, position angle, and radius."]
  - software: CASA
  - fitting: Manual adjustment; contour comparison [3.1.2 ALMA Data: "Contours of the models and the ALMA observations were compared to assess the model fit."]
  - validation: Radial profiles within 1 sigma and visibility comparison [3.1.2 ALMA Data: "We varied disk radius, position angle, and inclination for each object until the model profile was within 1$\sigma$ of the observed profile and the residuals were minimized."]
- result: CFHT Tau 4 outer radius = 80 ± 10 au [Table 3: "80$\pm$10"]
- result: 2M0444 outer radius = 100 ± 10 au [Table 3: "100$\pm$10"]
- result: CFHT Tau 4 inclination / PA = 70 / 40 ± 10 / 10 deg [3.2 Model Results: "a position angle of 40$^{\circ}$, and an inclination of 70$^{\circ}$"]
- result: 2M0444 inclination / PA = 40 / 70 ± 10 / 10 deg [3.2 Model Results: "a position angle of 70$^{\circ}$, and an inclination of 40$^{\circ}$"]
- limitation (authors): Uncertainties are qualitative; selection effects make conclusions difficult; brightest disks may be atypical [4.1 Disk Radius and BD Formation: "The selection effects resulting from the evolution of the disk, and the timescale on which this evolution occurs, make firm conclusions difficult."]
- not stated (card): DIAD code availability and exact grid steps; Photometry flux uncertainties used in chi2; Clean parameters (cell, image size, mask, threshold); How model images were generated from SED models (scaling, wavelength); Criteria for model-vs-data comparison in visibilities (no quantitative fit statistic); Gap inner edge assumptions for 2M0444 (0.02 au)

### 2025ApJ...995...98D: DiskMINT: Self-consistent Thermochemical Disk Models with Radially Varying Gas and Dust—Application to the Massive, CO-Rich Disk of IM Lup (2025; protoplanetary_disks; archival_reuse)
- question: Infer the mass and surface density distribution of the IM Lup disk with self-consistent thermochemical models.
- step 1: Set stellar illumination and dust opacities
  - method: Use a three-component stellar spectrum and compute size-dependent wavelength-dependent dust opacities. [Models and Main Results: "For all of the models, we adopt an input stellar spectrum with three components:"] [Dust properties: "We use the `optool`[^3] (cite: dominik_optool_2021) package to compute the wavelength-dependent dust opacity using the Bruggeman (cite: bruggeman_berechnung_1935) mixing rule,"]
  - software: optool
  - parameter: BT-settl 4300 K; blackbodies 8500 K and 1500 K, the latter at 0.055 AU [Models and Main Results: "a `BT-settl` stellar photosphere spectrum with $T_{\rm eff} = 4300\,{\rm K}$ (cite: Allard_2003IAUS..211..325A; Allard_2011ASPC..448...91A);"] [Models and Main Results: "a $8500\,{\rm K}$ blackbody radiation fitting the UV data-point in the SED; and a $1500\,{\rm K}$ blackbody radiation at $0.055\,{\rm AU}$ representing the accreting gas in the inner disk."]
  - parameter: a_min = 10−5 cm; fit a_max and q; 20 size bins [Dust properties: "We fix $a_{\rm min} = 10^{-5}\,\mathrm{cm}$ and fit the SED to infer the $a_{\rm max}$ and $q$."] [Dust properties: "We divide the dust grains into 20 different size bins"]
  - assumption: Power-law grain-size distribution n(a) ∝ a−q; DIANA composition: 60% amorphous silicates, 15% amorphous carbon, 25% porosity by volume [Dust properties: "The dust number density follows a power-law distribution"] [Dust properties: "a mixture 60% of amorphous silicates ((cite: dorschner_steps_1995), ${\rm Mg_{0.7}Fe_{0.3}SiO_{3}}$) with 15% amorphous carbon ((cite: zubko_optical_1996), BE-sample), with 25% porosity, by volume."]
  - fitting: Fit SED for a_max and q; fit UV point with 8500 K blackbody. [Dust properties: "We fix $a_{\rm min} = 10^{-5}\,\mathrm{cm}$ and fit the SED to infer the $a_{\rm max}$ and $q$."] [Models and Main Results: "a $8500\,{\rm K}$ blackbody radiation fitting the UV data-point in the SED;"]
  - validation: 20 size bins stated to constrain thermal structure to 5%, referring to earlier work. [Dust properties: "We divide the dust grains into 20 different size bins – which is sufficient to constrain the resulting thermal structure to an accuracy of $5\%$"]
- step 2: Fit the well-mixed dust disk
  - method: Solve vertical hydrostatic equilibrium and fit the SED at wavelengths ≳100 μm for dust mass and thermal structure. [dust-to-gas mass ratio: "Following (cite: Deng_2023_diskmint), we run `DiskMINT` by solving for disk vertical hydrostatic equilibrium."] [Models and Main Results: "We fit the long wavelengths ($\gtrsim 100\,{\rm\mu m}$) SED to derive the dust disk mass ($M_{\rm dust}$) and thermal structure."]
  - software: DiskMINT, RADMC-3D
  - parameter: r_c = 100 AU; gamma = 1.0; r_in = 0.055 AU; r_out ~1000 AU [A self-similar surface density distribu…: "We adopt typical values for the characteristic radius of $r_{c} = 100\,\mathrm{AU}$ and $\gamma = 1.0$"] [A self-similar surface density distribu…: "We set the inner disk radius $r_{\rm in} = 0.055\,\mathrm{AU}$"]
  - parameter: Inclination 47.5° [dust-to-gas mass ratio: "The SED is generated using `RADMC3D` with the disk inclination ($i = 47.5\ensuremath{^\circ}$) from ALMA continuum images by DSHARP (cite: huang_disk_2018)."]
  - assumption: Fixed gas-to-dust spatial coupling and constant dust-to-gas mass ratio [Well-mixed model: Perfectly coupled gas…: "We first build a well-mixed model where the dust and gas have a fixed mass ratio all through the disk (at all $(r,z)$)"]
  - assumption: Long-wavelength emission likely optically thin [dust-to-gas mass ratio: "where the disk emission is likely optically thin and a better indicator of dust mass."]
  - fitting: SED fitting, mainly using ALMA mm-wavelength continuum flux densities for dust mass. [dust-to-gas mass ratio: "We estimate $M_{\rm dust}$ from the SED, mainly referring to the $\mathrm{mm}$-wavelength continuum flux densities from ALMA,"]
  - validation: Integrated emission fits; radial continuum and C18O distributions do not reproduce observations well. [Discussions and implications: "the first well-mixed model reproduces the SED and the integrated $\rm C^{18}O$ luminosity,"] [Spatially Structured Model: decoupled g…: "the radial distributions of neither the dust nor $\rm C^{18}O$ gas line emission of the well-mixed model well reproduce the observations"]
- step 4: Solve settled vertical structure
  - method: Iterate dust radiative transfer, dust-gas thermal equilibrium, gas hydrostatic equilibrium, and steady-state dust diffusion. [Vertical distribution: dust settling: "we go through iterations including solving radiative transfer with `RADMC3D` to estimate $T_{\rm dust}(a)$, thermal equilibrium to estimate $T_{\rm gas}$, vertical hydrostatic equilibrium to estimate $\rho_{\rm gas}$, and the dust diffusive equation to estimate $\rho_{\rm dust}(a)$."]
  - software: DiskMINT, RADMC-3D
  - parameter: Best-fit alpha_v = 10−3 from SED; test alpha_v = 10−4, 10−3, 10−2 [Results for Structured Model: "we also find the best-fit value for the turbulent parameter $\alpha_{\rm v} = 10^{-3}$ based on the SED fitting"] [Effect of dust settling: "apply dust settling with three different $\alpha_{\rm v} = 10^{-4}, 10^{-3}, 10^{-2}$,"]
  - assumption: Schmidt number ~1; vertically integrated grain-size distributions remain fixed at each radius [Vertical distribution: dust settling: "${\rm Sc}$ is the Schmidt number assumed to be $\sim 1$ (cite: fromang_n_Nelson_settling_2009)."] [Vertical distribution: dust settling: "the overall dust size distribution at each radius and the vertically integrated (surface) densities for each size, $\Sigma_{\rm dust}(a, r)$ – are kept fixed."]
  - assumption: Gas-phase heating and cooling omitted; gas temperature from thermal equilibrium with dust grains [Omission of gas cooling: "Specifically, gas-phase heating and cooling mechanisms are omitted and $T_{\rm gas}$ is computed from the thermal equilibrium with dust grains of different sizes"]
  - fitting: Constrain turbulence from the infrared SED near 100 μm. [alpha_v: "It is constrained from the infrared SED at $\sim 100\,{\rm\mu m}$, and we use the best-fit $\alpha_{\rm v} \sim 10^{-3}$ from the structured model."]
  - validation: Settling models improve the ~100 μm SED; mass estimates are insensitive to tested alpha_v choices. [Effect of dust settling: "We find that the models with dust settling (green, red, and purple lines) match the SED better at $\lambda \sim 100\,{\rm\mu m}$,"] [Effect of dust settling: "We also find that neither the $M_{\rm dust}$ nor $M_{\rm gas}$ estimates are sensitive to the choice of $\alpha_{\rm v}$,"]
- result: Well-mixed dust mass = 2.5×10−4 ± not stated M_sun [dust-to-gas mass ratio: "we find $M_{\rm dust}= 2.5\times10^{-4}\,M_\odot = 83\,M_\oplus$."]
- result: Well-mixed dust mass = 83 ± not stated M_earth [dust-to-gas mass ratio: "we find $M_{\rm dust}= 2.5\times10^{-4}\,M_\odot = 83\,M_\oplus$."]
- result: Structured turbulence parameter = 10−3 ± not stated dimensionless [Results for Structured Model: "we also find the best-fit value for the turbulent parameter $\alpha_{\rm v} = 10^{-3}$ based on the SED fitting"]
- result: Gas-mass range in settling sensitivity tests = 0.025 - 0.056 ± not stated M_sun [Effect of dust settling: "We find the best-fit $\varepsilon = 0.007-0.016$ with $M_{\rm gas}= 0.025 - 0.056\,M_\odot$,"]
- limitation (authors): Gas-phase heating and cooling omitted; gas masses may be underestimated by up to ~2. [Omission of gas cooling: "Specifically, gas-phase heating and cooling mechanisms are omitted"]
- limitation (authors): Inside ≲40 AU, optical thickness makes surface densities lower limits. [The optically thick inner disk and fain…: "the dust and gas become optically thick inside $\lesssim 40\,{\rm AU}$, hence what we derive within is a lower limit to the surface density."]
- not stated (card): Exact observed continuum images, C18O cubes, product filenames, and versioned download locations.; Photometric measurements, uncertainties, source citations for individual SED points, and multi-epoch averaging procedure.; Observed-data calibration, self-calibration, continuum subtraction, spectral channelization, weighting, masks, and noise estimates.; Annular widths, radial sampling, image cente…

### 2024ApJ...964...95S: Physical Properties of Hyperluminous, Dust-obscured Quasars at z ∼ 3: Multiwavelength Spectral Energy Distribution Analysis and Cold Gas Content Revealed by AL… (2024; agn_and_quasars, high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Physical properties and cold gas content of 16 Hot DOGs at z ∼ 3; their role in massive galaxy and SMBH coevolution.
- step 6: Fit stellar, dust, and AGN SED components
  - method: BayeSED V3.0 three-component fitting; PCA compresses libraries, ANN or K-nearest-neighbor interpolation, MultiNest posterior sampling. [SED Analysis: "The SED of each galaxy in our sample was decomposed into three components: stellar, cold dust, and central AGN."] [SED Analysis: "For SED models given as a SED library, principal component analysis is employed to reduce memory usage."]
  - software: BayeSED, MultiNest, CLUMPY
  - parameter: Graybody λ0 = 125 μm; Tdust and β free; final Tdust range 30–50 K. [SED Analysis: "where $\lambda_0$=125$\mu$m, and the dust temperature $T_{\rm dust}$ and the emissivity index $\beta$ are two free parameters in the SED fitting."] [Influence of AGN contamination to FIR: "We constrained the $T_{\rm dust}$ parameter of the graybody component to be within the range 30-50 K and refitted the SEDs in our sample."]
  - parameter: 12 free parameters deferred to Table 3 of Fan2019; omit stellar component for three objects without optical-NIR photometry. [SED Analysis: "The 12 free parameters used in the fitting process are listed in Table 3 of (cite: Fan2019)."] [SED Analysis: "For the three Hot DOGs without optical-NIR photometry, we excluded the stellar population component to prevent overfitting."]
  - parameter: Upper limits encoded using negative flux-density errors. [SED Analysis: "The upper flux density limit was taken into account by setting the corresponding flux density error to a negative value according to the convention in BayeSED."]
  - assumption: BC03 stellar populations, Chabrier IMF, exponentially declining SFH. [SED Analysis: "The stellar emission was modeled by using the (cite: bc03) simple stellar population library with a (cite: chabrier2003) initial mass function (IMF), and an exponential declining star formation history (SFH)."]
  - assumption: Calzetti attenuation and energy balance: absorbed stellar energy entirely reemitted in IR. [SED Analysis: "The (cite: Calzetti2000a) dust attenuation law was used, and the energy of stellar emission absorbed by cold dust was assumed to be totally reemitted in the IR band."]
  - assumption: CLUMPY torus for central AGN UV-IR emission. [SED Analysis: "The CLUMPY torus model (cite: Nenkova2008a; Nenkova2008b)"] [SED Analysis: "has been utilized to model the UV-IR emission of the central AGN of our samples."]
  - fitting: MultiNest; posterior medians and 68% intervals; final Tdust prior range 30–50 K; other priors and likelihood not stated. [SED Analysis: "Finally, the MultiNest algorithm is employed to sample the parameter space and calculate the posterior probability distribution of the parameters."] [Results of the SED Fitting and Dust Pro…: "We adopted the median of the posterior probability distribution of each parameter as the fiducial value, and the uncertainties are reported as the 68% confidence intervals around the fiducial values."]
  - validation: Compare 30–50 K and 10–100 K fits; W0533-3401 reduced χ² improvement 0.10; reported Bayes-factor range 0.1–3.6. [Influence of AGN contamination to FIR: "Initially, we fitted our sample with BayeSED by setting the $T_{\rm dust}$ parameter range to default values (10-100K)."] [Influence of AGN contamination to FIR: "The reduced $\chi^2$ only improve by a value of 0.10 after we apply our prior knowledge of the $T_{\rm dust}$ for W0533-3401."]
- step 7: Derive luminosities and star formation rates
  - method: Integrate cold dust over 8–1000 μm and integrate the AGN component; average SFH over the last 100 Myr; calibrate SFR–LIR for objects without optical-NIR data. [Results of the SED Fitting and Dust Pro…: "The cold dust infrared luminosity $L_{\rm IR}$ was calculated by integrating the cold dust component from 8 to 1000 $\mu$m."] [Stellar mass and molecular gas fraction: "their AGN bolometric luminosity（$L_{\rm AGN}$）derived by integrating the AGN component of the best-fit UV to millimeter SED."]
  - software: BayeSED
  - parameter: SFR [M⊙/yr] = 8×10−11 LIR [L⊙]. [Star formation rate and star formation …: "our sample generally follows the relation of SFR $[\rm M_{\odot}/\rm yr]$ = $8\times10^{-11}\,L_{\rm IR}\,[\rm L_{\odot}]$"]
  - parameter: Use the remaining 13 Hot DOGs to calibrate SFR–LIR for the three optical-NIR nondetections. [Star formation rate and star formation …: "For the three sources without an optical-NIR detection, we estimate their SFRs based on the relation between SFR and $L_{\rm IR}$ calibrated for the remaining 13 Hot DOGs."]
  - assumption: Use Tdust-constrained fitting for all physical-property analysis. [Influence of AGN contamination to FIR: "The analysis of the physical properties in this paper is entirely based on the $T_{\rm dust}$-constrained fitting."]
  - validation: SFR–LIR relation approximately 0.1 dex below the Chabrier-calibrated Kennicutt relation. [Star formation rate and star formation …: "which is about 0.1 dex lower than the (cite: Kennicutt1998) relation calibrated by the (cite: chabrier2003) IMF."]
- step 9: Calculate gas fractions and gas-to-dust ratios
  - method: Combine CO-based molecular masses with SED stellar and dust masses; fgas = MH2/(MH2+Mstar). [Stellar mass and molecular gas fraction: "we calculated the molecular gas fraction, which is defined as $f_{\rm gas}$ = $M_{\rm H_2}/(M_{\rm H_2}\,+\,M_\star)$"] [Gas-to-dust ratio: "Most of our Hot DOGs exhibit a high $\delta_{\rm GDR}$ value $\sim$ $474_{-324}^{+711}$, with a median uncertainty of 43% (uncertainties propagated from $M_{\rm H_2}$ and $M_{\rm dust}$)."]
  - assumption: For three objects without stellar masses, adopt Mstar = 10^10.3 M⊙ to obtain upper limits on fgas. [Stellar mass and molecular gas fraction: "we adopted $M_\star$ = $10^{10.3}\,\rm M_\odot$ which is the minimum $M_\star$ estimated for the other galaxies in our sample."] [Stellar mass and molecular gas fraction: "To put an upper limit on the molecular gas fraction for these three Hot DOGs"]
  - validation: Propagate molecular-mass and dust-mass uncertainties; fgas median uncertainty approximately 60%. [Gas-to-dust ratio: "with a median uncertainty of 43% (uncertainties propagated from $M_{\rm H_2}$ and $M_{\rm dust}$)."] [Stellar mass and molecular gas fraction: "the median uncertainty of $f_{\rm gas}$ of our Hot DOG sample is approximately 60%, which results from the uncertainties in $M_{\rm H_2}$ and $M_{\star}$"]
- step 10: Calculate starburst offsets, depletion times, and SFE
  - method: Use Speagle2014 for ΔMS = SFR/SFRMS; tdepl = MH2/SFR; observational SFE = LIR/L′CO(1-0). [Star formation rate and star formation …: "we can estimate the offset of our Hot DOGs from the main sequence $\Delta_{\rm MS}$ = $\rm {SFR}\,/\,\rm {SFR}_{\rm MS}$ relation."] [Star formation rate and star formation …: "With the molecular gas masses and SFRs, we derived the gas depletion timescale $t_{\rm depl}\,=\,M_{\rm H_2}\,/\,\rm {SFR}$."]
  - parameter: Speagle2014: log(SFR) = (0.84−0.026τuniv)log(Mstar)−(6.51−0.11τuniv), τuniv in Gyr. [Star formation rate and star formation …: "\log({\rm SFR}/{\rm M}_{\ensuremath{\odot}}~{\rm yr}^{-1}) &=(0.84-0.026\times \tau_{\rm univ})\log(M_{\star}/{\rm M}_{\ensuremath{\odot}})"] [Star formation rate and star formation …: "& \qquad -(6.51-0.11\times \tau_{\rm univ})\, ,"]
  - validation: Compare with literature SMGs/quasars and Sargent2014 main-sequence relations. [Star formation rate and star formation …: "the compiled samples of SMGs and unobscured and obscured quasars from (cite: Perna2018)."] [Star formation rate and star formation …: "The SFEs exhibit a tight correlation with $L_{\rm IR}$ for both MS galaxies (cite: Sargent2014) and SMGs."]
- result: Median cold dust temperature = 45 ± +2/-1 K [Summary and Conclusion: "$T_{\rm dust}\,\sim\,45_{-1}^{+2}\,\rm K$"]
- result: Median dust emissivity index = 2.5 ± +0.2/-0.2 dimensionless [Summary and Conclusion: "$\beta\,\sim\,2.5_{-0.2}^{+0.2}$"]
- result: Median cold dust infrared luminosity = 1.1 × 10^13 ± +1.3/-0.3 × 10^13 L⊙ [Summary and Conclusion: "$L_{\rm IR}\,\sim\,1.1_{-0.3}^{+1.3}\,\times\,10^{13}\,L_\odot$"]
- result: Typical stellar mass = 5.8 × 10^10 ± +12.3/-2.3 × 10^10 M⊙ [Summary and Conclusion: "$M_\star\,\sim5.8_{-2.3}^{+12.3}\,\times\,10^{10}\,M_\odot$"]
- result: Typical SFR = 1008 ± +597/-409 M⊙ yr−1 [Summary and Conclusion: "SFR $\sim\,1008_{-409}^{+597}\,\rm M_\odot\,\rm yr^{-1}$."]
- result: Typical AGN bolometric luminosity = 9.7 × 10^13 ± +5.8/-4.3 × 10^13 L⊙ [Summary and Conclusion: "$L_{\rm AGN}\,\sim\,9.7_{-4.3}^{+5.8}\,\times\,10^{13}\,\rm L_\odot$."]
- result: Median gas-to-dust ratio = 474 ± +711/-324 dimensionless [Summary and Conclusion: "We estimated the gas-to-dust ratio, finding $\delta_{\rm GDR}\,\sim\,474_{-324}^{+711}$."]
- result: Median molecular gas fraction = 0.33 ± +0.33/-0.17 dimensionless [Summary and Conclusion: "We inferred the molecular gas fraction, finding $f_{\rm gas}\,\sim\,0.33_{-0.17}^{+0.33}$."]
- result: Median main-sequence SFR offset = 6.12 ± +5.1/-2.9 dimensionless [Summary and Conclusion: "$\Delta_{\rm MS}\,\sim\,6.12^{+5.1}_{-2.9}$"]
- result: Median gas depletion timescale = 39 ± +85/-28 Myr [Summary and Conclusion: "The gas depletion timescales, 39$_{-28}^{+85}$ Myr are remarkably short."]
- result: Median SFE = 297 ± +659/-195 K km s−1 pc−2 [Summary and Conclusion: "the average SFE of $\sim\,297_{-195}^{+659}\,\rm K\,\rm km\,\rm s^{-1}$ $\rm pc^{-2}$"]
- result: Reported Bayes-factor range for SED-fit comparison = 0.1-3.6 ± not stated dimensionless [Influence of AGN contamination to FIR: "We calculate the Bayes factor, defined as the difference in the Bayesian evidence between the two fitting methods, to be in the range of 0.1-3.6"]
- result: Reduction of LIR relative to temperature-unconstrained fitting = 0.16-0.47 ± not stated dex [Influence of AGN contamination to FIR: "The $L_{\rm IR}$ is smaller by 0.16-0.47 dex compared to the $L_{\rm IR,0}$"]
- limitation (authors): Sample selection may favor intense starbursts. [Gas-to-dust ratio: "therefore may be biased toward the most intense starbursting systems"]
- limitation (authors): Warm graybody dust may miss the diffuse cold ISM and underestimate total dust mass. [Gas-to-dust ratio: "The estimated $T_{\rm dust}\sim$ 45 K may trace a warmer dust component associated with photodissociation regions by starbursts, instead of the diffuse ISM with temperature below 30 K which represents the bulk of the dust mass"]
- not stated (card): CASA and observatory pipeline versions.; Exact spectral-window frequencies, channel widths, bandwidths, and final cube channelization for each source.; Continuum-free channels and uvcontsub fitting order.; Imaging task, deconvolver, cell size, image dimensions, cleaning masks, and stopping thresholds.; Primary-beam correction and treatment of observations from multiple dates.; Moment-0 integratio…

### 2021A&A...645A..49G: A proto-pseudobulge in ESO 320-G030 fed by a massive molecular inflow driven by a nuclear bar (2021; nearby_galaxies, star_formation, astrochemistry; archival_reuse)
- question: Infer the nuclear physical conditions and structure of ESO 320-G030, and test whether bar-driven molecular inflow feeds its nuclear starburst.
- step 3: Build excitation-model grids
  - method: Compute nonlocal, non-LTE H2O excitation, line fluxes and profiles, and dust SEDs in spherical gas-and-dust components. [A library of model components: "the model components consist of spherically symmetric distributions of gas and dust, for which the statistical equilibrium populations of the H$_2$O rotational levels are calculated through nonlocal, non-LTE radiative transfer calculations."] [A library of model components: "The fluxes and profiles of all involved lines and the spectral energy distribution (SED) of the dust continuum from the mid-IR to the mm are subsequently computed."]
  - parameter: Vary T_dust, tau_100, N_H2O, and n_H2; fix Delta V = 100 km s^-1 and T_gas = 150 K [A library of model components: "The physical parameters that are modified from model to model are $T_{\mathrm{dust}}$, $\tau_{100}$, $N_{\mathrm{H2O}}$, and $n_{\mathrm{H2}}$, and we keep fixed $\Delta V=100$ km s$^{-1}$ and $T_{\mathrm{gas}}=150$ K."]
  - parameter: Core: tau_100 >= 4 and T_dust >= 75 K [Groups of model components: "The “core” models are all optically thick in the far-IR ($\tau_{100}\ge4$) and very warm ($T_{\mathrm{dust}}\ge75$ K)."]
  - parameter: Disk: tau_100 >= 0.7 and T_dust = 45–75 K; envelope: tau_100 <= approximately 2 and T_dust = 40–60 K [Groups of model components: "The “disk” models have lower $\tau_{100}$ but are still (nearly) optically thick ($\tau_{100}\ge0.7$) with $T_{\mathrm{dust}}=45-75$ K."] [Groups of model components: "The “envelope” models mainly cover optically thin conditions but can reach optically thick values ($\tau_{100}\lesssim2$), and have moderate $T_{\mathrm{dust}}=40-60$ K."]
  - parameter: Three n_H2 grid values spanning (1.7–15) × 10^4 cm^-3; N_H2O multiplicative spacing 1.5–2 [Groups of model components: "While $N_{\mathrm{H2O}}$ is varied by more than 1 dex within each group with multiplicative factors of $1.5-2$"] [Groups of model components: "the grid for $n_{\mathrm{H2}}$ is coarser with only 3 values, representing typical densities of buried galactic nuclei ($(1.7-15)\times10^4$ cm$^{-3}$"]
  - assumption: Uniform physical properties within each component; mixed gas and dust [A library of model components: "We assume for each component uniform physical properties"] [A library of model components: "The gas and dust are assumed to be mixed."]
  - validation: Vary density to test collisional excitation; absence of a high-density trend supports insensitivity to the fixed gas temperature. [A library of model components: "That we do not find such a trend below (Section [3.2.2](#excit)) indicates that our results are insensitive to our choice of $T_{\mathrm{gas}}$."]
- step 5: Derive luminosities, star formation, and gas masses
  - method: Compare model SED with observations, add an extra-nuclear dust component, convert IR luminosity to SFR, and derive gas masses from size and dust optical depth; compare with CO. [The fit to the SED and the nuclear SFR: "an additional extra-nuclear component has been included"] [The fit to the SED and the nuclear SFR: "Using the (cite: ken12) star formation rate (SFR) calibration of the total IR luminosity"]
  - parameter: Extra-nuclear component: T_dust = 28 K, L_IR approximately 4 × 10^10 L_sun [The fit to the SED and the nuclear SFR: "with $T_{\mathrm{dust}}=28$ K and $L_{\mathrm{IR}}\approx4\times10^{10}$ L$_{\odot}$."]
  - parameter: N_H/tau_100 = 1.3 × 10^24 cm^-2; mu = 1.4 for helium [Gas masses: "$N_{\mathrm{H}}/\tau_{100}=1.3\times10^{24}$ cm$^{-2}$ is the gas column per unit optical depth at 100 $\mu$m (cite: gon14), and $\mu=1.4$ accounts for He."]
  - assumption: CO 2-1 and 1-0 have equal brightness; alpha_CO = 0.78 M_sun/(K km s^-1 pc^2) [Gas masses: "assuming the same brightness for the $2-1$ and $1-0$ lines with a ULIRG conversion factor of $\alpha_{\mathrm{CO}}=0.78$ $M_{\odot}/(\mathrm{K\,km\,s^{-1}\,pc^2})$"]
  - assumption: Evaluate both star-formation-powered and AGN-powered core scenarios for nuclear SFR [The fit to the SED and the nuclear SFR: "These values also assume that the very optically thick and compact core component is powered by star formation"] [The fit to the SED and the nuclear SFR: "if we assume that its luminosity is driven by an extremely buried AGN, the nuclear SFR is derived from the IR luminosities of only the disk and envelope"]
  - validation: Compare H2O-based mass with CO mass inside the 454 GHz 3-sigma contour; compare nuclear SED with the wider-aperture galaxy SED. [Gas masses: "Using the CO emission within the 3$\sigma$ contour of the 454 GHz emission"] [Gas masses: "This value is comparable to the mass derived from the H$_2$O model."]
- result: Combined nuclear IR luminosity = 1.23 × 10^11 ± ±0.17 × 10^11 L_sun [The fit to the SED and the nuclear SFR: "The three nuclear components combined, however, account for a luminosity of $L_{\mathrm{IR}}=(1.23\pm0.17)\times10^{11}$ L$_{\odot}$"]
- result: Nuclear fraction of total galaxy luminosity = approximately 70% ± not stated dimensionless [The fit to the SED and the nuclear SFR: "that is, $\sim70$% of the total galaxy luminosity."]
- result: Total SFR = approximately 25 ± not stated M_sun yr^-1 [The fit to the SED and the nuclear SFR: "the total and nuclear SFR are $\approx25$ and $\approx18$ M$_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$, respectively."]
- result: Nuclear SFR with star-formation-powered core = approximately 18 ± not stated M_sun yr^-1 [The fit to the SED and the nuclear SFR: "the total and nuclear SFR are $\approx25$ and $\approx18$ M$_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$, respectively."]
- result: Nuclear SFR excluding AGN-powered core = approximately 16 ± not stated M_sun yr^-1 [The fit to the SED and the nuclear SFR: "the nuclear SFR is derived from the IR luminosities of only the disk and envelope to give $\approx16$ M$_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$."]
- result: Combined gas mass of three nuclear components = 4.5 × 10^8 ± -0.6/+1.5 × 10^8 M_sun [Gas masses: "The combined gas mass of the 3 nuclear components is $4.5_{-0.6}^{+1.5}\times10^8$ M$_{\odot}$."]
- result: CO-derived gas mass inside the 454 GHz 3-sigma contour = 3.4 × 10^8 ± not stated M_sun [Gas masses: "Using the CO emission within the 3$\sigma$ contour of the 454 GHz emission"]
- result: Nuclear gas fraction = approximately 20% ± not stated dimensionless [Gas masses: "the gas fraction is $f_g=M_{\mathrm{gas}}/M_{\mathrm{dyn}}\sim20$%."]
- result: Nuclear starburst age upper limit = approximately 100 ± upper limit Myr [Gas masses: "the nuclear starburst has an age of $\sim100$ Myr."]
- limitation (authors): Some extra-nuclear contribution to the lowest-lying H2O lines is not excluded. [The core, disk, and envelope components: "although some extra-nuclear contribution to the lowest-lying H$_2$O lines is not ruled out."]
- limitation (authors): Core lines are saturated; core optical depth, abundance, mass, and size are uncertain. [H2O excitation, column densities, and a…: "all lines -including the submillimeter H$_2$O448 transition- are saturated in this component."]
- limitation (authors): The three-component geometry is schematic with sharp transitions. [Additional remarks: "it is obviously very schematic with sharp edges and transitions from one component to the next."]
- not stated (card): ALMA CASA version, calibration scripts, flagging choices, calibrators, and any self-calibration history.; Exact spectral-window setup, continuum channel selections, and H2O448 continuum-subtraction procedure.; Imaging task, image dimensions, clean masks, stopping thresholds, deconvolver, and primary-beam treatment.; Coordinates, systemic-velocity definition, and the intended frequency-frame speci…

### 2018Sci...362.1034D: The multiple merger assembly of a hyperluminous obscured quasar at redshift 4.6 (2018; agn_and_quasars, high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Is the hyper-luminous obscured quasar W2246−0526 (z=4.6) undergoing a multiple merger, with dusty bridges and tidal tails connecting it to companion galaxies?
- step 3: Derive dust and gas masses
  - method: Optically thin modified blackbody scaled to continuum flux; gas = 100 × dust; CO-based gas with alpha_CO [Calculation of Dust and Gas Masses: "we use a modified blackbody function scaled to the continuum flux density of each source"]
  - parameter: Tdust 50–100 K for W2246, 25–50 K for companions/extended [Calculation of Dust and Gas Masses: "we adopt a reasonable temperature range of T dust = 50–100 K for the Rayleigh-Jeans tail emission"]
  - assumption: beta=1.8, kappa(850µm)=0.0484 m2/kg, gas-to-dust 100, alpha_CO,MW=4.6 and ULIRG=0.8 [Calculation of Dust and Gas Masses: "We further assume that the dust emission is optically thin, with an emissivity index β = 1.8"]
  - validation: Dust-based gas compared with CO-based gas and dynamical mass [Calculation of Dust and Gas Masses: "which slightly favors an α CO conversion factor closer to the ULIRG regime"]
- step 4: Stellar mass and SFR via SED fitting
  - method: Empirical AGN + galaxy templates (Assef 2010), mass-to-light via ezgal/Galaxev [Materials and Methods: "we follow (10) and derive the mass-to-light ratio of the host using the code ezgal (52) and the Galaxev stellar population SED models (53)"]
  - software: ezgal, Sextractor
  - assumption: Z=0.008, Chabrier IMF, exponentially declining SFHs [Materials and Methods: "with a metallicity Z = 0.008 (≡ 0.4 Z ¤, where Z ¤ is the metallicity of the Sun) and a Chabrier IMF"]
  - fitting: non-negative combination of E, Sbc, Im and AGN templates [Materials and Methods: "An object is modeled as a non-negative combination of an old stellar population (E template), an intermediate stellar population (Sbc template), a young stellar population (Im template) and an AGN."]
  - validation: Tested contamination and host reddening [Materials and Methods: "the change in M ★ would be well within the error of a factor of two quoted below"]
- result: Dust mass of W2246−0526 (central 1″) = 5.6–17 × 10^8 ± not stated Msun [Calculation of Dust and Gas Masses: "This corresponds to a dust mass in the range M dust ~ 5.6–17 × 10 [8 ] M ¤ ( T dust = 100–50 K, respectively)."]
- result: Total gas mass of system from dust = 1.2–3.6 × 10^11 ± not stated Msun [Calculation of Dust and Gas Masses: "the entire system could contain as much as M gas = 1.2–3.6 × 10 [11 ] M ¤ ."]
- result: CO(2-1) luminosity = 3.3 ± 0.7 1e7 Lsun [Calculation of Dust and Gas Masses: "L CO(2→1) = 3.3 (±0.7) × 10 [7 ] L ¤"]
- limitation (authors): U1 may be at a different redshift; stellar mass and SFR uncertain by factor of 2 [Materials and Methods: "both with an uncertainty of a factor of 2"]
- not stated (card): Line-free channels / how continuum cube was built from SPWs and which channels were discarded; Flux calibration details (pipeline vs manual) and flux scale uncertainty; Mask for extended emission/companions during cleaning; Aperture photometry method for companions and tail; Image size and cell size; Self-calibration use

### 2021ApJ...918...76C: Toward a More Complex Understanding of Natal Super Star Clusters with Multiwavelength Observations (2021; star_formation, nearby_galaxies; archival_reuse)
- question: Disentangle individual natal super star clusters in He 2-10 at ~0.3'' resolution using multiwavelength radio, submillimeter, and infrared observations to assess their ionizing photon rates, extinction, masses, and star formation efficienci…
- step 1: Convolve radio/mm/submm maps to a common beam, stack them to define source apertures, and extract multiwavelength continuum and Pa-alpha photometry
  - method: Convolve all VLA and ALMA maps to a 0.3'' circular beam, stack the 8 convolved maps, define source footprints using the 20-sigma (73 uJy/bm) contour of the stacked map with manual masking in crowded regions, sum intensity divided by beam a… [Source Identification and Photometry fo…: "We overlaid contours of the stacked image and selected the contour of 20$\times$ the rms (73 $\mu$Jy bm$^{-1}$) of the stacked map."] [Source Identification and Photometry fo…: "To briefly summarize, we sum the intensity across the pixels associated with the source and divide it by the beam area."]
  - software: CASA, Photutils
  - parameter: Common synthesized beam = 0.300'' [Source Extraction and Photometry: "We convolved the maps to a common synthesized beam of 0.3$''$, which is roughly the synthesized beam at a robust of 0.5, excluding the two historical VLA programs."]
  - parameter: Stacked map contour level = 20 x rms (73 uJy bm^-1) [Source Identification and Photometry fo…: "We overlaid contours of the stacked image and selected the contour of 20$\times$ the rms (73 $\mu$Jy bm$^{-1}$) of the stacked map."]
  - parameter: Pa-alpha background annulus = 1.5 to 2 times source size (2 to 3 times for Knot 4b) [Photometry in Pa\alpha Map: "Regardless of the method used, we place an annulus around the source with an inner and outer radii of 1.5 and 2 times the size of the source for the background estimate. The exception is Knot 4b, for which we used an annulus with an inner and outer radii of 2 and 3 times the size of the source"]
  - assumption: Rayleigh-Jeans tail of hot star SEDs makes continuum in F187N 3% brighter than in F190N [HST NICMOS Archival Observations: "If the SEDs of the SSC are Rayleigh-jeans tails of hot star SEDs, the continuum in F187N will be 3$\%$ brighter than in F190N."]
  - assumption: Systematic flux calibration errors: 10% for ALMA, 5% for VLA < 15 GHz, 15% for VLA >= 15 GHz [Source Identification and Photometry fo…: "For the ALMA bands, the systematic errors are 10$\%$ (cite: Fomalont:2014), and for the VLA, 5$\%$ for frequencies $<$ 15 GHz and otherwise 15$\%$."]
  - fitting: 2D Gaussian model fit to the contoured region to approximate semi-major and semi-minor axes [Source Identification and Photometry fo…: "The semi-major and -minor axes in columns 4 and 5 are a 2D Gaussian model of the region extracted by the contour to approximate the size of each source."]
  - validation: Compared contour photometry with CASA imfit on polygonal regions and Photutils detect_sources/deblend_sources; checked VLA 5, 8, and 15 GHz flux calibration against 1995 AK0400 FITS maps and VLA Calibrator Manual; compared 33 GHz vs 22 GHz… [Source Extraction and Photometry: "As a check, we convolved the 5, 8, and 15 GHz maps from programs AJ314 and 16B-067 to the synthesized beam from (cite: Johnson:2003) to compare the flux calibrations against their 1995 AK0400 program."] [Remarks on Photometry: "In addition to the contour method, we used the CASA task imfit to extract sources from manually drawn polygonal regions. We also utilized an automated source identification program in the Python package *Photoutils* v0.7.2 (cite: Bradley:2019) to identify sources."]
- step 2: Fit 2-component and 3-component power-law SED models to disentangle non-thermal, free-free, and dust emission
  - method: Fit log S_model = log(S_0,nth nu^alpha_nth + S_0,ff nu^alpha_ff + S_0,dust nu^alpha_dust) via chi-squared minimization with fixed spectral indices, excluding the 33 GHz lower limit and modifying the 5 GHz and 15 GHz fluxes and uncertaintie… [Spectral Energy Distributions from 0.9 …: "We fix the spectral indices to the values listed above, and the model describing the 3 components is $$\begin{equation} \log{} S_{model} = \log{ (S_{0,nth}\;\nu^{\alpha_{nth}} +S_{0,ff}\; \nu^{\alpha_{ff}}+S_{0,dust}\;\nu^{\alpha_{dust}} )}, \label{eq:SED} \end{equation}$$"] [Spectral Energy Distributions from 0.9 …: "We perform a $\chi^2$ minimization for the three free parameters $S_{0,nth}$, $S_{0,ff}$, and $S_{0,dust}$."]
  - parameter: alpha_ff = -0.1, alpha_dust = 4, alpha_nth = -0.7 (-0.5 for Knot 3) [Spectral Energy Distributions from 0.9 …: "The free-free emission is likely optically thin with a spectral index of $\alpha_{ff}$ = –0.1 above 5 GHz (cite: Allen:1998; Kobulnicky:1999). We adopt an optically thin dust component of $\alpha_{dust}$ = 4 (cite: Scoville:2014)."] [Spectral Energy Distributions from 0.9 …: "thus we only show the results of $\alpha_{nth}$ = –0.7 in the sections below. The exception is Knot 3; for both the 2 and 3 component models, $\alpha_{nth}$ = –0.5 is a better fit to the data."]
  - parameter: 5 GHz flux modified to midpoint between Table 4 value and 0.5x value; 15 GHz flux modified to midpoint between Table 4 value and 1.4x value [Spectral Energy Distributions from 0.9 …: "The modified flux density at 5 GHz is the midpoint between the flux density in Table [\[tab:fluxcontour\]](#tab:fluxcontour) and half that value, and the error is the distance between the midpoint and the 5 GHz flux density."] [Spectral Energy Distributions from 0.9 …: "Similarly, the modified flux density at 15 GHz is the midpoint between the flux density in Table [\[tab:fluxcontour\]](#tab:fluxcontour) at 15 GHz and 1.4$\times$ that value; the errors are the distance between those two values."]
  - assumption: Free-free emission is optically thin above 5 GHz (alpha_ff = -0.1) and dust emission is optically thin (alpha_dust = 4) [Spectral Energy Distributions from 0.9 …: "The free-free emission is likely optically thin with a spectral index of $\alpha_{ff}$ = –0.1 above 5 GHz (cite: Allen:1998; Kobulnicky:1999). We adopt an optically thin dust component of $\alpha_{dust}$ = 4 (cite: Scoville:2014)."]
  - fitting: Chi-squared minimization for free amplitude parameters S_0,nth, S_0,ff, S_0,dust [Spectral Energy Distributions from 0.9 …: "We perform a $\chi^2$ minimization for the three free parameters $S_{0,nth}$, $S_{0,ff}$, and $S_{0,dust}$."]
  - validation: Compared alpha_nth = -0.7 vs -0.5 using reduced chi-squared; visually inspected 2- and 3-component models alongside multiwavelength optical/CO context to select 2-component (thermal+dust) for Knots 1a, 1b, 2, 5 and 3-component for Knots 4a… [Spectral Energy Distributions from 0.9 …: "We compared the models with $\alpha_{nth}$ = –0.7, which is a typical synchrotron spectral index, and $\alpha_{nth}$ = –0.5 and found that the reduced chi-squared, $\chi_{\nu}^2$, of $\alpha_{nth}$ = –0.7 models are slightly better"] [Spectral Energy Distributions from 0.9 …: "For this work, we visually inspect the models and leverage the available knowledge at other wavelengths to determine which model and interpretation is more appropriate for each knot."]
- step 3: Estimate ionizing photon production rates (Q_Lyc), cluster stellar masses (M_fit), and visual extinction (A_V)
  - method: Calculate Q_Lyc,fit from the SED-modeled 33 GHz thermal flux density (S_ff,fit) using Equation (2); convert Q_Lyc,fit to cluster stellar mass M_fit using Starburst99 models; calculate Q_Lyc,Pa-alpha from HST/NICMOS Pa-alpha flux density us… [Production Rate of Ionizing Photons and…: "Given the concerns about the 33 GHz data from the 15B-197 program, we utilize the thermal flux density modeled from the SED fit listed in Table [\[tab:finalmodel\]](#tab:finalmodel) to estimate Q$_{Lyc}$."] [Production Rate of Ionizing Photons and…: "We select a representative value of Q$_{Lyc}$ $\approx$ 5.3 $\times$10$^{52}$ photons s$^{-1}$ for a 10$^6$ $\textrm{M}_{\odot}$ cluster, with a standard deviation of 1.2 $\times$10$^{52}$ photons s$^{-1}$ which we treat as a systematic uncertainty in our analysis."]
  - software: Starburst99
  - parameter: T_e = 10^4 K, nu = 33 GHz, D = 9 Mpc [Production Rate of Ionizing Photons and…: "We adopt 10$^4$ K as the typical temperature of an H$\,$ii region."] [Production Rate of Ionizing Photons and…: "Calculated using the modeled thermal flux density at 33 GHz (S$_{ff,fit}$) in Table [\[tab:finalmodel\]](#tab:finalmodel)."]
  - parameter: Starburst99 conversion: Q_Lyc = (5.3 +/- 1.2) x 10^52 photons s^-1 per 10^6 M_sun cluster (< 1 Myr, Z = 0.008 and 0.014, Kroupa and Salpeter IMFs) [Production Rate of Ionizing Photons and…: "We select a representative value of Q$_{Lyc}$ $\approx$ 5.3 $\times$10$^{52}$ photons s$^{-1}$ for a 10$^6$ $\textrm{M}_{\odot}$ cluster, with a standard deviation of 1.2 $\times$10$^{52}$ photons s$^{-1}$ which we treat as a systematic uncertainty in our analysis."]
  - parameter: F(Pa-alpha) / F(H-beta) = 0.317, F187N FWHM = 0.0191 um, A_Pa-alpha / A_K ~ 1.24, A_V / A_K ~ 8.8 (R_V = 3.1) [Production Rate of Ionizing Photons and…: "$$\begin{equation} \frac{\textrm{F(Pa}\alpha)}{\textrm{F(H}\beta)} = 0.317, \label{eq:ratio} \end{equation}$$ for line intensities from Table 4.4 of (cite: Osterbrock:1989), assuming Case B and n$_e$ = 10$^6$ cm$^{-3}$."] [Extinction: "From Equation (4) of (cite: Indebetouw:2005), we expect A$_{\textrm{Pa}\alpha}$/A$_K$ $\sim$1.24 and for R$_V$ = 3.1, A$_V$/A$_K$ $\sim$8.8 (cite: Cardelli:1989)."]
  - assumption: T_e = 10^4 K, Case B recombination, n_e = 10^6 cm^-3, cluster age < 1 Myr, optically thin free-free emission, R_V = 3.1 [Production Rate of Ionizing Photons and…: "for line intensities from Table 4.4 of (cite: Osterbrock:1989), assuming Case B and n$_e$ = 10$^6$ cm$^{-3}$. With Equations ([\[eq:qlyman\]](#eq:qlyman)) – ([\[eq:ratio\]](#eq:ratio)), we can estimate Q$_{Lyc}$ at 33 GHz from F(Pa$\alpha$), assuming an electron temperature of 10$^4$ K and optically thin gas."] [Production Rate of Ionizing Photons and…: "For a 10$^6$ $\textrm{M}_{\odot}$ cluster that is less than 1 Myr old, the values of Q$_{Lyc}$ vary less than a factor of 2 for the choice of the metallicity, IMF, and rotation."]
  - validation: Compared derived A_V range (2.2 - 16) with literature large-aperture value A_V = 10.5 from (cite: Cabanac:2005). [Extinction: "Our estimates of the extinction are more localized but the range is consistent with the extinction reported by (cite: Cabanac:2005)."]
- step 4: Calculate dust masses, gas masses, total cluster masses, and instantaneous mass ratios (star formation efficiencies)
  - method: Use SED-modeled 340 GHz dust flux density (S_dust,fit) to compute dust mass M_d = L_nu / (4 pi kappa_nu B_nu(T_d)) for T_d = (25, 40, 100) K; multiply by gas-to-dust ratio to get M_gas, add M_fit for total mass, and compute instantaneous m… [Submillimeter Emission, Dust Mass, and …: "With S$_{dust,fit}$, we calculate the specific luminosity, L$_{\nu}$, and then the dust mass, M$_d$, is $$\begin{equation} \textrm{M}_d = \frac{\textrm{L}_{\nu}}{4\pi\,\kappa_{\nu}\,\textrm{B}_{\nu}(\textrm{T}_d)}, \end{equation}$$"] [Submillimeter Emission, Dust Mass, and …: "Adopting a dust-to-gas ratio of 1:100 (cite: Wilson:2008) and assuming M$_{fit}$ is the stellar contribution to the total mass, the knots have total masses between 0.5 – 1.1 $\times$ 10$^5$ $\textrm{M}_{\odot}$ for T$_d$ = 100 K."]
  - parameter: T_d = (25, 40, 100) K; nu_0 = 340 GHz; kappa_0 = 1.3 cm^2 g^-1; beta = alpha_dust - 2 = 2 [Submillimeter Emission, Dust Mass, and …: "we adopt a range of dust temperatures of T$_d$ = (25, 40, 100) K"] [Submillimeter Emission, Dust Mass, and …: "where $\nu$ = $\nu_o$ in this case and $\beta = \alpha_{dust} - 2$. We adopt $\kappa_o$ = 1.3 cm$^2$ g$^{-1}$ at $\nu_o$ = 340 GHz (cite: Whitcomb:1981; Hildebrand:1983)"]
  - parameter: Dust-to-gas ratio = 1:100 (also tested 1:300) [Submillimeter Emission, Dust Mass, and …: "Adopting a dust-to-gas ratio of 1:100 (cite: Wilson:2008)"] [Star Formation Efficiency: "for example, a dust-to-gas ratio of 1:300 yields IMR $\sim$45 – 72$\%$ for T$_d$ = 100 K and IMR $\sim$ 14 – 33$\%$ for T$_d$ = 25 K."]
  - assumption: Optically thin dust emission at 340 GHz, kappa_0 = 1.3 cm^2 g^-1 at 340 GHz, T_d = (25, 40, 100) K, dust-to-gas ratio of 1:100 [Submillimeter Emission, Dust Mass, and …: "We adopt $\kappa_o$ = 1.3 cm$^2$ g$^{-1}$ at $\nu_o$ = 340 GHz (cite: Whitcomb:1981; Hildebrand:1983) but note that the uncertainty can be as large as a factor of 2."] [Submillimeter Emission, Dust Mass, and …: "Adopting a dust-to-gas ratio of 1:100 (cite: Wilson:2008) and assuming M$_{fit}$ is the stellar contribution to the total mass"]
  - validation: Compared estimated free-free fraction at 340 GHz (48 - 68%) with previous estimate (45 - 64%) from (cite: Hirashita:2013). [Submillimeter Emission, Dust Mass, and …: "Comparing the modeled dust emission, S$_{dust,fit}$ to the measured flux density at 340 GHz, we estimate that 48 – 68$\%$ of the 340 GHz flux density is due to free-free emission. This agrees with other estimates from (cite: Hirashita:2013)"]
