- result: Instantaneous mass ratio (IMR) / SFE range (T_d = 25 K, 1:100 dust-to-gas ratio) = 32 - 60 ± not stated % [Star Formation Efficiency: "For T$_d$ = 100 K, the IMR is between $\sim$71 – 88$\%$ for the radio knots; for colder dust (25 K), the IMR is $\sim$32 – 60$\%$."]
- limitation (authors): The new 33 GHz VLA observations suffer from significant decorrelation due to weather and low declination (-26 deg), so the 33 GHz flux densities must be treated as lower limits and excluded from SED fitting. [New VLA Observations: "High frequency observing is sensitive to the weather conditions, and given the low declination of the source, we believe the data suffer from significant decorrelation. Thus, the flux densities reported here are treated as a lower limit thorough this work."]
- limitation (authors): Significant flux calibration discrepancies exist between the 2004 AJ314 / 2016 16B-067 VLA maps and the 1995 AK0400 maps (~2x higher at 5 GHz and ~1.4x lower at 15 GHz), requiring ad hoc adjustments to the 5 and 15 GHz fluxes and uncertain… [Source Extraction and Photometry: "We find that for any given size of aperture, our 2004 AJ314 5 GHz map is $\sim$2$\times$ higher in flux density than the 1995 AK0400 5 GHz map, which was previously noted by (cite: Reines:2011)."]
- limitation (authors): Forcing a 0.3'' circular restoring beam results in significant super-resolution in the declination direction for the 5 GHz map (native 0.620'' x 0.248'') and modest/moderate super-resolution for the 8 GHz and 113 GHz maps. [Source Extraction and Photometry: "For C-band, this is a significant super-resolution of the data in the declination direction."]
- not stated (card): Exact spectral window and line-free channel selections used to image the ALMA 113, 250, and 340 GHz continuum maps; Whether ALMA projects 2015.1.01569.S and 2016.1.00027.S were combined for the 113 GHz continuum map or if only 2016.1.00027.S was used (Table 2 lists only 2016.1.00027); Image cell sizes, image dimensions, deconvolution algorithms, and clean stopping thresholds used in CASA tclean; …

### 2016ApJ...832..187B: The Massive Protostellar Cluster NGC 6334I at 220 au Resolution: Discovery of Further Multiplicity, Diversity, and a Hot Multi-core (2016; star_formation; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What is the multiplicity, nature and SED of the sources in the massive protocluster NGC6334I at ~220 AU resolution from 5 cm to 1.3 mm?
- step 4: SED modeling
  - method: Free-free model V of Olnon75 (fit r_e) plus modified greybody dust fits with fixed beta=1.7; uncertainties from 1000 Monte Carlo realizations [5.1 SEDs: "The uncertainties on the fitted parameters were obtained by computing the standard deviation of the fitted parameters obtained from fitting an ensemble of 1000 realizations"]
  - parameter: Te=1e4 K; ne = 3e5, 3e6, 3e7 cm-3 for MM1D, MM1B, CM1 [5.1 SEDs: "we set $n_e$ to $3\times10^5$, $3\times10^6$, and $3\times10^7$ cm$^{-3}$ for MM1D, MM1B, and CM1, respectively"]
  - assumption: beta=1.7 (MM1D uses 2.4) [5.1 SEDs: "we first chose to use a fixed value of $\beta=1.7$"]
  - fitting: Least squares greybody; sampler/priors not stated [Discussion, Spectral Energy Distributio…: "we fit the SED of each of these sources with a modified greybody function"]
  - validation: Fitted tau compared with computed tau_dust [5.1 SEDs: "shows that our modeling approach is self-consistent"]
- result: MM4A dust temperature = 98 ± +5/-6 K [Table 5: "| MM4A | 97 (5) | 98$^{+5}_{-6}$ | 5.3$^{+1.6}_{-1.0}$"]
- result: MM2A-core dust temperature = 152 ± +48/-19 K [Table 5: "| MM2Acore | 98 (4) | 152$^{+48}_{-19}$"]
- result: MM2B 7mm-1.3mm spectral index = 1.63 ± 0.44 dimensionless [5.2.3 The Hot Core MM2: "The SED of MM2B exhibits a 7 mm-1.3 mm spectral index of $1.63\pm0.44$"]
- result: MM1D beta = 2.4 ± not stated dimensionless [5.1 SEDs: "The fit shown in Fig. [6](#seds1) uses $\beta=2.4$"]
- limitation (authors): SEDs lack points near peak so Tdust, tau, beta cannot be constrained simultaneously [5.1 SEDs: "we are unable to accurately constrain $T_{dust}$, the greybody opacity ($\tau_\nu$), and the grain emissivity index ($\beta$) simultaneously"]
- not stated (card): Line-free channel ranges and threshold per spw; Image sizes, clean thresholds and masks; Self-calibration solution intervals; Greybody fit code and frequency/flux inputs details; Initial guesses for Gaussian fits and fitting software; Details of the rms measurement regions

### 2023PASJ...75..874M: The centimeter-to-submillimeter broad-band radio spectrum of the central compact component in a nearby type-II Seyfert galaxy NGC 1068 (2023; agn_and_quasars; archival_reuse)
- question: Origin of the centimeter/submillimeter continuum emission around the vicinity of active supermassive black holes
- step 2: Measure S1 and assemble the broadband SED
  - method: Fit ALMA S1 flux and deconvolved size with imfit; combine with literature VLA and VLBA measurements [SED: "We measure the flux of S1 ($F_{\rm S1}$) and beam deconvolved source size (represented by $\theta_{\rm maj}$, $\theta_{\rm min}$, and position angle: PA) using `imfit` task in `CASA` by ourselves."] [Representative SED: "For the VLA and VLBA measurements, we use the literature data."]
  - software: CASA, imfit
  - parameter: σ = sqrt(σ_imfit^2 + (0.1 F_S1)^2) [SED: "\sigma=\sqrt{\sigma_\mathrm{imfit}^2+{(0.1F_{\rm S1})}^2}."]
  - assumption: 10% systematic error [SED: "Therefore, we assume the $10\%$ systematic error"]
  - fitting: 2D Gaussian fit; imfit fitting errors [Spectrum at $>10$ GHz: "S1 measured by the 2D Gaussian fit"] [SED: "The error ($\sigma_\mathrm{imfit}$) is estimated using the imfit task, which is based on the fitting error."]
  - validation: Use the same minimum uv-distance in all ALMA bands [SED: "We use images whose minimum baseline used for imaging is the same among Band 3, 6, 7, and 9 ($>300$ k$\lambda$)."]
- step 5: Test jet and dust alone
  - method: Compare a jet power law plus dust grey body with the SED [Two components (jet + dust): "tries to explain the SED by two components, i.e., jet PL (purple) and dust grey body (blue)."]
  - parameter: α_jet ≈ -0.5 [Two components (jet + dust): "Even if we assume the PL index of $\alpha_{\rm jet}\approx-0.5$ (without energy loss at the high frequency)"]
  - assumption: T_dust = 46 K; β = 2 [Multiple components: "Therefore, we assume $T_{\rm dust}=46$ K and $\beta=2$ according to (cite: Garc2016ApJ...823L..12G) for cold dust."]
  - assumption: κ_ν ~ κ_352GHz (ν[GHz]/352)^β; κ_352GHz = 0.09 m^2 kg^-1 [Multiple components: "$\sim\kappa_\mathrm{352GHz}\times(\nu\,\mathrm{[GHz]}/352)^\beta$ with $\kappa_\mathrm{352GHz}=0.09\,\mathrm{m^2 kg^{-1}}$"]
  - assumption: D_L = 13.97±2.10 Mpc [Introduction: "The nearby spiral galaxy NGC 1068 located at the distance of $D_{\rm L}=13.97\pm2.10$ Mpc (cite: Anand2021MNRAS.501.3621A)"]
  - validation: VLA points lie below prediction and Bands 6 and 7 above it; offsets cannot be explained by the observed beamsize effects [Two components (jet + dust): "the VLA data points are systematically lower than the predicted line (black) and Band 6 and 7 points are higher."] [Two components (jet + dust): "These systematic offsets can not be explained by beamsize effects"]
- step 6: Fit jet, corona and dust with an absorbing screen
  - method: Add coronal SSA to jet power law and dust grey body; apply free-free absorption for the low-frequency drop [Three components (jet + corona + dust) …: "Because the PL at low frequency and grey-body at high frequency cannot explain the excess of 90-400 GHz emission, we consider the SSA components originally from the corona."] [Screen by ionized gas: "to explain the rapid drop at $<10$ GHz, we consider that the emissions are screened by free–free absorption"]
  - parameter: Jet: S_100GHz = 6.5 mJy; α_jet = -0.75 [Three components (jet + corona + dust) …: "We consider that the 22 GHz continuum is mainly from synchrotron emission with $(S_\mathrm{100\,GHz},\alpha_{\rm jet})=(6.5\,\mathrm{mJy}, -0.75)$"]
  - parameter: Dust mass: 6.5×10^3 M_sun [Three components (jet + corona + dust) …: "For the high frequency (i.e., ALMA Band 9), we consider the grey-body with the dust mass of $M_\mathrm{dust}=6.5\times10^3\,M_{\odot}$"]
  - parameter: Example screen: n_e = 3×10^3 cm^-3; T_e = 10,000 K; l_L.O.S = 20 pc [Screen by ionized gas: "($n_\mathrm{e}$, $T_\mathrm{e}$, $l_\mathrm{L.O.S}$) = ($3\times10^3$ cm$^{-3}$, $10,000$ K, $20$ pc)"]
  - assumption: Electron-energy spectrum slope δ = 2.7 [Three components (jet + corona + dust) …: "Assuming $\delta=2.7$"]
  - assumption: T_dust = 46 K; β = 2; κ_352GHz = 0.09 m^2 kg^-1 [Multiple components: "Therefore, we assume $T_{\rm dust}=46$ K and $\beta=2$ according to (cite: Garc2016ApJ...823L..12G) for cold dust."] [Multiple components: "$\kappa_\mathrm{352GHz}=0.09\,\mathrm{m^2 kg^{-1}}$"]
  - fitting: Bayesian parameter estimation of S_νSSA and ν_SSA; uncertainty from marginalized-distribution percentiles [Three components (jet + corona + dust) …: "($15^{+3}_{-3}$ mJy, $134^{+28}_{-22}$ GHz) based on Bayesian parameter estimation"] [Three components (jet + corona + dust) …: "The error terms of $S_{\nu_\mathrm{SSA}}$, $\nu_\mathrm{SSA}$, $B$, and $R$ are calculated based on the 16th, 50th, and 84th percentiles of the samples in the marginalized distributions."]
  - validation: Includes the new 100 GHz point; fails to reproduce the inverse spectrum between Bands 6 and 7 [Three components (jet + corona + dust) …: "our new analysis improves the overall behavior of the SED by including new 100 GHz data."] [Three components (jet + corona + dust) …: "The SED fit does not reproduce the inverse spectrum between Band 6 and 7."]
- result: Nuclear flux density over 5–700 GHz = ≈10–20 ± not stated mJy [Summary: "We report that the nucleus region of Seyfert galaxy NGC 1068 has the flux density at 5–700 GHz (centimeter/submillimeter) of $\approx$ 10–20 mJy"]
- result: SSA normalization S_νSSA = 15 ± +3/-3 mJy [Three components (jet + corona + dust) …: "the best fit parameter is ($S_{\nu_\mathrm{SSA}},\nu_\mathrm{SSA}$)$=$($15^{+3}_{-3}$ mJy, $134^{+28}_{-22}$ GHz)"]
- result: SSA frequency ν_SSA = 134 ± +28/-22 GHz [Three components (jet + corona + dust) …: "the best fit parameter is ($S_{\nu_\mathrm{SSA}},\nu_\mathrm{SSA}$)$=$($15^{+3}_{-3}$ mJy, $134^{+28}_{-22}$ GHz)"]
- limitation (authors): Limited angular resolution prevents distinguishing competing emission scenarios [Summary: "However, due to limited angular resolution, a single free-free and a jet + dust scenario are also possible scenarios."]
- limitation (authors): Coronal-parameter assumption systematics are excluded from quoted errors [Three components (jet + corona + dust) …: "The systematic errors regarding assumptions (e.g., several coronal parameters such as a typical energy fraction of non-thermal electrons and the Thomson scattering optical depth) are not considered."]
- limitation (authors): The SED fit misses the inverse spectrum between Bands 6 and 7 [Three components (jet + corona + dust) …: "The SED fit does not reproduce the inverse spectrum between Band 6 and 7."]
- not stated (card): ALMA project codes, execution-block identifiers and the actual project table are absent from the supplied text.; Exact retained spectral windows, channel selections and effective continuum bandwidths.; Calibration CASA versions for individual execution blocks.; tclean image dimensions, deconvolver, stopping threshold, iteration limit and primary-beam treatment.; Exact geometry and sky position of…

### 2025A&A...699A.183H: Spatially resolved spectrophotometric SED modeling of NGC 253's central molecular zone: I. Star formation in extragalactic giant molecular clouds (2025; nearby_galaxies, star_formation; multi_facility_alma_minor)
- question: Build the first spatially-resolved panchromatic (near-UV to cm) SED of GMCs in the NGC 253 central molecular zone at 3 arcsec (~51 pc) and derive SFRs, stellar and dust masses, and test monochromatic SFR tracers against the SED values
- step 3: Fit panchromatic SEDs with GalaPy (primary)
  - method: In-Situ SFH model with parsec22.NTL SSPs, two-component age-dependent dust model, Chabrier IMF, one isolated molecular cloud per GMC, with free parameters over given priors [3.1 GalaPy photometric SED fitting: "We perform the SED fitting of our selected GMCs with GalaPy (cite: Ronconi2024)"] [3.1.1 The In-Situ model: "For our models, we use the default In-Situ SFH model"]
  - software: GalaPy
  - parameter: Parameter ranges for all GMCs and per-GMC tuned ranges listed in Tables 2 and variable-parameter table [3.1.3 Dust model: "A list of all common parameter ranges and individual values used to model the SEDs in the ten GMCs is provided in Table"]
  - parameter: Ages restricted to 10^6-11 or 10^6-12 yr [8 Individual GMC properties: "a permitted range of 10$^{6-11}$ or 10$^{6-12}$ years was imposed to all GMCs"]
  - assumption: Chabrier IMF; flat LCDM with h about 0.7; N_MC=1 (isolated GMCs) [3.1 GalaPy photometric SED fitting: "GalaPy assumes a Chabrier initial mass function (cite: Chabrier2003)"] [3.1.3 Dust model: "as in this work we are assuming isolated GMCs"]
  - validation: Cross-validated against CIGALE; best-fit SEDs compared with FIR 9 arcsec test including Herschel PACS [3 Analysis: "To cross-validate the results obtained from GalaPy, we compared them with those from the broadly used Code Investigating GALaxy Emission (CIGALE)."]
- step 4: Fit SEDs with CIGALE for validation
  - method: CIGALE 2022.1 UV-to-radio fits with delayed SFH plus burst, BC03 SSPs, Calzetti attenuation (dustatt_modified_starburst), Draine 2014 dust emission, radio module (q_IR); AGN version adds SKIRTOR [3.2 CIGALE photometric SED fitting: "simultaneous UV-to-radio SED modeling with the CIGALE 2022.1 version (cite: Yang22)"] [3.2 CIGALE photometric SED fitting: "Dust attenuation was modeled following the (cite: Calzetti2000) attenuation law"]
  - software: CIGALE
  - parameter: Parameter grid in CIGALE input table (tau_main 300-5000 Myr, f_burst, age, U_min, q_PAH, q_IR etc.) [7 CIGALE SED modeling with AGN component: "The list of input parameters to CIGALE analysis without and with AGN component is given in Table"]
  - assumption: Solar-like metallicity; text states Chabrier IMF while the parameter table lists Salpeter [3.2 CIGALE photometric SED fitting: "adopting the IMF of (cite: Chabrier2003) and assuming a solar-like metallicity"]
  - fitting: chi-squared minimization of data minus model weighted by uncertainties [3.2 CIGALE photometric SED fitting: "CIGALE minimizes $\chi^2$ by measuring the difference between observed data and the model predictions, weighted by the uncertainties in the observations."]
  - validation: Mean reduced chi2 of 2.2 over ten GMCs [7 CIGALE SED modeling with AGN component: "These CIGALE inputs have yielded a mean reduced $\chi^2$ of 2.2 for the ten GMCs studied in this work."]
- step 6: Derive attenuation and emission-line diagnostics
  - method: Balmer-decrement A_V with Calzetti law, comparison with CIGALE and STARLIGHT A_V; BPT and WHAN diagrams from emission lines after stellar continuum subtraction [4.2.2 Attenuation estimations: "The attenuation from dust in HII regions can be estimated using the Balmer lines H$\alpha$ and H$\beta$"] [4.2.1 Emission line diagnostic diagrams: "The fluxes of emission lines from the MUSE spectra were measured in the pure nebular spectra"]
  - software: STARLIGHT
  - parameter: Intrinsic Halpha/Hbeta = 2.86 [4.2.2 Attenuation estimations: "The intrinsic H$\alpha$/H$\beta$ flux ratio is 2.86 under typical conditions"]
  - assumption: Calzetti reddening law A_Hbeta/A_V=1.14 and A_Halpha/A_V=0.82 [4.2.2 Attenuation estimations: "where $A_{\mathrm{H}\beta}/A_{\rm{V}} = 1.14$ and $A_{\mathrm{H}\alpha}/A_{\rm{V}} = 0.82$ for a (cite: Calzetti2000) reddening law"]
  - validation: Balmer A_V compared with CIGALE and STARLIGHT (median 16% and 27% differences) [4.2.2 Attenuation estimations: "CIGALE values of $A_{\rm{V}}$ are, in median, 16% lower than those from the Balmer decrement"]
- step 7: Compare monochromatic SFR tracers with SED SFR
  - method: Linear regression in log space: scipy linregress for initial values then emcee MCMC with uncertainties on both axes; Halpha (Kennicutt 1998), H40alpha RRL, cm radio continuum and IR bands against GalaPy SFR [4.2.3 Linear regression: "The second step applied Bayesian inference through Markov Chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) sampling using the `emcee` Python package (cite: Foreman-Mackey2013)."] [4.3.3 Radio cm continuum and FIR: "by performing a linear regression in logarithmic space with all retrieved (E)VLA continuum images that detect at least four GMCs"]
  - software: emcee, scipy, CASA
  - parameter: 1,000 iterations per fit, 50 walkers, first 100 discarded as burn-in [4.2.3 Linear regression: "We ran 1,000 iterations per fit with 50 walkers, discarding the first 100 as burn-in to ensure convergence."]
  - assumption: Halpha SFR with A_Halpha = 0.82 x A_V(Balmer); distance 3.5 Mpc for mJy to erg/s/Hz [4.3.1 Kennicutt (1998): "where $L(\mathrm{H\alpha})$ is corrected for extinction using $A_{\rm{H}\alpha} = 0.82 \times A_{\rm{V}}^{\rm Balmer}$."]
  - fitting: MCMC (emcee) priors initialised from scipy linregress; likelihood form not stated [4.2.3 Linear regression: "to obtain initial priors"]
  - validation: Pearson correlation and slope vs 1:1 line; comparison with Bendo et al. 2015 total SFR [4.3.2 Radio recombination lines: "In general, the correlation is strong ($\langle R \rangle =$0.97)"]
- result: SFR (GalaPy instantaneous), total of GMCs 4-6 in a 9 arcsec aperture = 1.620 ± +0.201/-0.156 Msun/yr [4.3.2 Radio recombination lines: "yields a SFR of $1.620^{+0.201}_{-0.156}~\text{M}_{\odot}\,\text{yr}^{-1}$"]
- result: Total SFR GMCs 3-7 (GalaPy, 9 arcsec region plus GMCs 3 and 7) = 1.729 ± +0.201/-0.156 Msun/yr [4.3.2 Radio recombination lines: "the total SFR raises to $1.729^{+0.201}_{-0.156}~\text{M}_{\odot}\,\text{yr}^{-1}$"]
- result: GMC 5 instantaneous SFR (GalaPy) = 6450e-4 (0.645) ± +600/-590 (x1e-4) Msun/yr [Table (GalaPy main results): "| 5 | 6450$_{-590}^{+600}$ | 8.620$_{-0.105}^{+0.124}$ |"]
- result: Diffuse dust temperature T_DD, 9 arcsec aperture SED = 68.8 ± +7.8/-13.9 K [5.1 Reliability of our SED model along …: "of 68.8$_{-13.9}^{+7.8}$ K"]
- result: Median log(Mdust/Mstar), CIGALE values = -2.32 ± 0.29 dex [5.2 Comparison to high-z dusty starburs…: "$\log(M_{\rm dust}/M_{\star}) = -2.32 \pm 0.29$ (values from CIGALE)"]
- result: Median log(Mdust/Mstar), GalaPy values = -2.89 ± 0.45 dex [5.2 Comparison to high-z dusty starburs…: "$\log(M_{\rm dust}/M_{\star}) = -2.89 \pm 0.45$"]
- result: AGN fraction in CIGALE with AGN module = <7.5 (negligible) ± not stated percent [4.2.1 Emission line diagnostic diagrams: "is negligible ($<$7.5%)"]
- result: Mean reduced chi2 of CIGALE fits = 2.2 ± not stated dimensionless [7 CIGALE SED modeling with AGN component: "a mean reduced $\chi^2$ of 2.2 for the ten GMCs studied in this work"]
- limitation (authors): Large uncertainties on the observed Halpha/Hbeta ratio (10 to nearly 90%) [4.2.2 Attenuation estimations: "ranging from 10 to nearly 90%"]
- not stated (card): How the 3 arcsec aperture fluxes were measured from the ALCHEMI continuum maps (continuum definition, line-free channels, primary-beam correction, aperture centering software); ALMA continuum bandwidth/frequency selection behind each band flux, and flux scale/calibrator details (deferred to Martin et al. 2021); GalaPy sampler settings (number of walkers, steps, convergence) and likelihood form; F…

### 2020ApJ...905...89Y: Kinematical Signs of Dust Trapping and Feedback in a Local Pressure Bump in the Protoplanetary Disk around HD 142527 Revealed with ALMA (2020; protoplanetary_disks; archival_reuse)
- question: Does the gas kinematics (deviation from Keplerian rotation) in the HD 142527 disk reveal a local pressure bump, and how does it relate to the dust density, gas-to-dust mass ratio and dust beta (dust trapping and feedback)?
- step 2: Fit pixel-by-pixel SED to get dust temperature, optical depth and beta
  - method: Gray-body slab model I_nu = (B_nu(Td) - B_nu(Tbg)) (1 - exp(-tau_nu)) with tau_nu = tau_100GHz (nu/100 GHz)^beta, fitted to the six-wavelength continuum intensities at each position [3.1 Dust distribution: "We performed simple fitting of spectral energy distributions (SED) using the continuum data at six different wavelengths to derive the dust distribution in the protoplanetary disk around HD 142527."] [3.1 Dust distribution: "and we assumed $\tau_\nu$ to be a simple power-law function of frequency,"]
  - parameter: Free parameters per position: Td, tau_100GHz, beta [3.1 Dust distribution: "The derived maps of $T_{\rm d}$, $\tau_{\rm 100~GHz}$, and $\beta$ are shown in Fig."]
  - parameter: CMB temperature 2.73 K subtracted; absolute flux uncertainties of 5-20% by band included in the error estimate [3.1 Dust distribution: "$T_{\rm bg}$ is the comic microwave background temperature of 2.73 K"] [3.1 Dust distribution: "of 5% in Band 3 and 4, 10% in Band 6 and 7, and 20% in Band 8 and 9 were considered in our error estimate."]
  - assumption: Dust scattering not included in the SED model [3.1 Dust distribution: "The effects of dust scattering were not considered in our simple SED fitting."]
  - validation: Test fit at the 1.3 mm peak with 0.9-0.4 mm intensities artificially raised by 20-30%: Td and tau change by 10-30%, beta by 0.1 (<10%) [3.1 Dust distribution: "We have tested our SED fitting at the 1.3 mm intensity peak by artificially increasing the intensities at 0.9 mm to 0.4 mm by 20% to 30%."]
- step 4: Estimate gas surface density from the C18O (1-0, 2-1, 3-2) spectra
  - method: At each position, LTE radiative transfer through a vertical temperature and hydrostatic density structure plus a thin midplane dust layer, fitted to the three C18O spectra [3.2 Gas distribution: "we computed the radiative transfer equation assuming the local thermal equilibrium (LTE) condition, integrated the equation along the line of sight,"] [3.2 Gas distribution: "generated model spectra of the C$^{18}$O (3–2), (2–1), and (1–0) lines to fit the observations for every position in the HD 142527 disk."]
  - parameter: Vertical temperature profile exponent n = 2 [3.2 Gas distribution: "Here we adopted $n=2$."]
  - parameter: Midplane temperature Tm = dust temperature from the SED fit [3.2 Gas distribution: "In our calculations, $T_m$ was adopted to the dust temperature."]
  - parameter: Atmosphere temperature Ta = peak brightness temperature of 13CO (3-2) without continuum subtraction [3.2 Gas distribution: "$T_{\rm a}$ was adopted to be the peak brightness temperature of $^{13}$CO (3–2) without continuum subtraction"]
  - parameter: Za = one pressure scale height, with stellar mass 2.48 Msun [3.2 Gas distribution: "computed with the midplane temperature and the stellar mass of 2.48 $M_\odot$"] [footnote 3: "adopting $Z_{\rm a}$ to be one pressure scale height provides better fits to the observed spectra."]
  - parameter: Model centroid velocity and line width set to the mean of Gaussian fits to the three observed C18O lines [3.2 Gas distribution: "the centroid velocity and line width of the model spectra were adopted to be the mean centroid velocity and line width of the three C$^{18}$O lines"]
  - assumption: C18O abundance 2e-7, zero where temperature is below 20 K (CO freeze-out) [3.2 Gas distribution: "The C$^{18}$O abundance was assumed to be a constant of $2\times10^{-7}$ (cite: Wilson94; Jorgensen04), and it was set to be zero in the regions where the temperature is below 20 K"]
  - assumption: Dust settled to the midplane and thermally coupled with gas, so Td represents the midplane temperature [3.2 Gas distribution: "Assuming the dust grains have settled to the midplane and are thermally coupled with gas in the HD 142527 disk"]
  - assumption: Disk treated as face-on (no inclination correction in the radiative transfer), inclination 26 deg [3.2 Gas distribution: "We did not consider that the line of sight is inclined with respect to the disk midplane in our radiative transfer calculation"]
  - validation: Model fluxes typically match observations within 3 sigma or within 20% [3.2 Gas distribution: "The fluxes of our model spectra typically match with the observations within the 3$\sigma$ uncertainties, or the difference is less than 20%."]
- step 5: Compute the gas-to-dust mass ratio map
  - method: Ratio of the C18O-derived gas surface density to the SED-derived dust surface density at each position [3.3 Gas-to-dust mass ratio: "With the gas and dust surface density estimated from the C$^{18}$O lines and the continuum, the gas-to-dust mass ratio was computed."]
  - assumption: Absolute ratio scales inversely with the assumed C18O abundance; only the spatial trend is interpreted [3.3 Gas-to-dust mass ratio: "If the C$^{18}$O abundance is ten times lower than our assumed value, the derived gas-to-dust mass ratio becomes ten times higher."]
  - validation: Trend of lower ratios in the north is consistent with Soon et al. 2019 [3.3 Gas-to-dust mass ratio: "This trend of the lower gas-to-dust mass ratios in the north than in the south has also been reported by (cite: Soon19)."]
- result: Median fractional uncertainties of Td, tau_100GHz and beta from the SED fit = 18%, 22%, 8% ± not stated percent [3.1 Dust distribution: "The median uncertainties are 18%, 22%, and 8% in $T_{\rm d}$, $\tau_{\rm 100~GHz}$, and $\beta$, respectively."]
- result: Dust beta in the northern part of the dust ring = ~1.5-1.8 ± not stated dimensionless [3.1 Dust distribution: "Our derived $\beta$ of $\sim$1.5–1.8 in the northern part of the dust ring"]
- result: Dust beta from south to north = decreases from two to approximately one ± not stated dimensionless [Summary: "the dust $\beta$ decreases from two to approximately one from the southern to northern parts of the disk"]
- result: Gas-to-dust mass ratio range and mean = 0.4 to 9, mean 1.6 ± median 50% dimensionless [3.3 Gas-to-dust mass ratio: "The estimated gas-to-dust mass ratios range from 0.4 to 9 with a mean value of 1.6"]
- result: Gas-to-dust mass ratio in the northern region relative to disk mean = more than a factor of three lower ± not stated dimensionless [3.3 Gas-to-dust mass ratio: "the gas-to-dust mass ratio is more than a factor of three lower than the mean ratio in the disk"]
- limitation (authors): Absolute gas-to-dust ratios are not interpreted because of uncertain C18O abundance and dust absorption coefficient [3.3 Gas-to-dust mass ratio: "we do not discuss the absolute values of the estimated gas-to-dust mass ratio"]
- limitation (authors): Dust scattering not modelled in the SED fit [3.1 Dust distribution: "The effects of dust scattering were not considered in our simple SED fitting."]
- limitation (authors): Velocity features within the 0.45 arcsec beam are uncertain [5.1 Deviation from Keplerian rotation: "any velocity feature within a radius of 0.45$''$ is uncertain due to the beam convolution"]
- not stated (card): Array type (12 m vs 7 m) and configurations of each project, and which execution blocks or MOUS were used; CASA version and ALMA pipeline version, and which projects used the delivered scripts versus the pipeline; flux, bandpass and phase calibrators; Self-calibration details: solution intervals, number of iterations, reference antenna, which datasets were self-calibrated and the SNR criteria; Li…

### 2014ApJ...791...42Z: Comparison of the Dust and Gas Radial Structure in the Transition Disk [PZ99] J160421.7-213028 (2014; protoplanetary_disks; observational_alma_primary)
- question: How do the radial structures of mm-sized dust and CO gas compare in the transition disk J1604-2130, and what do they imply for the cavity formation mechanism?
- step 2: Constrain amax and inner-disk structure from SED
  - method: Beta from mm slope gives amax; LM chi2 fit of h0, phi, Rgap to 1-200 um SED with RADMC models [4.2 Dust emission: fitting procedure an…: "We then use the Levenberg-Marquardt $\chi^2$ minimization algorithm (LM) to search for the optimum values of $h_0$, $\phi$, and $R_{gap}$"]
  - software: RADMC
  - parameter: amax ~0.05 mm; kappa(880um)=7.26 cm2/g [4.2 Dust emission: fitting procedure an…: "The dust opacity at 880 $\,{\rm \mu m}$ is 7.26 cm$^2$ g$^{-1}$."]
  - assumption: Grain size distribution n(a)∝a^-3.5, amin=0.005 um; Mie spheres; silicates+carbonaceous [4.1 Dust emission: model description: "We assume a power-law grain size distribution, $n(a) \propto a^{-3.5}$, from $a_{ \rm min}$= 0.005 $\,{\rm \mu m}$ to a given $a_{\rm max}$."]
  - fitting: LM chi2 minimization [Dust emission: fitting procedure and re…: "We then use the Levenberg-Marquardt $\chi^2$ minimization algorithm (LM) to search for the optimum values of $h_0$, $\phi$, and $R_{gap}$ by comparing the synthetic disk emission with the measured SED"]
  - validation: WISE-based alternative blackbody ring fit gives larger Rgap [4.2 Dust emission: fitting procedure an…: "Fitting the WISE photometry with an optically thick blackbody component results in a larger R$_{gap}$ = 26.5$\pm$2.8 AU"]
- result: Rgap from SED = 14.6 ± ±2.5 AU [4.2 Dust emission: fitting procedure an…: "R$_{gap}$ = 14.6$\pm$2.5 AU, $\phi$ = 0.6$\pm$0.1, and $h_{100 {\rm AU}}$ = 4.0$\pm$0.2 AU"]
- limitation (authors): Fit uncertainties underestimate true uncertainty due to gamma degeneracy [4.5 Uncertainties from the choice of $\…: "Thus the uncertainties in Table [1](#table:paras) should be used with caution."]
- not stated (card): Archive/pipeline version and calibration scripts beyond CASA 4.1; Self-calibration solution intervals and number of iterations; Line-free channel ranges for continuum and CO continuum subtraction; CLEAN mask, threshold, cell and image size; Parameter priors/bounds for LM and MCMC; MCMC step settings, burn-in; Model grid setup (RADMC/RADLite resolution) and SED photometry values; Velocity range us…

### 2016ApJ...818...45S: The Inner Debris Structure in the Fomalhaut Planetary System (2016; debris_disks; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What is the nature of the warm (ice-line) excess in the inner Fomalhaut system, and what does ALMA 870 um imaging constrain about an inner asteroid-belt analog?
- step 2: SED model of warm asteroid belt and ALMA upper limit
  - method: Optically thin narrow-ring SED model fit to data beyond 10 um; flux limit from number of beams around ring times 3 sigma rms [4.2: "a narrow ring of 9 (13) AU would cover $\sim$13 (17) beams in its circumference in our cycle 1 map."]
  - parameter: Astronomical silicates, blowout size ~2.5-5 um, max grain 1 mm, ring width 2 AU, Gaussian profile, slope -3.5 to -3.7 [4.2: "We further assume the warm dust belt is confined in a narrow ring (NR) with a fixed width of 2 AU."]
  - assumption: Inner belt inclined 66 deg like outer belt [4.2: "Assuming the inner belt is inclined by 66$^\circ$ from face-on like the outer cold belt"]
  - validation: Simulated 13 AU belt observation with simobserve, noise tuned to 26 uJy/beam [4.2: "We then added the random thermal noise by adjusting the PWV parameters in $simobserve$ until the measured noise level reaches 26 $\mu$Jy beam$^{-1}$"]
- step 3: Model inner debris (asteroid belt + P-R disk + pile-up + hot ring) against SED and KIN nulls
  - method: Construct SED for each component, adjust scalings, simulate 7-14 um images and compute KIN null levels vs observed [4.3.1: "Using these model images, we then compute the expected null levels on the given dates and baselines of the KIN observations"]
  - parameter: Table 2 component parameters [Table 2: "Asteroid Belt | Silicates | 5 | 1000 | $-$3.65 | Gaussian Ring | 13 | 2"]
  - assumption: Inner structures share cold belt i=66 deg, PA=156 deg [4.3.1: "Assuming that the inner dust structures have the same inclination and position angles as the cold belt ($i$=66$^\circ$ and P.A.=156$^\circ$)"]
  - validation: Comparison of model optical depth with low and high collision theory and Lebreton model [4.3.1: "Figure [5](#complex_model_optdepth) shows the model optical depth distribution for two of the fits in comparison with the theoretical values"]
- result: 3 sigma upper limit of inner belt flux = 1 (9 AU ring) and 1.3 (13 AU ring) ± not stated mJy at 870 um [4.2: "the corresponding 3$\sigma$ upper limit for the whole inner belt is 1 mJy and 1.3 mJy at 870 ${\rm \mu m}$ for such rings."]
- result: Asteroid belt radial location = ~8-15 ± not stated AU [abstract: "indicates a radial location in the range $\sim$8–15 AU"]
- limitation (authors): Putative 13-AU belt needs deeper data; SED models degenerate due to lack of Rayleigh-Jeans constraints [4.2: "Nevertheless, a deeper map is needed to confirm this putative 13-AU belt."]
- not stated (card): Weighting/robust, cell size, image size, clean threshold and niter not given; Initial data reduction route (pipeline vs manual script) and calibrator flux values not given; uvmodelfit starting parameters; Exact SED fitting procedure/goodness of fit for the SED and KIN null comparison; simobserve configuration and integration settings

### 2026A&A...705A.196H: The ALMA survey to Resolve exoKuiper belt Substructures (ARKS): II. The radial structure of debris discs (2026; debris_disks, exoplanets; large_program_or_survey)
- question: What is the radial structure (rings, gaps, halos, fractional widths, edge slopes) of the 24 debris discs in the ARKS programme, and what does it imply for planets and for the link to protoplanetary discs
- step 2: Non-parametric deprojected and deconvolved profile in visibility space (frank)
  - method: 1D Fourier-Bessel series fit to the real visibilities regularised by a Gaussian process, fitted in log surface brightness [2.2 Non-parametric modelling: frank: "recovers the deprojected and deconvolved radial profile of discs from ALMA visibilities under the assumption of axisymmetry by modelling the real component of the visibilities in 1D with a Fourier-Bessel series regularised by a fast Gaussian process"]
  - software: frank, arksia
  - parameter: alpha sets the minimum S/N down to which visibilities are fit [2.2 Non-parametric modelling: frank: "sets the minimum signal-to-noise ratio (S/N) down to which"]
  - parameter: w_smooth sets smoothing of the reconstructed power spectrum [2.2 Non-parametric modelling: frank: "$w_\text{smooth}$ determines the amount of smoothing applied to the reconstructed power spectrum."]
  - parameter: Number of radial points N and outer radius R_out chosen per source; final per-target values (alpha, w_smooth, N, r_out) tabulated [2.2 Non-parametric modelling: frank: "were chosen for each source to ensure the full radial extent of disc emission was modelled over a dense grid"] [2.2 Non-parametric modelling: frank: "summarises the most appropriate hyperparameters selected to model each disc"]
  - parameter: Stellar flux subtracted from real visibilities as a constant offset (none subtracted for HD 109573; SED flux for HD 32297) [2.2 Non-parametric modelling: frank: "were subtracted from the real component of the visibilities as a constant offset in amplitude"] [2.2 Non-parametric modelling: frank: "so we conservatively did not subtract any stellar flux before fitting the radial profile"]
  - parameter: PA, inclination and centring offset fixed to ARKS paper I values [2.2 Non-parametric modelling: frank: "The position angle, inclination and centering offset of the discs were assumed to be those fitted in (cite: Marino2025) for consistency."]
  - assumption: Azimuthal symmetry and low optical depth [2 Extracting radial profiles: "Each of these methods enables radial profile recovery under the assumption of azimuthal symmetry, even if the disc is perfectly edge-on"]
  - assumption: Thin disc (h = 0) for the fits [2.2 Non-parametric modelling: frank: "Considering that the reduction of oscillatory artefacts and avoidance of negative surface brightness values outweighs the potential inaccuracies of performing the fits under the assumption that the disc is thin"]
  - fitting: Gaussian-process regularised Fourier-Bessel fit; hyperparameters chosen by inspection rather than by a sampler [2.2 Non-parametric modelling: frank: "the radial brightness profile should capture structural detail in the disc without introducing obvious oscillatory artefacts (a sign of over-fitting)"]
  - validation: Reconstructed power spectrum must reproduce the observed one without over-fitting undersampled baselines [2.2 Non-parametric modelling: frank: "the reconstructed power spectrum should reasonably reproduce that from the observations without over-fitting to baselines that are undersampled and carry high uncertainty"]
- step 3: Non-parametric deprojected and deconvolved profile in image space (rave)
  - method: Fit concentric annuli to the CLEAN image, solving for each annulus brightness [2.3 Non-parametric modelling: rave: "recovers the radial profile of a disc by fitting models of concentric annuli to its image, solving for the brightness required of each annulus to reproduce the image"]
  - software: rave
  - parameter: Fit repeated 100 times with Monte Carlo annuli boundary partitions [2.3 Non-parametric modelling: rave: "the fit is repeated 100 times, each time with a different partitioning of annuli boundaries, which are chosen in a Monte Carlo fashion"]
  - parameter: Number of annuli N is the only hyperparameter; largest value without significant oscillations [2.3 Non-parametric modelling: rave: "choosing the maximum value of $N$ that does not generate significant oscillatory artefacts in the fitted profile, beyond which the uncertainties on the radial profile increase"]
  - parameter: Edge-on rave version for HD 39060, HD 197481 and HD 32297, non-edge-on version for the rest [2.3 Non-parametric modelling: rave: "Following the approach in (cite: Han2025), we applied the edge-on version to the only three highly inclined discs in the sample that do not have a resolved central cavity"]
  - parameter: Stellar flux from ARKS paper I if well constrained, otherwise SED flux [2.3 Non-parametric modelling: rave: "The stellar flux values were assumed to be those fitted in (cite: Marino2025) if they were well-constrained, or the SED-fitted stellar flux otherwise."]
  - assumption: Scale height set to the best-fit aspect ratio derived with rave [2.3 Non-parametric modelling: rave: "profiles presented here were fitted using the best-fit scale height aspect ratio derived with"]
  - fitting: Uncertainties from scatter among the 100 Monte Carlo annuli partitions [2.3 Non-parametric modelling: rave: "The degree of scatter between the radial profiles fitted in each iteration was used to estimate the uncertainties on the radial profile"]
  - validation: Fitted both rave versions to all discs and kept the more appropriate one; low sensitivity to CLEAN robust parameter [2.3 Non-parametric modelling: rave: "Inspection of all discs fitted with both methods confirms that this choice of method produced lower uncertainties and oscillatory artefacts"] [2.3 Non-parametric modelling: rave: "is found to be low for reasonable robust parameter values"]
- limitation (authors): frank does not account for the primary beam, which could bias profiles of large discs [2.2 Non-parametric modelling: frank: "which could bias the profiles of large discs"]
- limitation (authors): frank uncertainties are likely underestimated [2.2 Non-parametric modelling: frank: "which is likely an underestimate of the true uncertainties"]
- limitation (authors): Planet masses from gaps ignore gap depth [4.3 Planet constraints from gaps: "these mass estimates are not tested for compatibility of the depth of the gap and are based solely on its width"]
- not stated (card): ALMA calibration details, CASA version and pipeline/manual choice for every project (deferred to ARKS paper I); Visibility averaging, weighting and flagging applied before frank and parametric fits; Versions of frank, rave, arksia, emcee, Galario and the parametric code; MCMC settings: number of walkers, steps, burn-in, convergence test and the priors on every parameter; Per-target fixed paramete…

### 2026ApJ...999...32W: Constraining Properties of Dust Formed in Wolf─Rayet Binary WR 112 Using Mid-infrared and Millimeter Observations (2026; evolved_stars, stellar_physics, astrochemistry, milky_way_ism; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the properties, particularly the grain size distribution and composition, of circumstellar dust formed in the carbon-rich Wolf-Rayet binary WR 112?
- step 3: Extract spatially resolved dust SEDs for 23 extended dust arc apertures
  - method: Define 23 annulus-sector apertures in two clean azimuthal sectors (13 in Sector I, 10 in Sector II) by locating radial profile troughs (cavities) in 21 um JWST image, mask 3-sigma background sources, integrate JWST fluxes, and compute ALMA… [Dust SED for the extended dust arcs: "The sectors are defined by position angles: $216^{\circ} \leq \mathrm{PA} \leq 256^{\circ}$ for the first and $94^{\circ} \leq \mathrm{PA} \leq 134^{\circ}$ for the second."] [Dust SED for the extended dust arcs: "The masks were computed by repeatedly applying the $3\sigma$ level mask estimated from `photutils.background`."]
  - software: photutils.background
  - parameter: Sector I: 216 deg <= PA <= 256 deg (13 apertures); Sector II: 94 deg <= PA <= 134 deg (10 apertures); 20% uncertainty adopted for JWST fluxes [Dust SED for the extended dust arcs: "There are 13 apertures in the first sector and 10 apertures in the second."] [Dust SED for the extended dust arcs: "To account for these uncertainties conservatively, we assume a $20 \%$ uncertainty for every JWST photometric data point."]
  - assumption: Deprojected distance estimated as sky-plane distance at PA = 216 deg for Sector I and PA = 134 deg for Sector II [Dust SED for the extended dust arcs: "The geometric model of WR 112 suggests the deprojected distance from the star to each aperture can be estimated as the distance (to the midpoint of the aperture along the radial direction) in the plane of sky at $\mathrm{PA} = 216^{\circ}$"]
  - fitting: Linear interpolation of cavity locations across position angles [Dust SED for the extended dust arcs: "The locations of the cavities at other position angles are computed by interpolating the predicted locations of the cavities at the three selected position angles."]
  - validation: Verified that read/Poisson noise and background/masking uncertainties are <1%. [Dust SED for the extended dust arcs: "The uncertainties of the JWST photometric points due to read and Poisson noise of the instrument are estimated to be much less than $1\%$. The uncertainties caused by background subtraction and masking of background stars is also found to be less than $1\%$."]
- step 4: Fit spatially resolved dust SEDs with free dust temperatures and a single grain radius
  - method: Perform a joint MCMC fit of modified blackbody emission across all 23 apertures treating each aperture temperature as a free parameter and sharing effective solid angles and a single grain radius. [Temperatures as Free Parameters: "To constrain the parameters that are shared across apertures in a robust way, we performed a joint Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) fit to the SEDs from the apertures using the `emcee` package (cite: ForemanMackey2013)."]
  - software: emcee, Mie scattering code (Dullemond 2012)
  - parameter: 26 free parameters: Omega_eff^I, Omega_eff^II, grain radius a (-9.0 <= log[a/m] <= -4.0), and 23 temperatures T_d^(1)...T_d^(23) [Temperatures as Free Parameters: "There are 26 free parameters in total, including three parameters shared over apertures $\Omega_{\text{eff}}^{\text{I}}, \Omega_{\text{eff}}^{\text{II}}, a$ as well as temperatures that are specific to each aperture, $T_d^{(1)}, \cdots ,T_d^{(23)}$."] [Temperatures as Free Parameters: "For the grain radius $a$, the bounds are chosen to be $-9.0 \leq \log [a/\textrm{meter}] \leq -4.0$, since it is unrealistic for grains to be smaller than 1 nanometer."]
  - assumption: Radiative equilibrium, optically thin uniformly expanding dust, ACAR-type amorphous carbon optical constants (Zubko 1996) [Temperatures as Free Parameters: "We adopted optical constants of ACAR-type amorphous carbon prepared by (cite: Colangeli1995) in laboratory and measured by (cite: Zubko1996)."] [Temperatures as Free Parameters: "Since the optically thin dust structure is expanding uniformly and the apertures are drawn between fixed position angles, the dust in one aperture will expand into the dust in the next aperture."]
  - fitting: MCMC with emcee; power-law fit T_d(r) = T_0 (r/r_0)^gamma to derived aperture temperatures [Temperatures as Free Parameters: "The best-fit power law for the temperature profile from all the apertures has power law index $\gamma = -0.384 \pm 0.021$."]
  - validation: Evaluated reduced chi^2 (chi_nu^2 = 5.28) and compared temperature power-law index with Marchenko (2002). [Temperatures as Free Parameters: "The $\chi^2$ for the median posterior model is 227.2. With 26 free parameters and 69 data points from 23 apertures, the reduced $\chi^2$ is $\chi_\nu^2 = 5.28$."]
- step 5: Fit spatially resolved dust SEDs using an equilibrium temperature grid and four grain size distributions
  - method: Compute radiative equilibrium temperature grid T_d(a, r) by balancing stellar photon absorption and thermal emission, then run MCMC fits to the 23 aperture SEDs for four grain size distribution parameterizations. [Equilibrium Temperature Grid: "$T_d(a,r)$ is the equilibrium temperature of dust grains derived numerically from Equation [\[eq:Tgrid\]](#eq:Tgrid) for every grain radius $a$ at every distance $r$ away from the stars."] [Equilibrium Temperature Grid: "We conducted MCMC fitting to the observed dust SEDs using the model-predicted temperatures with four parameterizations of the grain size distributions: a single grain radius, a (base-10) log-normal distribution, two grain radii (or two delta functions), and a continuous power law."]
  - software: emcee, Mie scattering code (Dullemond 2012)
  - parameter: Grain radius parameters sampled in log space between 1 nm and 100 um; 4 distributions (single radius a_e; log-normal a_c, sigma_log_a; two radii a_1, a_2, f_2; power law a_l, a_u, beta) [Equilibrium Temperature Grid: "All free parameters except $\sigma_{\log a}$ and $\beta$ are sampled from the $\log$ space during the MCMC fit. The sampling of all parameters related to the grain radius is restricted to the range 1 nm to 100 μm."]
  - assumption: Radiative equilibrium, no scattering, median stellar spectrum S_*(lambda), constant grain size distribution across outer apertures, ACAR optical constants [Equilibrium Temperature Grid: "$S_{\ast}(\lambda)$ is the spectral luminosity of the stellar photon flux, for which we adopt the median stellar spectrum of WR 112 and its companion derived in Section [3.2](#sec:stellarsed)"]
  - fitting: MCMC sampling with emcee [Equilibrium Temperature Grid: "We conducted MCMC fitting to the observed dust SEDs using the model-predicted temperatures with four parameterizations of the grain size distributions"]
  - validation: Compared equilibrium temperature power-law index (~-0.38 for a <= 30 nm) with free-temperature fit (-0.384) and compared reduced chi^2 across the four distributions. [Equilibrium Temperature Grid: "For $a \lesssim 3 \times 10^{-8}~\mathrm{m}$, the power law index is approximately $-0.38$, which agrees with similar computation using the optical constants by (cite: Williams2009). This value is also consistent with the $\gamma = -0.384$ derived in Section [3.3.2](#sec:Tfree)"]
- step 6: Fit spatially resolved dust SEDs accounting for stochastic heating of small grains
  - method: Compute grain temperature probability distributions P(T) on a 200-radius grid (1 nm to 10 um) for each aperture using DustEM, interpolate P(T) via quantile function linear interpolation, and fit the 23 aperture SEDs with MCMC for the four … [Stochastic heating: "In this work, we used `DustEM` (cite: Compigne2011) to compute the probability density $P(T;~a, S_{\ast}, r^{(i)}, \cdots)$, which is based on the formalism of (cite: Desert1986)."] [Stochastic heating: "To speed up computation, we computed the probability density of temperature $P(T;~a, S_{\ast}, r^{(i)}, \cdots)$ for each aperture and a grid of 200 grain radii $a$ between 1 nm and 10 μm."]
  - software: DustEM, emcee, Mie scattering code (Dullemond 2012)
  - parameter: Grid of 200 grain radii from 1 nm to 10 um; four grain size distributions (single radius, log-normal, two radii, power law) [Stochastic heating: "Using the probability density of temperatures and Equation [\[eq:stoch_heating2\]](#eq:stoch_heating2), we conducted MCMC fits to the observed SEDs. We adopted the four parameterizations of grain distributions used in Section [3.3.3](#sec:tempgrid): one radius, the base-10 log-normal, two radii and a power law."]
  - assumption: Graphite heat capacity (Draine 2001), dust bulk density rho = 1.81 g cm^-3, ACAR optical constants (also tested ACH2 and BE types) [Stochastic heating: "Thus, we adopted the heat capacity of graphite from (cite: Draine2001). We also set the density of dust to be $\rho = 1.81~\mathrm{g~cm^{-3}}$, which is in agreement with (cite: Compigne2011)."]
  - fitting: MCMC fitting with emcee [Stochastic heating: "Using the probability density of temperatures and Equation [\[eq:stoch_heating2\]](#eq:stoch_heating2), we conducted MCMC fits to the observed SEDs."]
  - validation: Tested robustness across three amorphous carbon types (ACAR, ACH2, BE), 16th and 84th percentile stellar spectra, and time-varying power-law indices per aperture. [Stochastic heating: "In addition to the ACAR-type grains adopted, we also conducted the fits for the two other laboratory analogues of amorphous carbon grain prepared by (cite: Colangeli1995) and measured by (cite: Zubko1996), the ACH2-type and the BE-type"] [Robustness and sources of errors: "To assess the robustness of the inferred grain size distribution against variations in the stellar spectrum, we performed MCMC fits using the 16th- and 84th-percentile stellar spectra among the acceptable models."]
- step 7: Model the spatially unresolved infrared SED to constrain inner dust properties
  - method: Approximate circumstellar dust as continuous concentric spherical shells from r_in to R = 90,000 au, fix outer shell (r > r_s) to the resolved bimodal distribution, and fit three inner-region models (Models A, B, C) to unresolved archival … [Dust SED at the location of the star: "Given the overall spatial uniformity of the circumstellar dust around WR 112, we can approximate the dust structure to be continuous concentric spherical shells."] [Dust SED at the location of the star: "We conducted MCMC fits of the three models to the unresolved SED, and the results are summarized in table [\[tbl:grain_stellar_sed\]](#tbl:grain_stellar_sed)."]
  - software: DustEM, emcee
  - parameter: Outer cutoff R = 90,000 au; outer bimodal parameters fixed to a_1 = 1.247 nm, a_2 = 0.134 um, f_2 = 2.85e-5; Model A (free r_in), Model B (free r_in, r_s, f_in), Model C (free r_in, r_s, a_3, n_3/n_1) [Dust SED at the location of the star: "We hence set outer radius cutoff for shells to be $R=90{,}000$ au."] [Dust SED at the location of the star: "the outer dust structures most likely have two populations of grains, one with radius $a_1 =$ 1.247 nm, and another with radius $a_2 =$ 0.134 μm and is $f_2 = 2.85 \times 10^{-5}$ less abundant than the smaller ones."]
  - assumption: Spherical shell number density equals 9 times the number of dust in a 40-deg aperture of the same radial width [Dust SED at the location of the star: "We can estimate $n(a_1)$ using the effective solid angles, by ignoring projection effects and equating the total number of dust in a spherical shell to the number of dust in a projected ring, which is 9 times the number of dust in a $40^{\circ}$ aperture with the same width."]
  - fitting: MCMC fitting with emcee [Dust SED at the location of the star: "We conducted MCMC fits of the three models to the unresolved SED, and the results are summarized in table [\[tbl:grain_stellar_sed\]](#tbl:grain_stellar_sed)."]
  - validation: Compared Model A, B, and C predictions against near-, mid-, and far-infrared unresolved data and ALMA 1.3 mm flux. [Dust SED at the location of the star: "The figure shows that all three models are able to reproduce the near-infrared and mid-infrared data relatively well, and they all underestimate the emissions in the far-infrared, which leads to the relatively large $\chi^2$."]
- result: Dust temperature profile power-law index (gamma, free-T model) = -0.384 ± +-0.021 dimensionless [Temperatures as Free Parameters: "The best-fit power law for the temperature profile from all the apertures has power law index $\gamma = -0.384 \pm 0.021$."]
- result: Small grain radius (a_1, bimodal stochastic heating ACAR model) = 1.247 ± +0.247/-0.159 nm [Stochastic heating: "| $a_1 = 1.247^{+0.247}_{-0.159}~\mathrm{nm}$ | | |"]
- result: Large grain radius (a_2, bimodal stochastic heating ACAR model) = 0.134 ± +0.084/-0.026 um [Stochastic heating: "| $a_2 = 0.134^{+0.084}_{-0.026}$ μm | | |"]
- result: Relative abundance of large to small grains (f_2, bimodal stochastic heating ACAR model) = 2.85e-5 ± +1.27e-5/-0.82e-5 dimensionless [Stochastic heating: "| $f_2 = 2.85^{+1.27}_{-0.82} \times 10^{-5}$ | | |"]
- result: Power-law grain size distribution index (beta, stochastic heating ACAR model) = -3.52 ± +0.08/-0.08 dimensionless [Stochastic heating: "| $\beta = -3.52^{+0.08}_{-0.08}$ | | |"]
- result: Inner dust grain radius (a_3, unresolved SED Model C) = 0.192 ± +0.038/-0.044 um [Dust SED at the location of the star: "| $a_3=0.192^{+0.038}_{-0.044}$ μm | | | |"]
- limitation (authors): Simplified parameterizations of the grain size distribution due to having only three resolved mid-IR filters and one mm upper limit per aperture. [Conclusions: "Our parameterizations of the grain size distribution are necessarily simplified, and additional photometric data points would allow more complex distributions to be tested."]
- limitation (authors): JWST saturation at r < 25,000 au prevents directly resolving whether the grain size distribution evolves shortly after formation. [Conclusions: "The saturation of JWST at $r < 25{,}000$ au also limits our ability to confirm with certainty whether the grain size distribution evolves shortly after dust formation."]
- limitation (authors): Degeneracy between grain chemistry (ACAR, ACH2, BE) and grain size prevents determining the hydrogen content of carbon grains from continuum SED fitting alone. [Types of carbon grains: "With the grain size distribution largely unknown, models of the continuum emissions from dust cannot place constraints on the hydrogen content of the grains."]

### 2025MNRAS.543..862V: Low-frequency spectra of neutron star + OB supergiant binaries: Does wind density drive persistent and flaring modes of accretion? (2025; stellar_physics, other; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What dominates the radio/millimeter emission of neutron star + OB supergiant binaries (stellar wind vs jet), and does wind density drive the SgXB (persistent) vs SFXT (flaring) dichotomy?
- step 6: Fit power-law SEDs
  - method: MCMC fit of S = xi S0 (nu/nu0)^alpha; three versions (ALMA full bands, ALMA sub-bands, ALMA+VLA); 4U 1907+09 uses ALMA 40+150 GHz and NOEMA 100 GHz [Low-frequency SED fitting: "We attempt to fit the low-frequency millimeter or radio + millimeter SEDs of our targets with a power law model $S_\nu = \xi S_0 (\nu / \nu_0)^{\alpha}$."] [Low-frequency SED fitting: "We perform three versions of the SED fit: firstly, we fit the ALMA SEDs using the three full ALMA bands, followed by a fit to the ALMA SEDs using the three times four sub-bands as a consistency check."]
  - software: emcee (cited in acknowledgements)
  - parameter: S0 = 1 mJy, nu0 = 121.6 GHz (log-mean of the three ALMA centroid frequencies) [Low-frequency SED fitting: "we set $S_0 = 1$ mJy and $\nu_0 = 121.6$ GHz, equal to the mean of the three ALMA centroid frequencies in log space"]
  - parameter: Parameter estimates reported as 16/84th percentile intervals [Low-frequency SED fitting: "we report the resulting parameter estimates and their $16/84^{\rm th}$ percentile confidence intervals"]
  - assumption: Single power law; 4U 1700-37 10 GHz tentative point (207 +/- 87 microJy) shown but excluded from fits; NOEMA two-sub-band indices not used [Radio observations: "but do not use in further SED fitting or interpreting the broad-band spectrum"]
  - fitting: MCMC with flat priors on alpha and log xi; models above a 3 sigma upper limit get zero probability (-inf log probability). Sampler name not stated for the fit; EMCEE appears in the acknowledgements [Low-frequency SED fitting: "We assume flat priors on the spectral index $\alpha$ and on the logarithm of the normalization parameter, $\log \xi$."] [Low-frequency SED fitting: "We take non-detections into account by assigning a zero probability (or, in the fitting routine, a minus infinity log probability) to any model that predicts a flux above a $3\sigma$ upper limit."]
  - validation: Sub-band fit as consistency check; fits with and without VLA compared [Low-frequency SED fitting: "using the three times four sub-bands as a consistency check"]
- step 8: Compare SFXT and SgXB millimeter luminosities and Fe K alpha EW
  - method: Rescale to 100 GHz luminosity and plot vs orbital period and vs weighted-average Fe K alpha EW; add literature SgXBs by extrapolating ATCA radio data to 100 GHz [Stellar wind properties in SgXBs and SF…: "we show the re-scaled 100-GHz luminosity versus the orbital period of our targets in the left panel of Figure"] [Stellar wind properties in SgXBs and SF…: "we calculate a weighted average of the EW for our comparison"]
  - parameter: Observed spectral index used for rescaling, otherwise alpha = 0.6 [Stellar wind properties in SgXBs and SF…: "using the observed spectral index, or otherwise assuming $\alpha = 0.6$"]
  - assumption: Literature ATCA upper limits extrapolated to 100 GHz with alpha = 0.6; assumes low-frequency SED is wind dominated [Stellar wind properties in SgXBs and SF…: "we therefore take their 9-GHz $3\sigma$ upper limit and extrapolate to 100-GHz assuming a standard spectral index $\alpha = 0.6$"] [Stellar wind properties in SgXBs and SF…: "We stress that these extrapolations, and comparison with our millimeter-observed sample, implicitly assume their low-frequency SED is stellar-wind dominated."]
- result: Spectral index range for accurately fitted systems = 0.6-0.8 ± not stated dimensionless [abstract: "with spectral indices in the range $\alpha =0.6-0.8$ for those systems where accurate SED fits could be performed"]
- result: alpha of 4U 1700-37 (ALMA full bands; with VLA) = 0.67; 0.70 ± 0.01; 0.01 dimensionless [Table 1: "$0.67\pm0.01$"]
- result: alpha of Vela X-1 (ALMA full bands; with VLA) = 0.78; 0.80 ± 0.02; 0.02 dimensionless [Variability in low-frequency stellar wi…: "than the millimeter spectrum ($\alpha = 0.78 \pm 0.02$)"]
- result: alpha of 4U 1907+09 (ALMA 40+150 GHz and NOEMA 100 GHz) = 1.0 ± +0.9/-0.7 dimensionless [Low-frequency SED fitting: "4U 1907+09: $\alpha = 1.0^{+0.9}_{-0.7}$, from the millimeter data only"]
- result: alpha of IGR J17544-2619 (ALMA full bands) = 0.70 ± +0.10/-0.07 dimensionless [Table 1: "$0.70^{+0.10}_{-0.07}$"]
- result: alpha of IGR J18410-0535 (ALMA full bands) = 1.2 ± +0.6/-0.5 dimensionless [Table 1: "$1.2^{+0.6}_{-0.5}$"]
- result: alpha of SAX J1818.6-1703 (ALMA full bands) = 0.67 ± 0.05 dimensionless [Table 1: "$0.67\pm0.05$"]
- result: alpha from the two NOEMA sub-bands: 2S 0114+650 / IGR J19140+0951 / IGR J18483-0311 = -1.2 / -1.0 / -0.6 ± 2.1 / 0.7 / 0.9 dimensionless [Low-frequency SED fitting: "we find values of $\alpha = -1.2\pm2.1$, $\alpha = -1.0\pm0.7$, and $\alpha = -0.6\pm0.9$ for 2S 0114+650, IGR J19140+0951, and IGR J18483-0311, respectively"]
- result: Density-profile index p implied by alpha = 0.6-0.8 = 2-2.25 ± not stated dimensionless [The origin of radio-millimeter emission: "the observed range of $\alpha = 0.6-0.8$ implies $p=2-2.25$"]
- result: 100 GHz luminosity range of the four SFXTs = 6e28 to 2e29 ± not stated erg/s [Stellar wind properties in SgXBs and SF…: "between $6\times10^{28}$ erg/s and $2\times10^{29}$ erg/s"]
- result: 100 GHz luminosity range of the six persistently accreting systems = <4.6e28 to 2.4e30 ± not stated erg/s [Stellar wind properties in SgXBs and SF…: "between $<4.6\times10^{28}$ erg/s and $2.4\times10^{30}$ erg/s"]
- limitation (authors): Jet-vs-wind estimate is order-of-magnitude only [The origin of radio-millimeter emission: "We emphasize that each of these steps in the calculation of the jet luminosity comes with statistical and systematic uncertainties, and therefore this comparison should be interpreted only at the order of magnitude level."]
- limitation (authors): Extrapolated millimeter limits for non-detected SgXBs are not constraining relative to the SFXT range [Stellar wind properties in SgXBs and SF…: "the (extrapolated) millimeter upper limits for non-detected SgXBs are not constraining, currently, compared to the SFXT luminosity range"]

### 2015ApJ...801...82H: ALMA Imaging of Millimeter/Submillimeter Continuum Emission in Orion KL (2015; star_formation; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the compact continuum sources in Orion KL at ALMA bands 6 and 7, what are their dust graybody properties, and what does the centimeter-to-submillimeter SED of Source I imply for its emission mechanism (H- free-free plus dust)?

  - method: Fit 245, 339 GHz (and 90 GHz when available) peak intensities to optically thin graybody with beta free [5.2 Graybody: "Thus we fit the observed flux densities at 245 GHz, and 339 GHz as listed in Table [4](#tab-cont) to the graybody function;"]
  - parameter: Tdust fixed to 100 K or 20 K [5.2 Graybody: "we assume the temperature of 100 K as obtained from the molecular line observations (cite: favre2011) and lower value of 20 K (cite: eisner2008) for comparison."]
  - assumption: kappa=0.1 cm2/g at 250 um, gas-to-dust 100, D=420 pc [5.2 Graybody: "assuming the gas to dust mass ratio of 100."]

- result: Source I graybody mass (100 K) = 0.082 ± 0.049 Msun [Table 7: "| power law$+$graybody | -1.51(30) | 1.60(24) | 0.082(49) | 1.34(83) |"]
- result: Masses of compact sources at 20 K = 0.08-0.27 ± not stated Msun [5.2 Graybody: "The derived masses (0.08$M_{\odot}$-0.27$M_{\odot}$ for assumed temperature of 20 K)"]

- question: Constrain the disc dust grain size and composition using aperture polarimetry and continuum emission.

  - method: Generate individual material SEDs with Hyperion, fixed disc geometry and grain distribution; include a scaled NEXTGEN photosphere. [Spectral energy distribution: "We use the 3D Monte Carlo radiative transfer code Hyperion (cite: 2011Robitaille) to model the dust continuum emission."] [Spectral energy distribution: "Using these extrapolated optical constants we run individual disc models through Hyperion, assuming the previously determined disc spatial extent and dust size distribution for all materials"]
  - software: Hyperion
