  - parameter: amin = 0.01 μm; amax = 1 mm; q = 3.95. [Spectral energy distribution: "We fix the minimum and maximum grain sizes for the dust as $a_{\rm min} = 0.01~\mu$m and $a_{\rm max} = 1~$mm."] [Spectral energy distribution: "The exponent of the size distribution $q$ was also fixed to be 3.95."]
  - parameter: Extrapolate optical constants to 0.1 μm–2 mm using the first or last two points. [Spectral energy distribution: "extrapolation down to 0.1 $\mu$m and up to 2 mm, using the linear slope of the first (or last) two data points for the measured optical constants to extend the range."]
  - parameter: Reference mass for each material SED: 3.45×10^23 g. [Spectral energy distribution: "each individual SED represented emission from the same mass of dust, in this case 3.45$\times10^{23}$ g."]
  - parameter: NEXTGEN: 7800 K; log(g) = 4.2 interpolated from 4.0 and 4.5; Solar metallicity; luminosity 8.093 L☉. [Spectral energy distribution: "an effective temperature of 7800 K, surface gravity $\log (g)$ of 4.2 (interpolated from $\log (g)$ 4.0 and 4.5), and Solar metallicity"] [Spectral energy distribution: "The photosphere model was scaled to provide a total luminosity of 8.093 $L_{\odot}$."]
  - assumption: The same dust grains produce continuum emission, scattering and polarisation. [Spectral energy distribution: "We have therefore tacitly assumed that the same dust grains that produce the dust continuum emission are responsible for the scattering and polarisation."]
  - assumption: Hard compact spherical grains. [Spectral energy distribution: "an appropriate material density assuming the grains are hard compact spheres"]
- step 5: Fit the dust composition
  - method: Fit a weighted combination of 11 reference material SEDs to photometry and mid-infrared spectral samples. [Table 6: "Summary of the material weights determined by fitting of a composite SED of 11 materials to the SED and mid-infrared spectrum."]
  - software: emcee
  - parameter: 14 IRS samples evenly distributed between 6 and 34 μm. [Spectral energy distribution: "taking 14 values evenly distributed between 6 and 34 $\mu$m."]
  - parameter: Weights 10^-3–10^3; initial log weights 0 with walker scatter 0.05. [Spectral energy distribution: "assigning each individual SED a weighting factor between 10$^{-3}$ and 10$^{3}$ (i.e. between -3 and 3 in log space)"] [Spectral energy distribution: "The weighting factors for each species were initialised at 1 (0 in log space), with the individual walkers given a small scatter of 0.05 about this value."]
  - parameter: 200 walkers; 1 000 steps; discard first 400 steps; report 16th, 50th and 84th percentiles. [Spectral energy distribution: "We used 200 walkers and 1 000 steps (200 000 realisations) to deduce the maximum probability weightings for the materials"] [Spectral energy distribution: "discarding the first 400 steps of each chain before calculating the probability distribution to extract the 16$^{\rm th}$, 50$^{\rm th}$, and 84$^{\rm th}$ percentile values."]
  - assumption: Fixed spatial extent and dust size distribution; composition is fitted. [Spectral energy distribution: "given that the disc spatial extent and dust size distribution are fixed, the only constraint left is to determine the dust composition that best replicates the SED."]
  - fitting: Affine-invariate MCMC with emcee; least squares for the weighted sum of component SEDs. [Spectral energy distribution: "we use the affine-invariate Markov Chain Monte Carlo code *emcee* (cite: 2013ForemanMackey)"] [Spectral energy distribution: "calculating the least squares fit of the weighted combination of individual SEDs to the observations."]
  - validation: Model agrees with the mid-infrared spectrum and far-infrared photometry and remains below the ALMA upper limit. [Spectral energy distribution: "The model is consistent, within uncertainties, with the mid-infrared spectrum across its full extent, reproduces the far-infrared photometry, and falls below the millimetre-wavelength upper limit."]
- step 6: Fit the polarising grain size
  - method: Calculate Mie albedo and Müller matrices, generate 3D disc polarisation spectra and bandpass synthetic measurements, then fit minimum grain size. [Polarimetry: "The albedo and scattering Müller matrix for each composition was calculated using a parallelized Fortran95 implementation of BHMie"] [Polarimetry: "We calculate the disc polarisation using a 3D model of the disc density distribution combined with the dust optical constants, following the model presented in (cite: 2015Scicluna)."]
  - software: BHMie
  - parameter: 200 wavelengths over 0.3–0.9 μm; minimum sizes 0.01–6.5 μm. [Polarimetry: "We proceed by generating model polarisation spectra for each dust composition spanning 0.3 to 0.9 $\mu$m, sampling 200 wavelengths within this range, for minimum dust grains sizes between 0.01 and 6.5 $\mu$m."]
  - parameter: Maximum size 1 mm; size-distribution exponent -3.95. [Polarimetry: "We fixed the maximum grain size as 1 mm, with a power law size distribution exponent of -3.95, as per the radiative transfer modelling."]
  - parameter: Compare the SED-derived mixture with astronomical silicate. [Polarimetry: "We calculate a single model polarisation spectrum for each grain size using the weightings calculated in the dust grain radiative transfer modelling."] [Polarimetry: "We also calculate polarisation spectra for astronomical silicate dust grains (cite: 2003Draine) as a point of comparison to the dust composition determined from this work."]
  - assumption: Single scattering of unpolarised starlight in an optically thin disc. [Polarimetry: "We assume the measured polarisation is produced in single scattering events for dust grains in the optically thin disc redirecting unpolarised starlight toward the observer."]
  - assumption: Orient the model so U is zero; retain grains between 0.1 and 1.0 μm. [Polarimetry: "The orientation of the disc in the model is chosen such that the degree of polarisation in the U vector is zero by default."] [Polarimetry: "We did not omit grains with sizes 0.1 to 1.0 $\mu$m from the scattering polarisation calculation despite these grains being swiftly removed by radiation forces."]
  - fitting: Least-squares fit of each grain-size model spectrum to multi-wavelength polarimetry. [Polarimetry: "The best-fit grain size was then determined using a least-squares fit of the model polarisation spectrum for each grain size to the multi-wavelength polarimetry."]
  - validation: Assess agreement within uncertainties; the shortest-wavelength rise is not reproduced. [Polarimetry: "The model polarisation spectra do not replicate the steep rise in polarimetric signal toward shorter wavelengths"] [Polarimetry: "are consistent within the substantial uncertainties attached to the magnitude and shape of the model polarisation spectra at those shortest wavelengths."]
- result: Dust mass in grains up to 1 mm = 1.10×10^-3 ± not stated M⊕ [Table 6: "Total dust mass of the disc (in grains up to 1 mm) is inferred to be 1.10$\times10^{-3}~M_{\oplus}$, or 7.19$\times10^{21}$ kg."]
- result: Best-fit minimum polarising grain size, SED-derived composition = 3.89 ± not stated μm [Polarimetry: "The best-fit polarisation model using this dust composition has a minimum grain size $a_{\rm min} = 3.89~\mu$m"]
- result: Best-fit minimum polarising grain size, astronomical silicate = 1.25 ± not stated μm [Polarimetry: "With the astronomical silicate models, we find a best-fit minimum grain size $a_{\rm min} = 1.25~\mu$m from a least squares fit between the grain size dependent polarisation spectra and the observations."]
- limitation (authors): Patchy ISM prevents control-star polarisation versus distance alone from determining the target's interstellar component. [Polarisation vs. distance: "the ISM in this region is patchy which prevents firm conclusions being drawn on the interstellar polarisation of HD 172555 from this data alone."]
- limitation (authors): A composite 425SP observation may have underestimated error. [Interstellar subtraction: "one of the 425SP observations is a composite of two measurements, and the associated error may be under-estimated."]
- limitation (authors): Unavailable optical constants prevent a direct composition comparison with the earlier spectral fit. [Spectral energy distribution: "Not all the materials utilized in the spectrum fitting presented in table 2 of (cite: 2009Lisse) had ($n$,$k$) values available through accessible online databases."]
- not stated (card): Exact JVO ALMA image product identifier and whether the image used was primary-beam corrected.; ALMA image beam, rms measurement region and statistical multiplier used to obtain the <0.240 mJy upper limit.; Treatment of the ALMA upper limit in the SED fitting objective.; Exact Jena optical-constant files, material densities and component optical-constant mixing procedure.; Hyperion grid, photon c…

### 2026MNRAS.549ag678P: AT 2024wpp: an extremely luminous fast ultraviolet transient powered by accretion onto a black hole (2026; transients, stellar_physics; multi_facility_alma_minor)
- question: What are the multi-wavelength observational properties, energy source, and progenitor system of the extremely luminous fast ultraviolet transient AT 2024wpp?
- step 2: Model radio/millimeter SEDs and derive shock and CSM properties
  - method: Fit a soft broken power-law to radio+ALMA SEDs at six epochs (30, 41, 66, 103, 119, 177 rest-frame days) to find peak flux and peak frequency, then infer shock radius, magnetic field, total energy, shock speed, and ambient electron density… [Radio evolution: "To infer the properties of the forward shock and CSM, we model the radio emission from AT 2024wpp using the traditional synchrotron self-absorption (SSA) framework (cite: Chevalier+1998; Soderberg+2005)."] [Radio evolution: "We first perform initial fits of a soft broken power-law ($F_\nu = F_{\nu,{\rm peak}}(({\nu/\nu_{\rm peak})^{-s\beta_1}+(\nu/\nu_{\rm peak})^{-s\beta_2}})^{-1/s}$) to the sub-band gridded radio data at 41 and 66 d (the only two epochs where the peak is continuously sampled) to estimate values of the spectral index bel…"]
  - parameter: beta_1 = +1.17, beta_2 = -0.90, s = 1.5 [Radio evolution: "these are found to be $\beta_1 = +$1.17, $\beta_2 = -$0.90, and $s=$ 1.5, respectively."]
  - parameter: Factor of 1.37 correction applied to 27d X and Ku measurements in first epoch SED [Radio evolution: "We apply a correction factor of 1.37 (consistent with a source rising as $F \sim t^{+2.5}$ between 27 and 31 days) to the X and Ku measurements, which removes the discontinuity"]
  - assumption: Nonthermal power-law electron energy distribution with p = 3, volume filling factor f = 0.5, and equipartition epsilon_B = epsilon_e = 1/3 [Radio evolution: "We assume an electron energy power-law index of $p=3$, a volume filling factor of $f=0.5$, and equipartition such that the magnetic energy density ($\epsilon_B$) and energy density of electrons ($\epsilon_e$) contribute equally to the post shock energy ($\epsilon_B$=$\epsilon_e$=1/3)."]
  - assumption: 10% overall flux calibration uncertainty and interstellar scintillation modulations included [Radio evolution: "Flux modulations induced by interstellar scintillation (see following text) were included in the uncertainty analysis, as was an overall 10% flux calibration uncertainty component applied uniformly to all bands."]
  - fitting: Soft broken power-law fit with uncertainties (95% confidence) determined from a Monte Carlo procedure [Radio evolution: "Uncertainties (95% confidence) on each parameter are determined from a Monte Carlo procedure."]
  - validation: Compared low-frequency SED deviations against interstellar scintillation predictions from Walker 1998 and Walker 2001 maps [Radio evolution: "From the equations in (cite: Walker+1998), and assuming a source expanding radially at 0.2$c$, large ($m>0.3$) scintillation effects are expected below 13 GHz at 30 d, below 11 GHz at 41d, and below 6 GHz at 119 d (the expected modulation range is shown as a light grey envelope in Figure 16), in agreement with the dev…"]
- step 3: Construct UVOIR light curves and fit blackbody evolution
  - method: Perform image subtraction and forced/aperture/PSF photometry across UVOIR facilities, apply empirical filter offsets and Galactic extinction correction, interpolate light curves using polynomial fits and local regression, and fit Planck fu… [Blackbody Evolution: "We interpolate the photometry shown in Figure 2 using a combination of direct polynomial fits and local regression, and sample the resulting model light curves at a list of selected times that roughly match the mean times of the Swift/UVOT epochs (at early times) or the HST and VLT multi-band observations (at late tim…"] [Blackbody Evolution: "These are then fit to a Planck function at each epoch."]
  - software: HEAsoft, uvotsource, uvotimsum, SWARP, sep, SExtractor, ASTROMETRY.NET, SFFT, mirar, EsoReflex, AstroDrizzle, ccdproc
  - parameter: t_0 = MJD 60578.40 [UV/Optical Light Curve: "The time of first light is taken to be MJD 60578.40 based on a power-law fit to the early $g$-band data, shown in the figure inset."]
  - parameter: 10% systematic error term included in SED fits; PS1 y-band after 16 rest-frame days excluded [Blackbody Evolution: "For all bands, we further include a 10% systematic error term to approximate the effect of calibration uncertainties, variation in the effective central wavelength, deviations from a perfect blackbody, and uncertainty in the interpolation model."] [Blackbody Evolution: "Pan-STARRS 1 $y$-band measurements after 16 rest-frame days were excluded from the fit due to the possibility of NIR excess affecting these measurements."]
  - assumption: Cosmology: Omega_M = 0.3, Omega_Lambda = 0.7, H_0 = 70 km s^-1 Mpc^-1; Galactic E(B-V) = 0.026 mag; zero host extinction [Introduction: "Throughout the paper we assume a cosmology with $\Omega_M=0.3$, $\Omega_\Lambda=0.7$, $H_0$ = 70 km s$^{-1}$ Mpc$^{-1}$ and estimate Galactic reddening via the dust maps of (cite: Schlafly+2011) and extinction curve of (cite: Fitzpatrick1999); for AT 2024wpp we use a value of $E_{B-V} = 0.026$ mag."] [Blackbody Evolution: "(We correct for Galactic extinction but do not correct for any host extinction, for the reasons described in the previous subsection.)"]
  - fitting: Planck function fit to interpolated multi-band fluxes at 16 epochs [Blackbody Evolution: "These are then fit to a Planck function at each epoch."]
  - validation: 10% systematic error chosen to achieve reduced chi-squared of approximately 1 on average across all fits [Blackbody Evolution: "The value of 10% was was chosen to achieve $\hat{\chi}^2\approx1$ on average across all fits."]
- step 6: Derive host galaxy physical properties from SED and IFU spectroscopy
  - method: Fit UV-to-MIR host galaxy photometry with prospector (using FSPS and dynesty) after removing nebular line contributions, and fit Gaussian profiles to continuum-subtracted KCWI IFU emission lines to derive Balmer decrement, SFR, and gas-pha… [Host galaxy properties: "We use the python package prospector (cite: Leja+2017; Johnson+2021) to derive the host galaxy properties from photometry. This package models the galaxy’s SED using the Flexible Stellar Population Synthesis (FSPS; (cite: Conroy+2010FSPS)) package, and fits the model to the observations with dynesty."] [Host galaxy properties: "To measure fluxes of major strong emission lines, we first subtract the stellar continuum using an SED model from the photometric fit. We then fit a Gaussian profile at the rest wavelength of each corresponding line of interest."]
  - software: prospector, FSPS, dynesty
  - parameter: 7 arcsec radius aperture for VLT, NTT, and UVOT host photometry; 2x FWHM elliptical aperture for KCWI host spectrum [Host galaxy properties: "We additionally perform photometry of the host using our late-time VLT and NTT reference images and a 7$''$ radius aperture."] [Host galaxy properties: "We use the 2025 January 1st KCWI IFU observation ($\Delta t_{\rm rest}$ = 90 d) to extract the spectrum of the host over an elliptical aperture of radius set to 2$\times$ the FWHM of the galaxy along each axis."]
  - assumption: Chabrier IMF, Calzetti dust attenuation model, delayed-tau SFH (t * exp(-t/tau)), fixed stellar metallicity log(Z/Z_sun) = -0.6 from mass-metallicity relation [Host galaxy properties: "We choose the Chabrier initial mass function (cite: Chabrier2003), the Calzetti dust attenuation model (cite: Calzetti+2000), and a star-formation history of the form $t\,{\rm e}^{-t/\tau}$."] [Host galaxy properties: "After an initial fit, we estimate the stellar mass and fix the stellar metallicity to log($Z/Z_\odot$) = $-$0.6 according to the mass-metallicity relation (cite: Gallazzi+2005)."]
  - fitting: Nested sampling with dynesty in prospector for SED; Gaussian line fitting for IFU spectrum [Host galaxy properties: "This package models the galaxy’s SED using the Flexible Stellar Population Synthesis (FSPS; (cite: Conroy+2010FSPS)) package, and fits the model to the observations with dynesty."] [Host galaxy properties: "We then fit a Gaussian profile at the rest wavelength of each corresponding line of interest."]
  - validation: Compared gas-phase oxygen abundance from Curti+2017 diagnostics with expectations from the low-redshift mass-metallicity relation [Host galaxy properties: "We use a variety of line diagnostics to estimate the metallicity (using the recent calibrations of (cite: Curti+2017)) and find a (gas-phase) oxygen abundance of 12+log[O/H] in the range of 8.4–8.6 or a metallicity of about 0.5–0.8 Solar (cite: Asplund+2021), consistent with expectations given the stellar mass and the…"]
- result: Radio SED peak frequency at 30.5 rest-frame days = 59.4 ± +4.2/-4.3 GHz [Radio evolution: "| 30.5$^{+0.8}_{-3.5}$ | 59.4$^{+4.2}_{-4.3}$ | 1837$^{+130}_{-130}$ | 11.58$^{ +0.75}_{ -0.71}$ | 3.35$^{+0.23}_{-0.24}$ | 2.9$^{+0.3}_{-0.2}$ | 0.14$^{+0.01}_{-0.01}$ | 98300$^{+27800}_{-21300}$ |"]
- result: Forward shock velocity from 30.5 to 119.3 rest-frame days = 0.14 to 0.26 ± not stated c [Radio evolution: "| 30.5$^{+0.8}_{-3.5}$ | 59.4$^{+4.2}_{-4.3}$ | 1837$^{+130}_{-130}$ | 11.58$^{ +0.75}_{ -0.71}$ | 3.35$^{+0.23}_{-0.24}$ | 2.9$^{+0.3}_{-0.2}$ | 0.14$^{+0.01}_{-0.01}$ | 98300$^{+27800}_{-21300}$ |"]
- result: Peak bolometric luminosity log10(L) at 2.10 rest-frame days = 45.21 ± +0.10/-0.08 log10(erg s^-1) [Table 5: "| 60580.68 | 2.10 | $45.21^{+0.10}_{-0.08}$ | $15.00^{+0.03}_{-0.03}$ | $4.62^{+0.04}_{-0.03}$ |"]
- result: Maximum photospheric radius log10(R) at 5.21 rest-frame days = 15.14 ± +0.02/-0.02 log10(cm) [Table 5: "| 60584.05 | 5.21 | $45.08^{+0.05}_{-0.06}$ | $15.14^{+0.02}_{-0.02}$ | $4.52^{+0.03}_{-0.02}$ |"]
- result: Total radiated UVOIR energy = 1.0e51 ± not stated erg [Blackbody Evolution: "From integrating the bolometric light curve, we infer a total radiated energy of $1.0\times10^{51}\,$erg."]
- result: Host galaxy stellar mass log10(M_*) = 8.96 ± 0.06 log10(M_sun) [Table 8: "log$_{10}$$M_*$ | $M_\odot$ | 8.96 $\pm$ 0.06"]
- limitation (authors): Empirical constant magnitude offsets applied to align multi-facility optical light curves do not account for the time-evolving color of the transient and leave a systematic flux calibration uncertainty. [Ground-based follow-up Imaging: "It should be cautioned that this technique does not account for the time-evolving colour of the object, and because the choice of which instrument to treat as the baseline is arbitrary a systematic uncertainty in the flux calibration comparable to the characteristic offsets will remain in any resulting SED analysis."]
- limitation (authors): Early blackbody temperature and radius estimates are somewhat uncertain because at T > 30 kK the true blackbody peak lies in the unobserved extreme UV. [Blackbody Evolution: "While these measurements are somewhat uncertain due to the very high temperatures ($>30$kK, such that the true blackbody peak is in the extreme UV and thus poorly constrained), they are consistent with the notion that the photosphere is expanding at very high speeds of 0.1–0.2$c$ for a few days before rapidly transiti…"]
- limitation (authors): Host ISM UV absorption lines in the COS spectrum are saturated, preventing abundance determinations. [Ultraviolet Spectroscopic Analysis: "We fit the high-S/N host lines in the COS spectrum (Si, O, C, S) with a Gaussian absorption model convolved with the COS line-spread function and find that the line is consistent with being saturated in all cases, so it is not possible to derive abundances from these observations."]

### 2021ApJ...910...21R: A Broadband View on Microquasar MAXI J1820+070 during the 2018 Outburst (2021; transients, stellar_physics; multi_facility_alma_minor)
- question: Build a quasi-simultaneous radio-to-hard-X-ray SED of MAXI J1820+070 on 12 April 2018 and model it with a leptonic jet, irradiated disk and corona
- step 2: Build the jet SED and fit a broken power law
  - method: Combine JVLA, VLITE, ALMA, X-shooter continuum and OMC points; fit radio-to-optical data with a broken power law to get the synchrotron peak and the optically thick and thin slopes, and fit the 0.3-10 GHz radio SED with a power law [The compact jet emission: "We fitted the radio to optical data set with a broken-power law to identify the synchrotron peak frequency and the spectral slopes of the optically thin and thick regions"] [The compact jet emission: "For X-shooter, we considered only the part of the spectrum not affected by absorption/emission features, and averaged values for each of these intervals to obtain continuum flux density values."]
  - software: Astropy
  - parameter: Data points below 20 GHz excluded from the broken-power-law fit [The compact jet emission: "Similarly, we did not consider data points below 20 GHz, since they show a different slope, probably belonging to a physically distinct (and more expanded) radio jet component."]
  - fitting: Levenberg-Marquardt least squares (Astropy BrokenPowerLaw1D with LevMarLSQFitter) [The compact jet emission: "We used the Astropy `BrokenPowerLaw1D` function (see (cite: astropy:2013; astropy:2018)) adopting the `LevMarLSQFitter` routine to perform a Levenberg-Marquardt least squares statistic."]
- step 4: Fit the full broadband SED with the JetSeT jet, disk and corona model
  - method: Fit DiskIrrComp and Comp. hump first over nu = 5e14 to 1e20 Hz, then freeze most disk parameters and fit the global model including JetAcc and RadioJet over the full SED band [Model fit results for the Disk and Coro…: "We fit first the `DiskIrrComp` and `Comp. hump` components restricting the fit range to $\nu$= $[5\times 10^{14},10^{20}]$ Hz"] [Model fit results for the jet emission: "and we fit the global model over the full SED band in the range $\nu$= $[5\times 10^{8},10^{20}]$ Hz"]
  - software: JetSeT, Minuit
  - parameter: 5% systematic error added over 1e8 to 1e16 Hz [Initial model setup: "we have added a 5% systematic error in the range $[1\times 10^{8},1\times 10^{16}]$ Hz"]
  - parameter: Jet launch distance z_0 = 50 R_S, initial jet radius 0.1 z_0, q_jet = 0.2 for the phenomenological setup [Phenomenological Model Setup: "We assume that the jet is launched at a distance $z=z_{0}$ from the BH with $z_0=50 R_S\approx 1.2 \times 10^{8}$ cm"] [Phenomenological Model Setup: "setting $q_{jet}=0.2$ we obtain $B_0\approx 6.8 \times 10^{6}$ G"]
  - assumption: Black hole mass 8 solar masses [Phenomenological Model Setup: "We use a black hole mass of $M_{BH}= 8 M_{\Sun}$."]
  - assumption: Ballistic jet with toroidal-dominated magnetic field and constant bulk Lorentz factor [Phenomenological Model Setup: "This choice assumes that the jet is very close to the ballistic regime, with a magnetic field dominated by the toroidal component"]
  - assumption: Only synchrotron cooling in the radio jet [Radio Jet: "In our analysis we take into account only synchrotron cooling"]
  - fitting: Frequentist fit with Minuit; errors from MIGRAD only, no MCMC [Initial model setup: "In the current analysis we use a frequentist approach and we use the `Minuit` `ModelMinimizer` option."] [Initial model setup: "For the error estimate we provide only errors derived form the"]
  - validation: Compared with XSPEC diskir fit parameters; model said to reproduce the UV-to-optical emission and flat radio spectrum [Model fit results for the jet emission: "we find that our assumption based on the connection between a compact acceleration region feeding the extended radio jet is able to model self-consistently the UV-to-optical emission, reproducing the observed flat radio spectrum"]
- result: Synchrotron peak frequency (broken power-law fit) = 1.6e13 ± 0.2e13 Hz [The compact jet emission: "We obtained a synchrotron peak frequency of 1.6$\pm$0.2$\times10^{13}$ Hz."]
- result: Optically thick spectral index alpha_thick = 0.28 ± 0.02 dimensionless [The compact jet emission: "The estimated slope for the optically thick part of the spectrum is $\alpha_{thick}=0.28\pm0.02$ and $\alpha_{thin}=-0.61\pm0.01$ for the optically thin one"]
- result: Optically thin spectral index alpha_thin = -0.61 ± 0.01 dimensionless [The compact jet emission: "The estimated slope for the optically thick part of the spectrum is $\alpha_{thick}=0.28\pm0.02$ and $\alpha_{thin}=-0.61\pm0.01$ for the optically thin one"]
- result: Low-frequency (0.3-10 GHz) radio spectral index = 0.11 ± 0.02 dimensionless [The compact jet emission: "The slope of the lower frequency radio SED (0.3$-$10 GHz), fitted with the `PowerLaw1D` Astropy function, is $\alpha=0.11\pm0.02$."]
- result: JetSeT global fit chi-square = 181 for 122 dof (reduced 1.48) ± not stated dimensionless [Model fit results for the jet emission: "The model fit converged with a final $\chi^2 = 181$ for 122 degree of freedom ($N_{dof}$), corresponding to a $\chi^2_{red}=1.48$."]
- result: Disk outer-irradiation fraction f_out from JetSeT fit = 7.33e-3 ± 0.15e-3 dimensionless [Model fit results for the jet emission: "adding the jet component results in a better constraint on the value of $f_{out}$=$(7.33\pm0.15)\times10^{-3}$"]
- result: Acceleration-region magnetic field and radius (best-fit model) = B ~ 1.8e4 G, R ~ 2.6e9 cm ± not stated G; cm [Discussion and Conclusions: "gives a magnetic field in the acceleration region of $\approx 1.8 \times 10^4$ G, and a region radius of $\approx 2.6 \times 10^9$ cm"]
- result: Jet luminosity (lower limit) = >= 8.0e37 (0.15 L_Edd) ± lower limit erg/s [Discussion and Conclusions: "We found that the relativistic jet required a total energy of $L_{jet}\geq 8.0\times10^{37}$ erg/s, corresponding to 0.15 $L_{Edd}$."]
- result: Radio-jet field index m_jet = ~1.2 ± not stated dimensionless [Discussion and Conclusions: "the best-fit index $m_{jet}\approx 1.2$"]
- limitation (authors): Parameter errors come only from MIGRAD; MCMC deferred to future work [Initial model setup: "a more reliable estimate based on a Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) will be presented in"]
- limitation (authors): Fit may be biased by parameter degeneracies [Model fit results for the jet emission: "even though the fit might be biased by the degeneracy among some parameters"]
- not stated (card): ALMA calibration details: pipeline version, flux and bandpass calibrators, flux-scale accuracy, whether any self-calibration was applied; ALMA imaging settings: tclean parameters (weighting, robust, cell, image size, deconvolver, masking, threshold) and the continuum or line-free channel selection in each spw; How the source flux density was measured in the ALMA images (aperture, point-source fit…

### 2017PASJ...69...20H: ALMA and RATIR observations of GRB 131030A (2017; transients, high_z_galaxies; multi_facility_alma_minor)
- question: Constrain the late-time 345 GHz afterglow/host emission of GRB131030A, model the forward shock with RATIR and Swift data, and assess the host galaxy and the nearby submm source
- step 2: Fit afterglow light curves and SEDs
  - method: Single and broken power-law fits to light curves; power-law fits to SEDs [2.3 Afterglow Lightcurve: "To describe the light curves, we employed a single power-law function by dividing light curve data into earlier"]
  - fitting: chi-square fits, no sampler stated [Afterglow Lightcurve: "We successfully fitted the single power-law function to the earlier light curves."]
  - validation: chi2/nu reported [2.4 Afterglow Spectrum: "$\beta=-0.57\pm0.09$ ($\chi^{2}/\nu=1.9$ with $\nu=8$)"]
- step 4: Host IR luminosity and SFR limits
  - method: Redshift Chary & Elbaz template SED to z=1.293 and scale to ALMA 3-sigma limit; convert L_IR to SFR [4.2 Host Galaxy and ALMAJ2300-0522: "We constrained the 3-$\sigma$ upper limits of IR luminosity as $L_{IR} <1.11\times10^{11} L\solar$."]
  - assumption: ALMAJ2300-0522 at same redshift as GRB [4.2 Host Galaxy and ALMAJ2300-0522: "By assuming the same redshift with GRB131030A, we also obtained $L_{IR}=8.65\times10^{11} L\solar$"]
  - validation: Compared with TOUGH sample SFR [4.2 Host Galaxy and ALMAJ2300-0522: "The upper limit of SFR was consistent with those of the TOUGH samples"]
- result: Host L_IR 3-sigma upper limit = <1.11e11 ± not stated Lsun [abstract: "$L_{IR}<1.11\times10^{11} L\solar$"]
- result: Host SFR 3-sigma upper limit = <18.7 ± not stated Msun/yr [abstract: "SFR$<18.7$ ($M\solar$ yr$^{-1}$)"]
- result: ALMAJ2300-0522 L_IR if at z=1.293 = 8.65e11 ± not stated Lsun [4.2 Host Galaxy and ALMAJ2300-0522: "we also obtained $L_{IR}=8.65\times10^{11} L\solar$ and the corresponding SFR of 147 $M\solar$ yr$^{-1}$"]
- result: Jet break time (r-band) = 2.51e5 ± 0.48e5 s [Table 1: "$(2.51\pm0.48)\times10^{5}$"]
- limitation (authors): ALMA astrometry bugs reported; large offset not explained [2.1 ALMA Submillimeter Follow-up Observ…: "Although several astrometry-related bugs (e.g. phase calibrator coordinate inconsistency) were reported from the ALMA ARC after data delivery"]
- not stated (card): Image size, cell size, masking and clean threshold; Flux calibrator flux scale and its uncertainty; Which frequency/spectral windows were used for continuum imaging; How the template SED was selected and normalized to 345 GHz; Boxfit fitting settings (priors, optimization, fixed parameters); Light-curve fitting method and extinction treatment details; Whether primary beam correction was applied

### 2018A&A...614A..17B: Modelling the carbon AGB star R Sculptoris. Constraining the dust properties in the detached shell based on far-infrared and sub-millimeter observations (2018; evolved_stars; multi_facility_alma_minor)
- question: What are the dust properties in the detached shell of R Scl, and how do they affect the SED and the derived dust shell mass?
- step 1: Fit stellar blackbody
  - method: Grid of blackbodies fit by chi-square to SED points shorter than 4 µm [3.3 Stellar parameters and contribution: "we calculated a small grid of black bodies with varying T eff,⋆ and L ⋆ to determine the best-fitting parameters to our observational data, shorter than 4 µm"]
  - assumption: Distance 370 pc [3.3 Stellar parameters and contribution: "The best-fitting parameters are T eff,⋆ = 2250 K and L ⋆ = 7000 L ⊙ for a distance of D = 370 pc."]
  - validation: ALMA points reproduced by stellar contribution alone [3.3 Stellar parameters and contribution: "It is evident that the ALMA observations do not represent any detached-shell contribution but are reproduced by the stellar contribution alone."]
- step 2: Model wind and shell SED, fit mass-loss and shell mass
  - method: MCMax radiative transfer grids of 50-150 models, chi-square minimisation, then fine scaled grid of 1000 models for shell mass and 1σ error [3.8 Model grid and refinement: "we calculated MCMax radiative transfer model grids with typical sizes of 50–150 models and varying step sizes"]
  - software: MCMax
  - parameter: Grain size 0.1 µm, density 1.8 g cm-3, condensation temperature 1500 K, Suh (2000) optical constants [3.6 Dust properties: "We adopted a grain size of 0.1 µm and a grain density of 1.8 g cm −3"]
  - assumption: Optically thin dust, full gas-dust coupling [3.2 General assumptions and modelling s…: "We assumed that the dust emission is optically thin."]
  - fitting: chi-square minimisation; scaling factor 0.05 to 2 in 1000 steps; 1σ confidence interval [3.8 Model grid and refinement: "multiplied the shell spectrum by a scaling factor, ranging from 0.05 to 2 in 1000 steps"]
  - validation: Compared with Schöier et al. (2005) [4.1 Best-fitting standard model for pre…: "our best-fitting shell mass is consistent with their published (3.2 ± 2.0) × 10 −5 M ⊙"]
- result: Detached shell dust mass (best fit) = 2.9e-5 ± 0.3e-5 Msun [abstract: "a detached shell dust mass of (2.9 ± 0.3) × 10 −5 M ⊙"]
- result: Present-day dust mass-loss rate = 2e-10 ± not stated Msun/yr [abstract: "a present-day dust mass-loss rate of 2 × 10 −10 M ⊙ yr −1"]
- result: Stellar effective temperature = 2250 ± not stated K [3.3 Stellar parameters and contribution: "T eff,⋆ = 2250 K and L ⋆ = 7000 L ⊙"]
- limitation (authors): Present-day mass-loss rate is poorly constrained and should be interpreted with care [3.4 Present-day wind description: "The determined value for the present-day mass-loss rate should therefore be interpreted with some care."]
- not stated (card): ALMA calibration and imaging details and continuum flux extraction method; CASA version; Band 7 continuum image used for stellar subtraction; Opacity tables and grid steps for MCMax

### 2018ApJ...853..195W: Herschel and ALMA Observations of Massive SZE-selected Clusters (2018; galaxy_clusters_and_sz, high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: How do star formation, cold gas and dust, and AGN content of galaxies in massive SZE-selected clusters at 0.3<z<1.1 depend on redshift and clustercentric radius, and compare to field galaxies?
- step 4: Fit IR SEDs for L_IR and SFR
  - method: Fit 160 um PACS flux to Kirkpatrick+15 templates by chi2 with free normalization; emcee for normalization uncertainty; SFR = 1.65 Msun/yr x L_IR/1e10 Lsun [3.3 Far-infrared continuum sources: "In order to find the best fitting template, $\chi^2$ is minimized while the overall SED normalization is allowed to vary as a free parameter."]
  - software: emcee
  - assumption: Chabrier IMF; L_TIR(3-1100um) ~ 1.1 L_IR(8-1000um) [3.3 Far-infrared continuum sources: "we approximate $L_{\rm TIR}(3-1100~\ensuremath{\mu}m) \approx 1.1\times L_{\rm IR}(8-1000~\ensuremath{\mu}m)$"]
  - fitting: emcee MCMC on normalization; posterior checked as normal; sigma taken as std [3.3 Far-infrared continuum sources: "For each galaxy, we verify that the samples follow a normal distribution."]
  - validation: Compared with Chary & Elbaz templates and ALMA continuum predictions; PACS negative-map fidelity test [3.3 Far-infrared continuum sources: "Only a single 4 $\sigma$ detection is found in the negative J0438 100 $\mu$m map"]
- step 6: Radio AGN diagnosis and evolution trends
  - method: q_IR from L_IR and 1.4 GHz luminosity (alpha=0.8), threshold 1.8; power-law/linear fits with emcee for IRBG fraction and sSFR vs z [5.5 The FIR-radio correlation and AGN f…: "a separator of $q_{\rm IR} \approx 1.8$ is sometimes used to differentiate between star-forming galaxies and AGNs"]
  - software: emcee
  - assumption: radio spectral index alpha = 0.8 [3.4 Radio-bright sources: "assuming $S_\nu \propto \nu^{-\alpha}$ with spectral index $\alpha = 0.8$"]
  - fitting: chi2 MAP power-law then emcee sampling of index; 68% binomial intervals [5.4 Redshift evolution of infrared-brig…: "We sample the marginalized distribution of the power-law index parameter using `emcee`."]
- result: Mean log sSFR = -10.36 ± 0.57 log yr^-1 [5.6 Star formation rate as a function o…: "average $\log({\rm sSFR / yr}^{-1}) = -10.36 \pm 0.57$"]
- result: Mean q_IR of radio detections excluding AGN = 1.54 ± 0.22 dimensionless [5.5 The FIR-radio correlation and AGN f…: "we find a mean $q_{\rm IR} = 1.54 \pm 0.22$"]
- result: AGN fraction lower limit = ~0.2 ± not stated dimensionless [abstract: "implying an AGN fraction $f_{\rm AGN} \gtrsim 0.2$ for our PACS-selected sample"]
- limitation (authors): Optical selection of members may bias the star-forming census; small samples of CO detections. [5.3 Biases from optical selection of cl…: "we have grounds for concern that our optical selection induces a general bias in our search for dust-obscured galaxies"]
- not stated (card): Which CASA clean parameters (cell size, image size, niter, threshold, mosaic gridder, masks) were used; Line-free channels used for continuum subtraction; Exact flagging/pipeline modifications beyond edge-channel flagging; Self-calibration was or was not applied; SoFiA parameter files beyond SNR 5 and 3x3 smoothing; Moment-zero channel ranges per source; Primary-beam correction details; Number of…

### 2020MNRAS.491.1174M: Circumstellar CO J = 3→2 detected around the evolving metal-poor ([Fe/H] ≈ -1.15 dex) AGB star RU Vulpeculae (2020; evolved_stars, stellar_physics; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Explore the properties of RU Vul and its wind in the context of other metal-poor stars.
- step 3: Fit the stellar SED
  - method: Fit Hipparcos mean and 2MASS JHKs photometry independently of the spectroscopic parameters; derive luminosity and radius. [SED fitting: "To ensure a fully independent derivation from the spectroscopic determination, we do not use the metallicity, temperature or surface gravity derived in the fit."] [SED fitting: "photometry restricted to the *Hipparcos* mean magnitude (cite: vanLeeuwen07) and the 2MASS $JHK_{\rm s}$ magnitudes (cite: SCS+06)"]
  - software: code of (cite: MvLD+09), with additions of (cite: MZB12) and (cite: MZW17)
  - assumption: [Fe/H] = –1.6 dex; M = 0.6 M⊙; d = 2 kpc [SED fitting: "Instead, we assume the original \[Fe/H\] = –1.6 dex from (cite: UGT16)."] [SED fitting: "To set the model’s surface gravity, we further assume $M$ = 0.6 M$_\odot$ and $d = 2$ kpc."]
  - assumption: E(B−V) = 0.096 mag; (cite: Draine03) reddening law [SED fitting: "We also assumed $E(B-V) = 0.096$ mag (cite: SF11) and a (cite: Draine03) reddening law."]
  - validation: Vary metallicity, mass and reddening; compare photometric and spectroscopic temperatures. [SED fitting: "assuming \[Fe/H\] = –1.0 dex raises the fitted parameters by 27 K and 13 L$_\odot$"] [SED fitting: "assuming $M = 0.9$ M$_\odot$ has no effect on temperature but decreases the luminosity by 7 L$_\odot$"]
- step 11: Fit dust composition and shell parameters
  - method: Fit the SED by eye with dusty; compare amorphous carbon, metallic iron, astronomical silicates and a mixed model. [Fitting the SED: "we perform radiative-transfer modelling using the dusty code (cite: INE99)"] [Fitting the SED: "The resulting fits are heavily parameterised, and parameters are highly correlated, meaning a fit is best achieved “by eye”."]
  - software: dusty, bt-settl
  - parameter: density type = 3; bt-settl input: 3600 K, log(g) = 0 dex, [Fe/H] = –1.0 dex [Fitting the SED: "The wind was modelled using a purely radiation-driven wind (dusty option `density type = 3`)."] [Fitting the SED: "The 3600 K, log($g$) = 0 dex, \[Fe/H\] = –1.0 dex bt-settl model atmosphere (cite: AGL+03) was used as an input"]
  - parameter: Amorphous carbon: Tdust,in = 750 K; Rout/Rin = 100; τV = 0.65 mag [Fitting the SED: "*Amorphous carbon:* $T_{\rm dust,in} = 750$ K, $R_{\rm out}/R_{\rm in} = 100$ and $\tau_V = 0.65$ mag."]
  - parameter: Metallic iron: Tdust,in = 675 K; Rout/Rin = 33; τV = 1.1 mag [Fitting the SED: "*Metallic iron:* $T_{\rm dust,in} = 675$ K, $R_{\rm out}/R_{\rm in} = 33$ and $\tau_V = 1.1$ mag."]
  - parameter: Astronomical silicates: Tdust,in = 750 K; Rout/Rin = 1000; τV = 1 mag [Fitting the SED: "*Astronomical silicates:* $T_{\rm dust,in} = 750$ K, $R_{\rm out}/R_{\rm in} = 1000$ and $\tau_V = 1$ mag."]
  - parameter: Mixed: 80 per cent metallic iron, 13 per cent silicate, 7 per cent Al2O3; Tdust,in = 600 K; Rout/Rin = 50; τV = 0.37 mag [Fitting the SED: "Components of this wind were fit as: 80 per cent metallic iron, 13 per cent silicate and 7 per cent Al$_2$O$_3$."] [Fitting the SED: "Other properties are: $T_{\rm dust,in} = 600$ K, $R_{\rm out}/R_{\rm in} = 50$ and $\tau_V = 0.37$ mag."]
  - assumption: Instantaneous thin-shell dust formation; constant mineralogy and grain properties; dust–gas temperature equilibrium [Fitting the SED: "It also assumes that dust is formed in an instantaneous thin shell, that dust mineralogy and grain properties remain constant throughout the stellar envelope, and that temperature equilibrium remains between the dust and surrounding gas."]
  - assumption: MRN grains: a = 0.005–0.25 μm; N(a) ∝ a−3.5; same size distribution for each component [Fitting the SED: "We use the standard (cite: MRN77) size limits ($a$ = 0.005–0.25 $\mu$m) and slope ($N(a) \propto a^{-3.5}$)."] [Fitting the SED: "that grains of each type come from the same (cite: MRN77) size distribution of grains."]
  - fitting: By eye; correlated parameters; results indicative [Fitting the SED: "The resulting fits are heavily parameterised, and parameters are highly correlated, meaning a fit is best achieved “by eye”."] [Fitting the SED: "Consequently, the results should only be used indicatively."]
  - validation: Mixed model improves longer-wavelength agreement but under-predicts 2–10 μm flux. [Fitting the SED: "The model still under-predicts the flux between 2 and 10 $\mu$m but performs better when reproducing the longer-wavelength data."]
- step 12: Infer radial extent and dust geometry
  - method: Convert dust temperatures to shell radii; compare model absorption with the stellar SED and unresolved ALMA emission. [Radial dust geometry and wind driving: "we can combine these with our values of $T_{\rm dust,in}$ from Section [4.3.1](#DustSEDSect) to obtain $R_{\rm in}$, then use $R_{\rm out}/R_{\rm in}$ to compute the expected extent of the dust shell."] [Optical dust absorption and dust inhomo…: "our dusty fits indicate a $V$-band absorption of $\sim$0.37 mag for our adopted mixed-dust wind, while SED fitting (Section [2.3.1](#SEDSect)) indicates $\tau_V < 0.1$ mag."]
  - software: dusty
  - parameter: Condensation temperatures and radii from Equation 10 and Figure 1 of (cite: BH12) [Radial dust geometry and wind driving: "We can use Equation 10 and Figure 1 of (cite: BH12) to derive expected condensation temperatures and radii ($T_{\rm cond}$, $R_{\rm cond}$) for our different dust species."]
  - assumption: Radiative equilibrium, printed as T⁴ ∝ R² [Radial dust geometry and wind driving: "Assuming radiative equilibrium ($T^4 \propto R^2$)"]
  - validation: Compare expected reddening with agreement of spectroscopic and photometric temperatures. [Optical dust absorption and dust inhomo…: "these temperatures agree to within uncertainties ($\lesssim$100 K; Section [2.2](#SpecAtmosSect) & [2.3](#PhotAtmosSect))."] [Optical dust absorption and dust inhomo…: "This implies an aspherical or clumpy geometry to the wind that affords us a clear line of sight towards the stellar surface."]
- result: Photometric SED temperature = 3639 ± ±∼100 K [SED fitting: "returns a temperature of 3639 K, which we expect is accurate to within $\pm\sim$100 K"]
- result: Luminosity = 3125 ± ± 516 L⊙ [SED fitting: "we determine the luminosity of RU Vul to be $L = 3125 \pm 516$ L$_\odot$"]
- result: Stellar radius = 142 ± ± 14 R⊙ [SED fitting: "$R_\ast = 142 \pm 14$ R$_\odot$ (0.66 $\pm$ 0.07 AU)."]
- result: Circumstellar V-band optical-depth estimate = ≲0.1 ± not stated mag [SED fitting: "we can estimate that $\tau_V \lesssim 0.1$ mag for RU Vul"]
- result: Mixed-model metallic-iron component = 80 ± not stated per cent [Fitting the SED: "Components of this wind were fit as: 80 per cent metallic iron, 13 per cent silicate and 7 per cent Al$_2$O$_3$."]
- result: Mixed-model silicate component = 13 ± not stated per cent [Fitting the SED: "Components of this wind were fit as: 80 per cent metallic iron, 13 per cent silicate and 7 per cent Al$_2$O$_3$."]
- result: Mixed-model Al2O3 component = 7 ± not stated per cent [Fitting the SED: "Components of this wind were fit as: 80 per cent metallic iron, 13 per cent silicate and 7 per cent Al$_2$O$_3$."]
- result: Mixed-model inner dust temperature = 600 ± not stated K [Fitting the SED: "Other properties are: $T_{\rm dust,in} = 600$ K, $R_{\rm out}/R_{\rm in} = 50$ and $\tau_V = 0.37$ mag."]
- result: Mixed-model outer-to-inner radius ratio = 50 ± not stated dimensionless [Fitting the SED: "Other properties are: $T_{\rm dust,in} = 600$ K, $R_{\rm out}/R_{\rm in} = 50$ and $\tau_V = 0.37$ mag."]
- result: Mixed-model V-band optical depth = 0.37 ± not stated mag [Fitting the SED: "Other properties are: $T_{\rm dust,in} = 600$ K, $R_{\rm out}/R_{\rm in} = 50$ and $\tau_V = 0.37$ mag."]
- result: ALMA upper limit on outer radius = ≲0.5 ± not stated arcsec [Radial dust geometry and wind driving: "the resolution of ALMA only provides a limit of $R_{\rm outer} \lesssim 0.5^{\prime\prime}$ (1000 AU)."]
- result: ALMA upper limit on outer radius at the adopted distance = 1000 ± not stated AU [Radial dust geometry and wind driving: "the resolution of ALMA only provides a limit of $R_{\rm outer} \lesssim 0.5^{\prime\prime}$ (1000 AU)."]
- limitation (authors): Non-LTE effects may introduce uncharacterised spectroscopic errors [Spectral synthesis: "Out-of-local-thermodynamic-equilibrium (non-LTE) effects were seen in the spectrum, including line doubling and emission in chromospherically active lines, which may cause uncharacterised errors in the final fit."]
- limitation (authors): Only three Fe II lines; no unique stable positive-log(g) solution [Spectral synthesis: "only three Fe ii lines were measurable in the data, and it was not possible to find a unique, stable solution with positive log($g$)."]

### 2015A&A...581A..97S: Collisional modelling of the AU Microscopii debris disc (2015; debris_disks; theory_or_simulation_compared_to_alma)
- question: What dust production, dust transport and planetesimal dynamics in the AU Mic debris disc are consistent with the SED, the ALMA 1.3 mm radial profile and scattered light data?
- step 2: Collisional modelling of outer disc
  - method: ACE simulations varying wind strength, material, e_max, belt width; fit SED vertical scaling by timestep; compare to scaled convolved 1.3 mm profile, scattered light and polarisation [4 Resolved outer disc: "we vary other initial disc parameters of this reference run in order to more tightly constrain the parameters and reveal degeneracies among them"]
  - software: ACE
  - parameter: Belt at 40 au, width 5 au, e_max=0.03, SW50 reference, material M1 [4.1 Stellar wind strength: "having the full width $\Delta r_\mathrm{PB}=5$ au. In all three runs, the maximum eccentricity of the planetesimals was set to $e_\text{max}=0.03$"]
  - assumption: Inclination 89.5 deg; Mie spheres; wind speed 400 km/s; stellar mass 0.5 Msun [4.1 Stellar wind strength: "we assumed a disc inclination of 89.5$^\circ$ from face-on (cite: Krist2005)"]
  - fitting: Not fitted; chi2_SED/N used for comparison only [4.1.1 Impact on thermal emission: "We stress that the models are not fitted to the data through variations of the initial disc parameters."]
  - validation: Compare to SED, ALMA profile, scattered light and polarisation [Resolved outer disc: "First, we seek to explain the available observational data on the resolved outer AU Mic disc, including the SED, the ALMA radial brightness profile, and scattered light measurements."]
- step 3: Power-law parametric fit
  - method: Grid fit of two power laws (radial and size) to SED and ALMA profile, with and without inner ring [6 Comparison with parametric modelling: "we used the `SAnD` code (cite: Ertel2012; Loehne2012)"]
  - software: SAnD
  - assumption: Compact astronomical silicate grains, inclination 90 deg, s_max 2 mm [6 Comparison with parametric modelling: "We fixed $s_\mathrm{max}$ to 2 mm."]
  - fitting: Grid chi2 minimisation with 3 sigma intervals [Comparison with parametric modelling: "we additionally analysed the data with a multidimensional fitting approach."]
  - validation: Compared with collisional results [Comparison with parametric modelling: "In order to find an independent confirmation of the collisional modelling results, we additionally analysed the data with a multidimensional fitting approach."]
- result: Power-law best-fit R_out = 41.9 ± 3 sigma 37.3 – 45.5 au [Table 2: "41.9 \[37.3 – 45.5\]"]
- result: Power-law surface density index alpha = 2.8 ± 3 sigma 1.8 – 4.8 dimensionless [Table 2: "2.8 \[1.8 – 4.8\]"]
- result: Max fraction of central emission from inner dust ring = 60 ± not stated % [7 Conclusions: "emits at most 60% of the observed central emission"]
- limitation (authors): Axisymmetric discs only; models not unique [7 Conclusions: "All the models presented here are not unique as a full exploration of the parameter space by collisional modelling is impossible."]
- not stated (card): CASA version, imaging task and weighting for the re-reduction; Calibration/flagging details and flux calibrator; Number of ACE grid points and size range of the grid; Fitting range and details of SAnD chi2 (error handling); Scaling factors applied to ALMA profile per model

- result: BCG dust mass = 3.5 × 10^8 ± +2.3/-1.9 × 10^8 M⊙ [Unresolved source: "we measure values for the dust mass and temperature of $3.5\substack{+2.3\\-1.9}\cdot10^8~\mathrm{M_{\odot}}$ and $19.8\substack{+7.0\\-6.6}~\mathrm{K}$, respectively"]

- question: Is RMC 143 an LBV, and what are the dust content, mass and ionization of its circumstellar nebula, using ALMA Band 7 together with ATCA, X-shooter and IR data?

### 2023A&A...673A.132B: Extinction towards the cluster R136 in the Large Magellanic Cloud. An extinction law from the near-infrared to the ultraviolet (2023; stellar_physics, milky_way_ism, nearby_galaxies; multi_facility_alma_minor)
- question: Construct an extinction law from the near-infrared to the ultraviolet towards the cluster R136 in the LMC, measure extinction towards individual sources, map the spatial distribution of dust, and improve mass measurements of the very massi…
- step 1: Compile observed SEDs from UV to NIR for 50 stars in the R136 core
  - method: Convert optical (HST/WFC3) and NIR (VLT/SPHERE) magnitudes to fluxes using SVO filter transmission curves, and bin flux-calibrated HST/STIS UV spectra into 9 synthetic passbands. [Observed SEDs: "The optical and NIR observed magnitudes are converted to fluxes using filter transmission curves taken from the SVO Filter Profile Service"] [Observed SEDs: "For the UV, we bin the observed flux points by taking the flux average over nine different wavelength regions as specified in 5."]
  - software: multispec
  - parameter: 9 UV synthetic passbands between 1265.00 Å and 1700.00 Å; 16 total flux points per SED [Observed SEDs: "With the 4 optical and 3 NIR photometry points for the R136 core stars, the observed SEDs consist of 16 flux points in total."] [UV bands: "5 lists the wavelength ranges that we used for binning our UV flux measurements."]
  - validation: Summed the STIS spectra of all stars in the cluster core and compared the integrated flux to the large-aperture (2' x 2') HST/GHRS spectrum, finding agreement within 25% overall and within 10% for wavelengths > 1350 Å. [Sample and data: "To check our flux calibration for systematic uncertainties, we evaluate the integrated flux of the cluster core. To this end, we sum the flux-calibrated spectra of all stars in the cluster core."] [Sample and data: "For all wavelengths considered in the fitting, the integrated fluxes match within 25%, and for wavelengths > 1350 Å the match is even within 10% (1)."]
- step 2: Compute intrinsic model SEDs for the R136 core stars
  - method: Compute non-LTE stellar atmosphere models using Fastwind adopting stellar parameters from Brands et al. (2022), scaling the output SED by stellar surface area to vary stellar radius. [Intrinsic SEDs: "We compute models of intrinsic SEDs with the model atmosphere code Fastwind (cite: 1997A&A...323..488S; 2005A&A...435..669P; 2012A&A...537A..79R; 2016A&A...590A..88C; 2018A&A...619A..59S)."] [Intrinsic SEDs: "For the stellar parameters, we adopt the values of the optical and UV analysis of (cite: 2022arXiv220211080B) for stars in the core of R136."]
  - software: Fastwind
  - assumption: Small changes in stellar radius R_* do not significantly change the atmospheric structure, allowing R_* to be varied by scaling the model SED. [Intrinsic SEDs: "However, for small changes in $R_*$, the structure of the atmosphere will not change significantly and this means that we can change $R_*$ of the model simply by multiplying the output SED with a factor that represents an increase or decrease in the stellar surface area."]
  - validation: Tested mimicking the UV absorption-line forest with a 10% UV flux decrease (finding minor effect on c2 and none on A_5495, R_5495, L), and compared low mass-loss Fastwind models to hydrostatic TLUSTY models in U-B vs B-V and J-H vs H-K pla… [Intrinsic SEDs: "We mimic the magnitude of the effect by representing the UV absorption-line forest by an overall decrease in the UV model flux of 10%. In this case, the best-fitting UV extinction is only slightly lower ($\Delta c_{2} = -0.05$)"] [Intrinsic SEDs: "We find that the qualitative behaviour of the two sets of models is similar, and that the absolute differences between the Fastwind and TLUSTY colours are never more than 2%."]
- step 3: Parameterise the modified NIR-to-UV extinction law and fit individual stellar SEDs
  - method: Construct a cubic spline extinction law in the optical/NIR based on Maíz Apellániz et al. (2014) and a UV parameterisation from Fitzpatrick & Massa (1990) with independent c2 and c4, then fit 5 free parameters per star to the observed SEDs. [Extinction law: "Briefly, we use the optical and NIR law of (cite: 2014AA...564A..63M), which we transform to the functional form of (cite: 1999PASP..111...63F). For the UV, we adopt the parameterisation of and (cite: 1990ApJS...72..163F)."] [Fitting: "We fit five parameters: $R_{5495}$, affecting the shape of the optical and NIR part of the curve; $c_2$ and $c_4$, relating to the UV part of the curve; the extinction in the $V$ band $A_V$; and the stellar angular radius $\theta_R = R_*/d$"]
  - software: Python, scipy, numpy, lmfit
  - parameter: Fixed parameters: c1 = 2.030 - 3.007 c2, c3 = 1.463, c5 = 5.9, x0 = 4.558, gamma = 0.945; Free parameters: R_5495, A_V, R_*, c2, c4 [Extinction law: "We do not fit the data of the bump as we lack observations here, and instead adopt the values that (cite: 2003ApJ...594..279G) find for LMC SGS 2 (near 30 Doradus), that is, $c_3 = 1.463$, $\gamma = 0.945,$ and $x_0 = 4.558$."] [Table 3: "$c_1$ | 2.030 – 3.007 $c_2$"]
  - assumption: Fixed LMC distance d = 49.59 kpc [Fitting: "We adopt a fixed LMC distance of $d = 49.59$ kpc for all stars (cite: 2019Natur.567..200P)"]
  - assumption: NIR power-law slope alpha_NIR = 1.61 from Maíz Apellániz et al. (2014) [Extinction law: "The NIR part of the law can be approximated by a power law of the form $A_\lambda \propto \lambda^{\alpha_\mathrm{NIR}}$ with $\alpha_\mathrm{NIR} = 1.61$"]
  - fitting: Least-squares Levenberg-Marquardt chi^2 minimisation using lmfit.minimize [Fitting: "In order to find the best-fitting values of the free parameters $A_V$, $R_*$, and those describing the shape of the curve ($R_{5495}$, $c_2$, and $c_4$), we use the `minimize` function of the Python package `lmfit` (cite: 2014zndo.....11813N). We adopt the least-squares Levenberg-Marquardt method for the minimisation,…"]
  - validation: Tested refitting SEDs with fixed R_5495 and tested varying the number of UV points, confirming conclusions and spatial trends remain robust. [Observed SEDs: "We note that the results are robust to changes in the number of UV points that we include in our analysis; a change in the number of points has a negligible effect on the outcome of our fits and would not affect our conclusions."] [Spatial trends in extinction: "We note that if we refit the SED while adopting a fixed value of $R_{5495}$(equal for all stars), we still recover the spatial trend for $A_{5495}$, although the gradient is slightly less steep."]
- result: Cluster average total-to-relative extinction R_V (or R_5495) = 4.38 ± 0.87 dimensionless [abstract: "In line with previous measurements of R136 and the 30 Doradus region, we obtain a high total-to-relative extinction ($R_V = 4.38 \pm 0.87$)."]
- result: Cluster average extinction A_5495 = 1.71 ± 0.41 mag [An R_5495-dependent average extinction …: "and cluster averages of $A_{5495} = 1.71\pm0.41$ and of $R_V = 4.38 \pm 0.87$."]
- result: Minimum core extinction A_5495 (H108) = 0.76 ± 0.06 mag [An R_5495-dependent average extinction …: "with $A_{5495}$ ranging from $A_{5495} = 0.76\pm0.06$ (H108) to $A_{5495} = 2.63\pm 0.05$ (H36)"]
- result: Maximum core extinction A_5495 (H36) = 2.63 ± 0.05 mag [An R_5495-dependent average extinction …: "with $A_{5495}$ ranging from $A_{5495} = 0.76\pm0.06$ (H108) to $A_{5495} = 2.63\pm 0.05$ (H36)"]
- result: Minimum core R_5495 (H108) = 1.91 ± 0.26 dimensionless [An R_5495-dependent average extinction …: "and $R_{5495}$ ranging from $R_{5495} = 1.91\pm0.26$ (H108) to $R_{5495} = 5.79\pm0.31$ (H36)"]
- result: Maximum core R_5495 (H36) = 5.79 ± 0.31 dimensionless [An R_5495-dependent average extinction …: "and $R_{5495}$ ranging from $R_{5495} = 1.91\pm0.26$ (H108) to $R_{5495} = 5.79\pm0.31$ (H36)"]
- result: Cluster average UV extinction slope parameter c_2 = 1.30 ± 0.22 dimensionless [An R_5495-dependent average extinction …: "for which we find values in the range of $c_2 = 0.78-2.36$, with an average of $c_2 = 1.30\pm0.22$."]
- result: Cluster average far-UV curvature parameter c_4 = 0.09 ± 0.08 dimensionless [The UV curvature (c_4): "For the parameter $c_4$, describing the curvature of the extinction curve in the far-UV ($x > 5.9~\mu\mathrm{m}^{-1}$ or $\lambda < 1695~ \mathrm{\AA}$), we find an average value of $c_4 = 0.09\pm0.08$."]
- limitation (authors): Pointing inaccuracies in the crowded HST/STIS long-slit observations leave flux calibration uncertainties of order 10% (up to a factor of two in extreme cases), and potential relative calibration divergence at lambda < 1400 Å may cause c_4… [Sample and data: "As a consequence of pointing inaccuracies, uncertainties in the calibration remain; we estimate these to be on the order of 10% (see below), but in the most extreme cases they could be as large as a factor two."]
- limitation (authors): The adopted NIR extinction law slope (alpha_NIR = 1.61) is likely imperfect for R136, causing systematic residual patterns in NIR bands and high reduced chi^2 values (dominated by H and K_s bands). [Extinction law: "Upon inspecting the residuals of our fits as a function of $A_V$, we see clear patterns for all NIR bands, indicating that the law is not completely adequate."]
- limitation (authors): Lack of observations across the 2175 Å bump prevents fitting the Drude profile parameters directly. [Extinction law: "We do not fit the data of the bump as we lack observations here, and instead adopt the values that (cite: 2003ApJ...594..279G) find for LMC SGS 2"]
- not stated (card): ALMA project code, calibration, and imaging parameters for the 13CO(2-1) column density map (taken from Wong et al. 2022); Exact Fastwind version and input configuration files used to compute the intrinsic stellar SEDs

### 2021A&A...647A..10G: Bottlenecks to interstellar sulfur chemistry. Sulfur-bearing hydrides in UV-illuminated gas and grains (2021; astrochemistry, milky_way_ism; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Whether gas-phase reactions of S atoms and SH+ molecules with vibrationally excited H2 can explain the presence of abundant H2S in FUV-illuminated gas, or if grain surface chemistry has to be invoked.
- step 3: Derive single-slab non-LTE excitation and column densities of SH+, SH, and H2S
  - method: Solve statistical equilibrium and nonlocal radiative transfer with a Monte Carlo code including chemical formation pumping and destruction rates, dust continuum radiation, and inelastic collisions with H2, H, and e-. [4 Coupled nonlocal excitation and chemi…: "In these cases, a proper treatment of the molecule excitation requires including chemical formation and destruction rates in the statistical equilibrium"] [4. Coupled nonlocal excitation and chem…: "Here we implemented this formalism in a Monte Carlo code that explicitly models the nonlocal behavior of the excitation and radiative transfer problem (see Appendix of Goicoechea et al. 2006)."]
  - parameter: Modified blackbody dust continuum at T_d ∼ 50 K with τ_λ = 0.03 (150/λ[µm])^1.6 [4. Coupled nonlocal excitation and chem…: "for completeness we also included radiative excitation by a modified blackbody at a dust temperature of ∼ 50 K and a dust opacity τ λ = 0.03 (150/λ[µm]) [1][.][6]"]
  - parameter: T_form ≈ 2000 K for SH+; T_form = T_k for SH [4.1. SH + excitation and column density: "Since the formation of SH [+] is driven by reaction (1) when H 2 molecules are in v ≥ 2, here we adopted T form ≃ E (v = 2, J = 0) / k − 9860 K ≈ 2000 K ."] [4.2. SH excitation and column density: "Models in Fig. 7 include these chemical rates for T form = T k (a lower limit to the unknown formation temperature)."]
  - assumption: f(H2) = 2n(H2)/n_H = 2/3; n_e = 1.4x10^-4 n_H; H2 OTP ratio thermalized to T_k [4. Coupled nonlocal excitation and chem…: "The molecular gas fraction, f (H 2 ) = 2 n (H 2 )/ n H, is set to 2/3, where n H = n (H) + 2 n (H 2 ) is the total density of H nuclei ."] [4. Coupled nonlocal excitation and chem…: "the electron density n e is set to n e ≃ n (C [+] ) = 1.4×10 [−][4] n H (e.g., Cuadrado et al. 2019). For the inelastic collisions with o -H 2 and p -H 2, we assumed that the H 2 ortho-to-para (OTP) ratio is thermalized to the gas temperature."]
  - assumption: SH-H and SH-H2 collisional rates scaled from SH-He rates by the square root of reduced mass ratios [4.2. SH excitation and column density: "Lacking specific inelastic collision rate coefficients, we scaled the available SH– He rates of Kłos et al. (2009) by the square root of the reduced mass ratios and estimated the SH– H and SH– H 2 collisional rates."]
  - fitting: Grid search / comparison of single-slab models against observed line intensities and ratios [4. Coupled nonlocal excitation and chem…: "we search for the combination of single T k, n H, and N that better reproduces the observed line intensities (so-called “single-slab” approach)."]
  - validation: Compared models with and without formation pumping (F=D=0 vs F,D vs F,Dx10) and verified consistency with the 683 GHz SH+ upper limit. [4.1. SH + excitation and column density: "Figure 6 shows results of several models: without formation pumping (dotted curves for model “ F = D = 0” ), adding formation pumping with SH [+] destruction by H and e [−] (continuous curves for model “ F, D ” )"] [4.1. SH + excitation and column density: "This model is consistent with the upper limit intensity of the 683 GHz line (Nagy et al. 2013) ."]
- result: SH+ column density N(SH+) = 1.1x10^13 ± not stated cm^-2 [4.1. SH + excitation and column density: "The best fit to the observed lines in model F, D is for N (SH [+] ) ≃ 1.1×10 [13] cm [−][3], n H ≃ 3×10 [5] cm [−][3], and T k ≃ 200 K ."]
- result: SH column density upper limit N(SH) = ≤ (0.6-1.6)x10^14 ± not stated cm^-2 [4.2. SH excitation and column density: "If SH and SH [+] arise from roughly the same gas at similar physical conditions ( n H ≃ 10 [6] cm [−][3] and T k ≃ 200 K ) the best model column density is for N (SH) ≤ (0.6-1.6)×10 [14] cm [−][2] ."]
- result: H2S total column density N(H2S) = 2.5x10^14 ± not stated cm^-2 [4.3. H 2 S excitation and column density: "The vertical dotted lines in Fig. 8 show the best model, N (H 2 S) = N ( o -H 2 S)+ N ( p -H 2 S) = 2.5×10 [14] cm [−][2], with an OTP ratio of 2.9 ± 0.3"]
- result: H2S ortho-to-para (OTP) ratio = 2.9 ± 0.3 dimensionless [abstract: "explain the observed H 2 S column density ( ∼ 2.5 × 10 [14] cm [−][2], with an ortho-to-para ratio of 2.9 ± 0.3; consistent with the high-temperature statistical value)."]
- result: N(SH) / N(H2S) column density ratio upper limit = ≤ 0.2-0.6 ± not stated dimensionless [4.3. H 2 S excitation and column density: "They result in a limit to the SH to H 2 S column density ratio of ≤ 0.2-0.6 ."]
- limitation (authors): Bad weather limited SOFIA/GREAT observations to ~70 min on a single flight, so the achieved rms (50 mK) is insufficient for an unambiguous detection of SH, providing only upper limits. [2.4. SOFIA/GREAT search for SH emission: "The original plan was to observe during two flights in November 2019 but due to bad weather conditions, only ∼70 min of observations were carried out in a single flight."]
- limitation (authors): Specific inelastic collision rate coefficients for SH with H and H2 are not available, requiring mass-scaling from SH-He rates without hyperfine resolution. [4.2. SH excitation and column density: "Lacking specific inelastic collision rate coefficients, we scaled the available SH– He rates of Kłos et al. (2009) by the square root of the reduced mass ratios and estimated the SH– H and SH– H 2 collisional rates."]
- limitation (authors): Gas-grain models do not include formation of more complex S-bearing ices (s-OCS, s-H2S2, s-S_n, s-SO2, s-HSO) or gas-phase reactions of S+ with desorbed organic molecules like H2CO, and assume steady state, making predictions deep inside t… [6.3. Gas-grain PDR model results: "However, we stress that here we do not consider the formation of more complex S-bearing ices such as s-OCS, s-H 2 S 2, s-S n, s-SO 2 or s-HSO (Jiménez-Escobar & Muñoz Caro 2011; Vidal et al. 2017; Laas & Caselli 2019)."]
- not stated (card): CASA / GILDAS version and exact parameters used for ALMA calibration and imaging (inherited from Goicoechea et al. 2016); Weighting scheme (robust parameter), deconvolver, pixel cell size, and image size for the ALMA SH+ mosaic; Method for combining ALMA 12 m interferometric visibilities with Total Power single-dish maps (e.g., feather vs. joint deconvolution); Continuum subtraction procedure for…

### 2014ApJ...790..160S: ALMA Observations of a Candidate Molecular Outflow in an Obscured Quasar (2014; agn_and_quasars, nearby_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the morphology, kinematics and mass of molecular gas in the obscured quasar SDSS J1356+1026, and is there a molecular outflow related to the known ionized outflow?
- step 4: IR SED decomposition for SFR upper limit and Lbol
  - method: Two-temperature modified blackbody fit to seven points; Tcold fixed 35 K, beta=1.5, 10% errors; Bell 2003 calibration for SFR [3.4 SED: "The temperature of the cold dust is fixed at $T_{\rm{cold}}=35$ K"]
  - assumption: photometry errors 10% [3.4 SED: "All photometry errors are assumed to be 10%, which is representative of the systematic errors."]
  - fitting: free: Twarm, Lwarm, Lcold; reduced chi2 of 1 [3.4 SED: "The best-fit model (black line, Fig. [5](#fig:SED)) matches the data well with a reduced $\chi^2$ of 1."]
  - validation: Fitting Tcold free recovers 35 K [3.4 SED: "Even if we fit the temperature of the cold component we recover $T_{\rm{cold}}=35$ K."]
- result: SFR upper limit from FIR SED = 21 ± upper limit Msun/yr [3.4 SED: "upper limit on the star formation rate of 21 $M_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$"]
- result: Warm dust temperature = 81 ± not stated K [3.4 SED: "The best-fit temperature of the warm component is high $T_{\rm{warm}}=81$ K"]
- limitation (authors): Outflow quantities order-of-magnitude; XCO and line ratio uncertain [5.2.1 Properties of the Molecular Outfl…: "First, the $X_{\rm{CO}}$ factor in the context of an outflow is uncertain."]
- not stated (card): Exact clean parameters (cell size, image size, niter, masks, spectral-line channel ranges for CO 3-2); Line-free channel ranges for CO(3-2) 300 GHz continuum check; Aperture sizes/centres for flux extraction; Kinematic fitting details of 2D Gaussian per channel; Herschel photometry values (Petric et al. in prep.); Exact formulae for outflow energetics and Kennicutt SFR conversion; HST astrometric…

### 2025A&A...696A.100G: PDRs4All: XII. Far-ultraviolet-driven formation of simple hydrocarbon radicals and their relation with polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbons (2025; milky_way_ism, astrochemistry, star_formation; observational_alma_primary)
- question: How are simple hydrocarbon radicals (C2H, C, CH_n) formed in the FUV-irradiated Orion Bar PDR, and are they linked to PAH aliphatic content traced by the 3.4/3.3 um AIB ratio?
- step 5: Derive C2H and C column densities and abundances
  - method: Nonlocal non-LTE Monte Carlo radiative transfer grid in n_H and T_k; f(H2)=2/3; dust continuum excitation; new C-H2 rates to 3000 K [4.4: "we updated our nonlocal and non-LTE radiative transfer Monte Carlo model (cite: Goico22) to treat the rotational excitation of C$_2$H"]
  - parameter: T_k = 600 K upper limit; sigma_turb = 1 km/s [4.4: "Taking $T_{\rm k}$ = 600 K as an upper limit"]
  - assumption: dust modified blackbody, T=50 K, opacity 0.03(160/lambda)^1.6 [Nonlocal and non-LTE appendix: "with a color temperature of 50 K and a wavelength-dependent (in $\upmu$m) dust continuum opacity that varies as 0.03(160/$\lambda$)$^{1.6}$"]
  - validation: T_rot(C2H 4-3)=20-24 K agrees with population diagram [Nonlocal and non-LTE appendix: "in agreement with the rotational temperature inferred from a population diagram"]
- result: N(C2H) in DF3/DF4 = <=1e15 ± not stated cm^-2 [4.4: "$N$(C$_2$H) $\lesssim$ 10$^{15}$ cm$^{-2}$ and $n_{\rm H}$ of a few 10$^{5}$ cm$^{-3}$"]
- result: Peak [C2H] in DF3 = (2-4)e-7 ± not stated relative to H [4.4: "\[C$_2$H\] $\simeq$ (2–4)$\times$10$^{-7}$ in DF3"]
- result: N(C) at [CI] peak behind DF3 = 2e18 ± not stated cm^-2 [4.5: "We obtain $N$(C) $\simeq$ 2$\times$10$^{18}$ cm$^{-2}$"]
- limitation (authors): Exact chemical pathways and relative contributions of bottom-up and top-down processes remain unquantified; models omit PAH chemistry [abstract: "the exact chemical pathways and their relative contributions remain to be quantified"]
- not stated (card): CASA version and pipeline version; flux, bandpass and phase calibrators and flux scale uncertainty; CLEAN threshold, masks, weighting, cell and image size; continuum subtraction and line-free channels; exact crosscut positions and widths; Monte Carlo radiative transfer model details and code availability; Meudon PDR grid inputs beyond Table 1

### 2014ApJ...795L..25R: Turbulence Sets the Initial Conditions for Star Formation in High-pressure Environments (2014; milky_way_ism, star_formation; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Do theoretical predictions of supersonically turbulent, isothermal cloud structure and star formation thresholds, validated in solar neighbourhood clouds, hold in a high-pressure CMZ cloud?
- step 1: Combine ALMA with scaled Herschel 500 um
  - method: Scale Herschel 500 um to 3 mm assuming greybody nu^(2+beta); combine with ALMA in CASA via CLEAN; test beta values 1.0-2.0 [Deriving the column density: "we performed the image scaling using a range of values for $\beta$ (1.0, 1.2, 1.5, 1.75, 1.9, 2.0)."]
  - software: CASA
  - parameter: beta = 1.2 ± 0.1 chosen [Deriving the column density: "Thus, we assume $\beta$ of 1.2 $\pm$ 0.1."]
  - assumption: T_dust = 20 ± 1 K [Deriving the column density: "thus, we assume T$_{dust}$ is 20 $\pm$ 1 K ."]
  - validation: Removal of zero-spacing imaging artefacts justifies beta ~1.2 [Deriving the column density: "The removal of the image artefacts justifies the value for $\beta$ $\sim$1.2"]
- result: Flux fraction recovered by ALMA-only = ~18 ± not stated % [The column density PDF: "Since the ALMA-only image recovers a small fraction of the total flux ($\sim$ 18%)"]
- limitation (authors): Core unresolved so density is a lower limit; deviation may be from temperature [The column density PDF: "the density is a lower limit since this core is unresolved"]
- not stated (card): Imaging weighting/robust, deconvolver, masks, and CLEAN parameters; Details of the SD+interferometer combination (feathering vs CLEAN model, scaling); N-PDF binning, fitting method, and range used for log-normal fits; Calibrator names and flux scale; Source coordinates and distance adopted; How the 72 Msun core was defined (aperture) and the velocity dispersion source line; CASA/Miriad versions

### 2024PASP..136l4401K: Radiative Transfer Modeling of Haumea's Dust Ring (2024; solar_system; theory_or_simulation_compared_to_alma)
- question: Can the ring of Haumea be directly detected with ALMA and JWST, and what grain sizes and materials would its SED reveal, modeled with radiative transfer instead of a simple ring model
- step 4: Build model SEDs and compare to Haumea body
  - method: Ring SEDs for each material and grain size compared with a Near-Earth Asteroid Thermal Model SED of Haumea [5.1 Spectral Energy Distributions of th…: "We marked the approximate SED of Haumea as a black curve, which was calculated using a Near-Earth Asteroid Thermal Model"]
  - parameter: beaming parameter eta = 2.5 [5.1 Spectral Energy Distributions of th…: "We used a beaming parameter of $\eta$ = 2.5 to match the observed far-infrared flux densities presented in (cite: Muller2019)."]
  - assumption: D_eq = 1595 km, p_V = 0.75, q = 0.44 [5.1 Spectral Energy Distributions of th…: "assuming a volume-equivalent diameter of $D_{eq}$ = 1595 km (cite: Ortiz2012; Muller2019)"]
  - validation: Checked against Muller et al. 2019 far-infrared upper limits [5.2 Comparison with Observational Const…: "Our sum of the contribution of the main body and its ring aligns well with these constraints at the far-infrared."]
- step 5: Extract V-band, ALMA and JWST flux densities
  - method: V-band flux via filter-weighted integral of model SEDs; flux densities at ~870 micron for ALMA and F070W profile convolution for JWST [5.2 Comparison with Observational Const…: "We extracted the flux densities at $\sim$ 870 micron for every grain size of the carbon, water and silicate models."] [5.2 Comparison with Observational Const…: "we obtained the flux densities for JWST by convolving the NIRCAM F070W filter profile in a similar way as we performed for the Johnson $V$ filter"]
  - software: JWST Exposure Time Calculator, ALMA Sensitivity Calculator
  - validation: V-band ring contribution compared with the 2.5% best-fit of Ortiz et al. 2017 [5.2 Comparison with Observational Const…: "all the models are below the $2.5\%$ best fit model of (cite: Ortiz2017)"]
- result: Largest V-band ring contribution among radiative transfer models (100 nm water ice and 100 nm pyroxene) = 1.6 ± not stated percent of total system brightness [5.2 Comparison with Observational Const…: "The models closest to this estimation are the $100\,$nm water ice and the $100\,$nm pyroxene models with a contribution of $1.6\%$."]
- result: Typical V-band brightness ratio of ring models = 0.1-0.2 ± not stated percent of total system brightness [6. Summary and conclusions: "However, most of the models show a brightness ratio of around 0.1–0.2%."]
- limitation (authors): Models assume single-material, single-size grains and ignore porosity; a size distribution or mixture is left for future work [3. Comparison with Planetary Rings and …: "in this analysis we focus on identifying the potentially dominant component and particle radius for the Haumea ring"]
- not stated (card): Ring optical depth and width values adopted in the RADMC-3D ring element; Grain bulk density and the specific optool material (optical constant) entries used for each material; Wavelength grid and photon packet count for RADMC-3D; Heliocentric distance and solar geometry for 2017 and 2025; Bond albedo, phase integral and reflectivity adopted for the simple model flux numbers; Details of ALMA Cycl…

### 2015A&A...576L..15O: ALMA view of the circumstellar environment of the post-common-envelope-evolution binary system HD 101584 (2015; evolved_stars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the morphology, kinematics, masses and energetics of the circumstellar environment of the post-AGB binary HD 101584, and does it match common-envelope scenarios?
- step 1: Measure continuum and global line fluxes
  - method: Continuum map from line-free bandwidth; line fluxes integrated over global profiles; compared with SED and SEST [3.1 Global characteristics: "The ALMA total fluxes, integrated over the global line profiles, are 690, 150, and 18 Jy km s [−][1] for the [12] CO, [13] CO, and C [18] O _J_ = 2–1 lines, respectively."]
  - software: CASA
  - validation: Comparison with SEST single-dish profile to estimate missing flux [3.1 Global characteristics: "based on a detailed comparison of the ALMA global and the single-dish line profiles, no flux is missed in the central 20 km s [−][1]"]
- result: Continuum flux density at 230 GHz = 112 ± not stated mJy [3.1 Global characteristics: "The total flux density, _S_ 230 = 112 mJy"]
- result: Systemic velocity = 42 ± 1 km/s (LSR) [3.1 Global characteristics: "estimated to be 42 ± 1 km s [−][1] (LSR)"]
- limitation (authors): Flux lost to missing short spacings; large uncertainties in masses and orbital estimates [3.1 Global characteristics: "suggesting a significant loss of flux originating from a region larger than 10 [′′] ."]
- not stated (card): clean threshold, number of iterations and masks; image size and cell size; line-free channels for continuum subtraction; self-calibration solution intervals; flagging details beyond the antennas mentioned; how the global line profiles were extracted (aperture); details of the kinetic energy and momentum calculation; spectral resolution/velocity binning for the channel maps
